# Vendor risk database and credibility labels — design

**Status:** Approved on 2026-10-29, with every recommendation of D7 accepted; M0 was done on 2026-10-30 ([the M0 plan](../plans/2026-10-29-vendor-risk-database-m0.md)), and Q25–Q27, raised by its final review, were answered with their recommendations the same day; M1 was done on 2026-10-31 ([the M1 plan](../plans/2026-10-30-vendor-risk-database-m1.md)), and M2 was built the same day ([the M2 plan](../plans/2026-10-31-vendor-risk-database-m2.md)): the identifiers, the read-through of the NVD, the status job and the CVE, SEC and UN sources, the last three behind the gates Q7, Q4 and Q6; M3 was done on this branch ([the M3 plan](../plans/2026-10-31-vendor-risk-database-m3.md)): the backfill of the label and its script, the re-check of the watched sources and Part B1, which removed the old path and the flag, so that the database is always on and `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS=false` is the only switch, while Part B2, which drops the `confidence` column, is on `feature/vendor-finding-drop-confidence` for release N+1. The open questions of D7 were **decided on 2026-10-31**: Q4 (`tech@verifywise.ai`), Q5 and Q7 (accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-31, without a separate legal review), Q8, Q9, Q21, Q22 and Q28; the gates Q4 and Q7 are open, and **Q6 stayed closed** (the UN's terms allow personal, non-commercial use only, and its permission is not sought). **The AI-specific sources of the product owner's request were decided on 2026-11-05 (Q29 to Q32) and built** (D2.14 to D2.18, D3, D4.12): MITRE ATLAS, the NHTSA Standing General Order crash reports and the FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices are open, and **the AI Incident Database (`aiid`) is built and tested behind the closed gate Q29**, which opens only on its operator's written confirmation or the product owner's written acceptance of a licence conflict; **two more keyless lists were decided on 2026-11-07 (Q33 and Q34) and built** (D2.19, D2.20, D4.13): the US Consolidated Screening List (`csl`, the export control and trade restriction lists of Commerce, State and Treasury without the SDN list that `ofac` mirrors) and the FDA recalls of AI-enabled medical devices (`fda_device_recalls`, the openFDA recall dataset joined to the FDA list of D2.17), both open, with no gate; **on 2026-11-10 the product owner asked for every list of the research of 2026-11-09 that can be added at once, and delegated the decisions (Q35 to Q53); 23 lists were built** (22 mirrors and the read-through of GLEIF; D2.21 to D2.43, D4.14), all open, the FDA's device enforcement reports were folded into the recalls of D2.20 (Q37), and eight lists were not built (D2.44); Q39 and Q42 are built open on the delegation and wait for the product owner's confirmation (the fallback is a gate and the deletion of the copy), and the HHS OIG list waits for a certificate; the registry is `sr-6` and the prompt version `vr-15` · **Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Branch:** `feature/vendor-agents-and-risk-page`

This document is Phase 1 of the vendor risk database (VRDB) brief: research and design only, deliverables D1 to D8. It extends the vendor research agent of [the 2026-10-11 design](2026-10-11-vendor-research-web-agent-design.md) and of the [performance roadmap](../plans/2026-10-27-vendor-research-agent-performance.md); it replaces neither. No code, migration, configuration or dependency was changed in Phase 1, and this file is the only one created. Phase 2 starts only after approval.

**Evidence.** Every statement about an external source was checked on 2026-10-29 on the operator's own pages and is linked; Appendix A lists what each page established. **UNVERIFIED** marks what could not be checked, most often because the page refused automated readers in this environment; such a cell must not be relied on. **Observed** marks a fact seen in a response header or a small probe, which the operator does not state. Probes were header requests or ranged requests of 100 to 200 bytes; two SEC endpoints ignored the range and sent 0.8 MB and 0.16 MB, which were discarded unread. No list was downloaded.

**Fixed decisions.** The decisions of brief §2 are taken as given and not reopened. Where the brief leaves a product choice open, D7 asks it, with a recommendation; nothing below decides one silently. Where this design changes a ruling of the roadmap (R1–R39), §D4.9 names it.

---

## D1. Current state

1. **One run, one job.** `Servers/services/vendorResearch/pipeline.ts` runs a job of the `vendor-research` queue (`queue.ts`; worker `worker.ts:31`, started by `jobs/worker.ts`). It builds the target (`target.ts`, `identity.ts`, `aliases.ts`: name, `other_names`, website domain), does the routine work (`gather.ts:704` `gatherSources`: vendor pages, code-planned news searches and the public-database lookup, started together), runs one research loop per category (`loops.ts`, `researchAgent.ts`), one analysis call (`analysis.ts`, `prompts.ts`, `schema.ts`), the validation (`validation.ts`), the snippet check (`verifySnippets.ts`), the verifier call (`verifier.ts`: same company, quote supports the claim, inside the window) and the de-duplication (`clusters.ts`, `dedupe.ts`).
2. **One model.** Every call uses the organisation's key (`keySelection.ts`, `settings.service.ts`); `PROMPT_VERSION` is `vr-6` (`prompts.ts:56`).
3. **Web.** `Servers/services/webResearch/`: `guardedFetch.ts` (the page guard; `BOT_USER_AGENT` is `VerifyWiseResearchBot/1.0 (+https://verifywise.ai)`, line 23), `webSession.ts` (ledger and budgets; `addRecords` at 958), the search over the Bing RSS feed (the state of Phase 1: Bing was removed on 2026-10-31, Q21, and a run now searches with Claude web search for an Anthropic key and with the GDELT news index for every other key), `pageText.ts` (title, date, links and the canonical URL, 54–57), caches in Redis.
4. **Records, per run.** `records/index.ts` asks, from the memory of the process, CISA KEV (`kev.ts`: one JSON file, 30-minute memo), the NVD CVE API (`nvd.ts`: `virtualMatchString=cpe:2.3:a:<token>`, critical only, 119-day window, 4 requests per 30 s), Have I Been Pwned (`hibp.ts`), the SEC (`sec.ts`: tickers file, CIK by name, submissions, Form 8-K Item 1.05), the vendor's status page (`statuspage.ts`, `history.atom`) and the OFAC SDN list (`ofac.ts`: `SDN.CSV` and `ALT.CSV` from `sanctionslistservice.ofac.treas.gov/api/PublicationPreview/exports/`, entities only, 6-hour memo, one 45-second download per process). The lookups of a run share 8 s (`RECORDS_TIMEOUT_MS`, `index.ts:77`). The off switch is `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` (`config.ts:56`, on by default, documented in `Servers/.env.example`).
5. **Matching.** `records/match.ts`: exact equality of names compacted to letters and digits (`nameKeys`: the name, the other names, their short forms and the brand of the domain; OFAC uses names only, of 4 characters or more), and `cpeVendorOf` for the NVD. There is no fuzzy match and no identifier but names and the domain (R34, R35, R39).
6. **Records are sources.** Each record enters the ledger first, as a source that was read (`gather.ts:741–746`), in its category (`records/types.ts` `RECORD_CATEGORY`: KEV, NVD, HIBP and SEC are `security_incidents`, the status page `availability`, OFAC `legal_regulatory`), capped by `MAX_RECORDS` (KEV 5, NVD 3, HIBP 3, SEC 3, status page 4, OFAC 2). KEV and NVD records both link to the NVD detail page (`kev.ts:64`).
7. **Source tiers.** `sourceTiers.ts` holds hard-coded lists of 16 regulator and court hosts, 43 aggregator hosts and 81 major-publisher hosts, and the `GOVERNMENT_HOST` suffix pattern (line 90); `tierOf` (276), `publisherKey` (66; `bbc.co.uk` and `bbc.com` give one key) and `corroborationOf` (297: an aggregator is no publisher, the vendor's pages are one).
8. **Confidence.** The model returns `confidence` (`schema.ts:65`; the rule is in `prompts.ts:262–275`). `validation.ts:219` `confidenceCeiling` allows `high` for two or more publishers of which one is in a credible tier, `medium` for two page URLs or one page of the vendor, and `low` otherwise; the stored value is the lower of the model's and the ceiling (`lowerOf`, 204). `verifySnippets.ts:141` sets `low` when the page was read and does not hold the quote, and keeps the value when no page could be read (R26).
9. **Data.** `vendor_research_runs` (category summary, evidence, trace, progress, counts, `prompt_version`) and `vendor_risk_findings` (`confidence VARCHAR(10) CHECK (low, medium, high)` from migration `20261010012217`; `sources JSONB` of `{url, title, quote, published_at}`; `rationale`; `snippet_only`; status and decision columns). The SQL is in `utils/vendorResearch.utils.ts`; a register entry exists only after a person accepts a finding (`findings.service.ts`).
10. **Jobs.** `queue.ts`: the jobs `vendor_research` and the hourly `vendor_research_scan` (`upsertJobScheduler`, pattern `17 * * * *`), scheduled from `jobs/producer.ts:69` with `safeSchedule`; the queue is never emptied (R28).
11. **Tenant isolation.** Every research table has `organization_id` and an entry in `tests/integration/tenant-isolation/tenantIsolation.registry.ts` (144–169). `scripts/auditTenantIsolationCoverage.ts` checks the tables that have `organization_id` against that registry and against a justified `sharedTables` list (36–67: organizations, roles, frameworks, subscription_history, subscriptions, tiers, `*_struct_*`). The feature has no shared table yet.
12. **Client.** `Clients/src/presentation/pages/Vendors/VendorResearchFindings/`: `FindingRow.tsx:198–207` shows a neutral "Confidence" chip (`confidenceLabel` in `application/utils/vendorResearchText.ts`) and, from line 225, the sources (host, date, quote); `RunSummary.tsx` shows the category lines, "Searches: {searches}, pages read: {pages}" (190) and "Last run: {date}" (198). Strings: `Clients/src/i18n/translations.ts` (de, fr, es) and `Servers/locales/{en,de,fr}.json`, whose "Confidence is required" strings belong to AI Detection, not to research.
13. **Evaluation.** `services/vendorResearch/eval/`: 21 replay cases, a scorer with an `overconfident` measure (`scorer.ts:128`: `high` with fewer than two publishers) and `maxOverconfident` targets, and a ratchet test. `npm run eval:vendor-research` replays offline: 21 cases, 0 regressions on 2026-10-29; Appendix B lists the 22 findings the replay keeps.
14. **Docs.** `docs/technical/infrastructure/web-research.md`, `docs/technical/domains/vendors.md`, the 2026-10-11 spec, the 2026-10-27 roadmap with rulings R1–R39, and the user guide page `shared/user-guide-content/content/risk-management/vendor-risks.ts`.

---

## D2. VRDB source inventory

The criteria of brief §2.3, as letters used below: **(a)** official operator, **(b)** keyless public access, **(c)** machine-readable, **(d)** licence allows storage, display of derived data and commercial use, **(e)** stability (documented cadence, stable ids), **(f)** linkable to an organisation or its products.

| 1 | CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities | yes | yes | yes | yes (CC0) | yes, by diff | product names | **MVP, mirror** |
| 2 | OFAC SDN list | yes | yes | yes | accepted (Q5, 2026-10-31) | partly, by diff | names only (weak) | **MVP, mirror** |
| 3 | UK Sanctions List (FCDO) | yes | yes | yes | yes (OGL) | partly, by diff | names, website, registration numbers | **MVP, mirror** |
| 4 | EU consolidated financial sanctions (FSF) | yes | fixed public token (Q2) | yes | yes, Decision text unread | partly, by diff | names, identification numbers | **MVP if Q2 is yes**, else later |

Rows 20 to 42 are the lists built on 2026-11-10 (Q35 to Q53), in the order of `VRDB_SOURCES` and of D2.21 to D2.43; rows 43 to 50 are the lists of the same research that were not built (D2.44). The FDA's device enforcement reports, which were folded into row 19 (Q37), have no row. A count or a cadence in rows 20 to 50 was observed on 2026-11-10; where an operator states one, the cell says so.

### D2.1 CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities (KEV)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | CISA, US Department of Homeland Security, a federal agency that maintains the authoritative list of vulnerabilities exploited in the wild ([catalog page](https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog)). |
| Category · level | `security_incidents` · product: an exploited vulnerability in a product of the vendor. |
| Endpoint, format | JSON `https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/feeds/known_exploited_vulnerabilities.json`, CSV `https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/csv/known_exploited_vulnerabilities.csv`, schema `https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/feeds/known_exploited_vulnerabilities_schema.json` ([catalog page](https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog), mirror [cisagov/kev-data](https://github.com/cisagov/kev-data)). Observed: 1.8 MB, `ETag` and `Last-Modified` headers. |
| Auth, limits | None. No rate limit or bulk-download guidance is documented (UNVERIFIED that none applies). |
| Cadence, increments | The mirror is updated whenever the catalog is, on weekdays in US Eastern business hours ([kev-data](https://github.com/cisagov/kev-data)). There is no delta file, no per-entry modification date and no removal marker ([schema](https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/feeds/known_exploited_vulnerabilities_schema.json)), so changes are found by comparing snapshots. Removals are rare and announced ([2023-10-04](https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2023/10/04/cisa-adds-two-known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog-removes-five-kevs), [2023-12-01](https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2023/12/01/cisa-removes-one-known-exploited-vulnerability-catalog)). |
| Licence | CC0 1.0; commercial use allowed; no attribution required; the CISA logo and the DHS seal are not licensed ([licence](https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/licenses/kev/license.txt)). We name the source anyway. |
| Identifiers | `cveID` (the record id), `vendorProject`, `product` (free text), `vulnerabilityName`, `dateAdded`, `shortDescription`, `requiredAction`, `dueDate`, `knownRansomwareCampaignUse`, `notes`, `cwes`; no CPE or package URL ([schema](https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/feeds/known_exploited_vulnerabilities_schema.json)). The schema may gain fields, so the parser ignores unknown ones. |
| Match | `vendorProject` equal to a name key of the vendor: strong (brief §2.6). |
| Verdict | **MVP, mirror.** Built in M1; the connector read the real file on 2026-10-31 (1,766 records, 0 errors). It trims the free-text fields, and counts one error when the catalog's own `count` is a number that differs from the length of its list, so that a list cut short with its count intact does not mark live records withdrawn. |

### D2.2 OFAC Specially Designated Nationals (SDN) list

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | US Treasury, Office of Foreign Assets Control. The Sanctions List Service is its primary application for list files ([other lists](https://ofac.treasury.gov/other-ofac-sanctions-lists)). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor; entities only (people, vessels and aircraft are skipped, as today). |
| Endpoint, format | Files under `https://sanctionslistservice.ofac.treas.gov/api/PublicationPreview/exports/`. The [SDN page](https://sanctionslist.ofac.treas.gov/Home/static/sdn.html) labels them `SDN.CSV`, `SDN_ALT.CSV`, `SDN_ADD.CSV`, `SDN_COMMENTS.CSV`, `SDN.XML`, `SDN_ADVANCED.XML`, `SDN_ENHANCED.XML`, `SDN_XML.ZIP`, but its own links point at another host. Observed on 2026-10-29: `ALT.CSV`, the path `ofac.ts` uses, answers a ranged GET with 206 `text/csv` after a redirect; `SDN_ALT.CSV` answers 200 with an empty body; HEAD answers are unreliable. The CSV files have no header row and write `-0-` for an empty field ([data spec](https://ofac.treasury.gov/media/29976/download?inline=), [flat-file tutorial](https://ofac.treasury.gov/sdn-list-data-formats-data-schemas/tutorial-on-the-use-of-list-related-legacy-flat-files)). |
| Auth, limits | No key or login for the files. No rate limit is documented. OFAC gives no advice on designing an automated download; it notes that many institutions schedule one and should re-evaluate the schedule now and then, since the lists change ever faster ([FAQ 88](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/88)). It advises using the comprehensive data files and refreshing the list completely ([FAQ 90](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/90)); its search tool is not for automated use ([FAQ 287](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/287)). |
| Cadence, increments | No timetable: names are added or removed as needed ([FAQ 20](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/20)); Recent Actions lists sanctions list updates on 11 days of October 2026, the latest on the 28th ([recent actions](https://ofac.treasury.gov/recent-actions)). A delta file exists ([FAQ 90](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/90); `https://sanctionslistservice.ofac.treas.gov/changes/latest`); its format is UNVERIFIED. The files keep no history ([archive of changes](https://ofac.treasury.gov/specially-designated-nationals-list-sdn-list/archive-of-changes-to-the-sdn-list)), and a Recent Actions notice describes removals in its text (for a clerical update it may give only a list of UIDs: [FAQ 1031](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/1031)), so removals are found by comparing snapshots. |
| Licence | **UNVERIFIED as a licence.** No page of OFAC or the Treasury says anything about copying or reuse; the [privacy policy](https://home.treasury.gov/subfooter/privacy-policy) has only a no-warranty disclaimer, and the [site policies](https://home.treasury.gov/subfooter/site-policies-and-notices) nothing. Under 17 U.S.C. §105, "Copyright protection under this title is not available for any work of the United States Government" ([copyright.gov](https://www.copyright.gov/title17/92chap1.html)); whether the files are such works is a legal reading (Q5). The data spec says the Federal Register governs where the two differ. OFAC does not confirm potential matches or false positives ([FAQ topic](https://ofac.treasury.gov/faqs/topic/1591)). |
| Identifiers | `ent_num` (the unique listing number; the tutorial's general rule is that such numbers are not reused), `SDN_Name`, `SDN_Type`, `Program`, `Title`, `Remarks`; aliases in the ALT file (`ent_num`, `alt_num`, `alt_type`, `alt_name`, `alt_remarks`), addresses in the ADD file. A registration number appears only as free text in `Remarks`. |
| Match | Name only: weak (Low, with the note). |
| Verdict | **MVP, mirror** (the brief puts OFAC in the MVP), with Q5 answered before the flag is on by default. |

### D2.3 UK Sanctions List

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | Foreign, Commonwealth and Development Office. **The brief's claim is VERIFIED:** the UK Sanctions List is now the only source of UK sanctions designations, and the OFSI Consolidated List of Asset Freeze Targets closed on Wednesday 28 January 2026 and is no longer updated ([GOV.UK](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/the-uk-sanctions-list)). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor; entities only (individuals and ships are skipped). |
| Endpoint, format | `https://sanctionslist.fcdo.gov.uk/docs/UK-Sanctions-List.xml`, also `.csv`, `.ods`, `.odt`, `.html`, `.txt` and `.pdf` ([GOV.UK](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/the-uk-sanctions-list)); schema [XSD 4.33.3](https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/6916e72cdb01ecfcf96fc96b/SanctionsListSchema-4.33.3.xsd); [format guide](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/format-guide-for-the-uk-sanctions-list). Observed: XML 22 MB, CSV 50 MB, `ETag` and `Last-Modified` headers. |
| Auth, limits | None documented; anonymous 200 observed. No rate limit is documented (UNVERIFIED). |
| Cadence, increments | Not documented; e-mail alerts are offered, and the files were observed regenerated on the day of a change. The page's log lists delistings per regime; the XSD has no status element, and no page says whether a delisted entry leaves the files (UNVERIFIED), so the mirror compares snapshots. The existing file types (ODT, ODS, XML, HTML) did not change with the move to one list; TXT, CSV and PDF were added, and the URLs of all seven are static, the same however often the list is refreshed ([single list guidance](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/moving-to-a-single-list-for-uk-sanctions-designations-28-january-2026)); the format guide was published on 22 December 2025. |
| Licence | Open Government Licence v3.0 "except where otherwise stated" ([GOV.UK](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/the-uk-sanctions-list)); the OGL allows commercial use with attribution and does not license personal data ([OGL](https://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk/doc/open-government-licence/version/3/)). |
| Identifiers | `Unique ID` (XML `UniqueID`; one designated person can have one per regime), `OFSI Group ID` (only for entries made before 28 January 2026), the type (Individual, Entity, Ship), `Name 1` to `Name 6` with a name type (primary name, variation, alias), addresses, `Website`, `Email address`, `Business registration number (s)` (free text), `Type of entity`, `Parent Company`, `UN Reference Number`, `Date Designated`, `Last Updated`; no LEI ([format guide](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/format-guide-for-the-uk-sanctions-list)). |
| Match | Name only: weak. The host of `Website` equal to the vendor's verified domain: strong (verified domain, §2.6). A registration number equal to one stored for the vendor (Q1): strong. |
| Verdict | **MVP, mirror.** Built in M1; the connector read the real file of 2026-10-30 on 2026-10-31 (1,667 entities, 0 errors). A designation that the UN made shows its UN reference number (`UNReferenceNumber`) in its text, never as a key; an e-mail address typed into the Website field names a mailbox and gives no domain key; a designation whose kind is missing or unknown counts as one error, so that a renamed kind fails the sync and does not delist entities unseen. |

### D2.4 EU consolidated list of financial sanctions (FSF)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | European Commission, DG FISMA ([overview](https://finance.ec.europa.eu/eu-and-world/sanctions-restrictive-measures/overview-sanctions-and-related-resources_en)). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor; enterprises only (classification code `E`; persons, `P`, are skipped). |
| Endpoint, format | `https://webgate.ec.europa.eu/fsd/fsf/public/files/<file>/content?token=<value>`, where `<file>` is `xmlFullSanctionsList_1_1`, `xmlFullSanctionsList`, `csvFullSanctionsList_1_1`, `csvFullSanctionsList`, `pdfFullSanctionsList`, `xsdFullSanctionsList_1_1` or `xsdFullSanctionsList`; observed sizes 19 to 26 MB. The URLs are listed in the operator's [RSS feed](https://webgate.ec.europa.eu/fsd/fsf/public/rss) and in the [data.europa.eu record](https://data.europa.eu/api/hub/search/datasets/consolidated-list-of-persons-groups-and-entities-subject-to-eu-financial-sanctions). |
| Auth | No account or login, but every file URL carries a fixed `token` parameter (403 without it). The value is printed in those public pages, is the same for everyone, and no page promises that it will not change. Whether that is keyless is Q2. This document does not repeat the value. |
| Cadence, increments | Daily, per the metadata; the files lag the Official Journal; on 29 October the newest were dated 23 October 2026. None of the distributions is a delta (a legacy page describes a `DELTA.XML`; whether it still exists is UNVERIFIED), so the mirror compares snapshots; entries carry a `delistingDate`. |
| Licence | The data.europa.eu record gives the "European Commission reuse notice" (Decision 2011/833/EU) for most files and CC BY 4.0 for CSV 1.0; the Commission's [legal notice](https://commission.europa.eu/legal-notice_en) gives CC BY 4.0 with "© European Union". The Decision's own text could not be read here (UNVERIFIED). Only the Official Journal is authentic. |
| Identifiers | `logicalId` (kept from the previous database, so stable), `euReferenceNumber`, `unitedNationId`, `designationDate`, `delistingDate`, `nameAlias` `wholeName`, `regulation` `programme`, `identification` `number` and `identificationTypeCode` (optional) ([XSD 1.1](https://webgate.ec.europa.eu/fsd/fsf/public/rss)). |
| Match | Name only: weak. An identification number equal to one stored for the vendor (Q1): strong. |
| Verdict | **MVP, mirror** (Q2 was yes). The connector reads the file URLs from the operator's RSS feed at every sync, so that no token is kept in our code or configuration. Checked on 2026-10-31: the XSD 1.1 was read (16,116 bytes) and the file generated on 2026-10-23 (6,269 entries, 1,779 enterprises) holds all 11 attribute paths and 6 element paths that the connector reads. `unitedNationId` is filled on 23 enterprises only; the UN reference number is shown in the text, never as a key. |

### D2.5 UN Security Council Consolidated List

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | UN Security Council, through its Secretariat; intergovernmental ([list page](https://main.un.org/securitycouncil/en/content/un-sc-consolidated-list), read in the browser pane because automated readers were refused). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor; the entities section (274 entities on 2026-10-29). |
| Endpoint, format | XML ordered by reference number `https://scsanctions.un.org/resources/xml/en/consolidated.xml`, alphabetical `https://scsanctions.un.org/resources/xml/en/name/consolidated.xml`; HTML and PDF at `https://scsanctions.un.org/consolidated` and `https://scsanctions.un.org/r` (all linked from the list page). Observed: a ranged GET answers 206 `application/xml` after a redirect; HEAD answers 404. Observed on 2026-10-31: the XML address answers 302 to a time-limited address on `unsolprodfiles.blob.core.windows.net` (blob `consolidatedLegacyByPRN.xml`), then 200 `application/xml`, 2.2 MB, with `ETag`, `Last-Modified` and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=28800`; the HTML list redirects the same way and has no anchor per entry; the schema the file names (`https://www.un.org/sc/resources/sc-sanctions.xsd`) answered 403. |
| Auth, limits | None documented, and none needed for the GET (observed). |
| Cadence, increments | The page gives the date of the last update (29 October 2026) and says a press release follows every change. No delta is documented, so the mirror compares snapshots. |
| Licence | **No: personal, non-commercial use only (Q6, read on 2026-10-31).** The [terms of use](https://www.un.org/en/about-us/terms-of-use) let a user download and copy material for personal, non-commercial use only, without the right to resell, redistribute, compile or create derivative works, subject to more specific restrictions, and a waiver binds the UN only in writing. The [copyright](https://www.un.org/en/about-us/copyright) page reserves all rights: any other use, storage in a retrieval system included, needs the publisher's written permission. UN Publications' [rights and permissions](https://shop.un.org/rights-permissions) page asks for written permission to reuse content from a UN online platform or database (through the Copyright Clearance Center or permissions@un.org; a fee may apply). The list page, the HTML list and the XML state no more specific terms. |
| Identifiers | Permanent reference number (for example `QDe.001`, where `e` marks an entity), name, name in the original script, a.k.a., f.k.a., address, listed on, other information. Checked against the file on 2026-10-31: `DATAID`, `FIRST_NAME` (the name), `UN_LIST_TYPE`, `REFERENCE_NUMBER`, `LISTED_ON`, `COMMENTS1`, `ENTITY_ALIAS` (`QUALITY` a.k.a. or f.k.a., `ALIAS_NAME`), `ENTITY_ADDRESS` (`COUNTRY` and others), and on some entities `NAME_ORIGINAL_SCRIPT`, `LAST_DAY_UPDATED`, `LAST_REVIEWED_ON` and `INTERPOL_LINK`. |
| Overlap | Checked on 2026-10-31 against the files of 2026-10-30 (UN, UK) and 2026-10-23 (EU). The UK Sanctions List holds all 274 UN entities: 269 carry their UN reference number (`UNReferenceNumber`; two write `QDE.`), three have an equal name without it, one a near-identical spelling, and one is a ship designation. The EU list holds 244 by UN reference or equal name and 26 more under a near-identical spelling, but fills `unitedNationId` on 23 enterprises only. By the connectors' own name keys, the UK copy shares a key with 262 UN entities, the EU copy with 245, and either with 269. |
| Verdict | **Reject** (d), answered on 2026-10-31 (Q6): the gate stays closed and opens only with the UN's written permission, which is not sought. The connector, built in M2 and checked against the real file (274 entities, no error), stays behind it. The UN's designations reach a run through the UK and EU lists, whose records show the UN reference number. Should the gate ever open, the citation of an entry is the list page `https://main.un.org/securitycouncil/en/content/un-sc-consolidated-list` with `?referenceNumber=`, since the HTML list has no anchor per entry and the XML is served from a time-limited address. |

### D2.6 CVE Program records (cvelistV5)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The CVE Program, sponsored by CISA; the records are © The MITRE Corporation ([terms](https://www.cve.org/Legal/TermsOfUse)); a recognised public programme (brief §2.3). |
| Category · level | `security_incidents` · product. |
| Endpoint, format | [CVEProject/cvelistV5](https://github.com/CVEProject/cvelistV5): one JSON file per record under `cves/<year>/<n>xxx/`, plus `cves/delta.json` and `cves/deltaLog.json` (rolling 30 days; observed 35 MB), and hourly delta and daily baseline zip files as release assets (observed baseline 646 MB) ([README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CVEProject/cvelistV5/main/README.md)); record format 5.2.0 ([schema](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CVEProject/cve-schema/main/schema/CVE_Record_Format.json)). Asset names are irregular (a doubled `.zip`, a baseline dated a day before its tag), so a connector reads the release's list of assets instead of building names. |
| Auth, limits | Anonymous download (observed). No rate limit is documented by the programme (UNVERIFIED; GitHub's own limits were not checked). |
| Cadence | Updated about every 7 minutes from CVE Services ([README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CVEProject/cvelistV5/main/README.md)). |
| Licence | MITRE grants a perpetual, worldwide, no-charge, royalty-free, irrevocable copyright licence to reproduce, prepare derivative works of, publicly display, publicly perform, sublicense and distribute CVE, and its only condition is that every copy reproduces MITRE's copyright designation and the licence ([terms](https://www.cve.org/Legal/TermsOfUse), read on 2026-10-31 from the page's bundle). There is no commercial-use or field-of-use limit; trademarks are outside the grant. **Accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-31 (Q7), without a separate legal review.** |
| Identifiers | The CVE id; `cveMetadata.state` (`PUBLISHED` or `REJECTED`) and `dateRejected` (a rejected record stays on the list so that users know it is invalid, and should be ignored: [FAQs](https://www.cve.org/ResourcesSupport/FAQs)); `containers.cna.affected[]` `vendor` and `product` (free text; `vendor` may be "N/A"), optional `cpes` and `packageURL`. |
| Verdict | **M2, mirror**: the source of `REJECTED` and of vendor and product matching where the NVD has no CPE data (Risk G). Built; the gate Q7 has been open since 2026-10-31. Observed that day on the real data: the baseline is 648,475,703 bytes with 417,486 records and 0 unreadable, and the copy of our subset is 34,853 rows; the hourly deltas are cumulative, so a day without an end-of-day release is read from its last hourly release. |

### D2.7 NVD CVE API 2.0

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | NIST, US Department of Commerce. |
| Category · level | `security_incidents` · product. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://services.nvd.nist.gov/rest/json/cves/2.0` ([API](https://nvd.nist.gov/developers/vulnerabilities)): `virtualMatchString`, `cvssV3Severity` (LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH, CRITICAL), `pubStartDate`/`pubEndDate` and `lastModStartDate`/`lastModEndDate` (both ends required, at most 120 consecutive days), `hasKev`, `resultsPerPage` (at most 2,000); the response has `vulnStatus`, `configurations` and `affected`. |
| Auth, limits | No key needed. Without a key, 5 requests in a rolling 30-second window (50 with a key, which needs a request with an organisation and an e-mail and is not used); a sleep of 6 seconds between requests is advised ([start here](https://nvd.nist.gov/developers/start-here)). |
| Cadence | The NVD processes the CVE List every hour ([FAQ](https://nvd.nist.gov/general/FAQ-Sections/General-FAQs)). **The brief's claim is VERIFIED:** since 15 April 2026 enrichment goes first to CVEs in KEV (goal: one business day), to software used by the federal government and to critical software under EO 14028; other CVEs are "Lowest Priority - not scheduled for immediate enrichment"; unenriched CVEs published before 1 March 2026 are "Not Scheduled" (`vulnStatus` `Deferred`) ([NVD](https://www.nist.gov/itl/nvd), [NIST news](https://www.nist.gov/news-events/news/2026/04/nist-updates-nvd-operations-address-record-cve-growth), [status](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/vulnerability-status)). CPE applicability statements come from enrichment ([process](https://nvd.nist.gov/general/cve-process)), so `virtualMatchString` finds only enriched CVEs; how `Deferred` records answer is UNVERIFIED. Since 2026-06-17 records carry the CVE record's `affected` lists, but no parameter filters on them. |
| Licence | Public domain ([NIST](https://www.nist.gov/copyrights-disclaimers)). Services are asked to display: "This product uses data from the NVD API but is not endorsed or certified by the NVD." ([start here](https://nvd.nist.gov/developers/start-here); the [terms](https://nvd.nist.gov/developers/request-an-api-key) word it slightly differently). Modified content must not be attributed to the NVD. |
| Match | A CPE vendor token equal to the vendor's: strong. |
| Verdict | **Later (M2), read-through.** Today's per-run lookup stays until then. |

### D2.8 SEC EDGAR

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | US Securities and Exchange Commission. |
| Category · level | `security_incidents` · vendor: Form 8-K Item 1.05 reports a material cybersecurity incident of the registrant, generally four business days after the registrant determines it is material ([SEC](https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/press-releases/2023-139)). |
| Endpoint, format | `https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK##########.json` (10-digit CIK) and `https://www.sec.gov/files/company_tickers.json`; the APIs need no authentication or API key ([EDGAR APIs](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-application-programming-interfaces)). |
| Limits, User-Agent | At most 10 requests per second for all of a user's machines together; the SEC asks clients to declare a User-Agent such as `Sample Company Name AdminContact@<sample company domain>.com` ([accessing EDGAR data](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-search-assistance/accessing-edgar-data)). Observed: requests without a User-Agent get 403; ours, which had no e-mail, got 200 on 2026-10-29. Since 2026-10-31 the User-Agent is `VerifyWise tech@verifywise.ai` (Q4), the SEC's form exactly, on every request to the SEC, the reading of a filing's text included. |
| Cadence | Submissions update in real time; a bulk zip is built nightly. |
| Licence | Government-created content and EDGAR filings are free to reuse without the SEC's permission; cite the SEC; no seal or logos ([webmaster FAQ](https://www.sec.gov/about/webmaster-frequently-asked-questions)). |
| Identifiers | The CIK; tickers, exchanges and former names, in prose. The SEC's pages do not document the JSON keys (`items`, `website`, `ein`, …), so a CIK found by name cannot be confirmed from the documentation (UNVERIFIED). |
| Match | A CIK found by name alone (R35): weak until Q3. |
| Verdict | **M2, read-through**; built. Q4 was decided on 2026-10-31 and the gate is open. The folder of a filing belongs to every party that files about the company, so the filer is read from the filing's SGML header (Q28). |

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | World Bank Group ([list page](https://www.worldbank.org/en/projects-operations/procurement/debarred-firms)). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor. |
| Access | A web page whose table a script loads; no documented API, CSV or Excel download and no data catalog entry was found (UNVERIFIED that none exists). A third party reports an internal endpoint called with a key embedded in the page: undocumented, and not keyless by our rule. The page says the list updates every 3 hours. |
| Licence | The site's terms allow non-commercial use only and forbid derivative works and commercial use without written consent, unless specific terms such as the Data Catalog's CC BY 4.0 apply ([terms](https://www.worldbank.org/en/about/legal/terms-and-conditions), [dataset terms](https://www.worldbank.org/ext/en/legal/terms-conditions/datasets)). |
| Verdict | **Reject** (b, c, d). Its pages remain citable web sources. |

### D2.10 Regulators' enforcement

| Operator | What exists | Licence | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| UK ICO | A listing of enforcement notices, reprimands, penalties and prosecutions (the organisation in the title, no reference number); the enforcement RSS feed "is currently unavailable"; no documented dataset; notices, penalties and undertakings stay on the site for two years ([enforcement](https://ico.org.uk/action-weve-taken/enforcement/), [retention](https://ico.org.uk/action-weve-taken/retention-period-for-undertakings-enforcement-notices-and-civil-monetary-penalties/)). | OGL v3.0 for text ([re-use](https://ico.org.uk/global/copyright-and-re-use-of-materials/)). | **Reject** for the VRDB; `ico.org.uk` stays OFFICIAL in the registry. |
| EDPB | The register of Article 60 final decisions: ids such as `EDPBI:LU:OSS:D:2026:4243`, PDF decisions, no controller field, legal persons left out by many authorities ([register](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/our-work-tools/consistency-findings/register-for-article-60-final-decisions_en)); national news, whose titles name companies, with a feed at `https://www.edpb.europa.eu/feed/news_en` (observed) ([national news](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/news/national-news_en)). | Reuse allowed for commercial and non-commercial purposes, with credit ([copyright](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/copyright_en)). | **Reject** (register); the news feed is **watch** (news items, not records). |
| CNIL | The data.gouv.fr dataset of CNIL sanctions holds yearly counts, no organisations ([dataset](https://www.data.gouv.fr/fr/datasets/sanctions-prononcees-par-la-cnil/)); the monthly archive of deliberations may include sanctions (UNVERIFIED); a sanctioned organisation stops being named after a period ([CNIL](https://www.cnil.fr/fr/donnees-de-sante-et-utilisation-des-cookies-doctissimo-sanctionne-par-une-amende-de-380-000-euros)). | Licence Ouverte 2.0 ([Etalab](https://www.data.gouv.fr/pages/legal/licences/etalab-2.0)). | **Reject**. |
| US FTC | The Legal Library lists 6,124 cases with their FTC matter or file numbers and civil action numbers ([cases](https://www.ftc.gov/legal-library/browse/cases-proceedings)); RSS feeds cover press releases ([feeds](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/stay-connected/ftc-rss-feeds)); the API covers only Do Not Call complaints and HSR notices and needs an api.data.gov key ([developer](https://www.ftc.gov/developer)); CSV datasets exist for some enforcement actions (`MatterName`, `MatterNumber`, `MatterEnforcementDate`, `MatterHyperlink`; no cadence stated) ([data sets](https://www.ftc.gov/policy-notices/open-government/data-sets)). | Most material is a work of the US Government ([website policy](https://www.ftc.gov/policy-notices/website-policy)). | **Watch** (whether the datasets cover privacy and data-security cases is UNVERIFIED). **Update, 2026-11-10:** two of the four datasets, the civil penalty actions of consumer protection and the nonmerger enforcement actions of competition, were read and built (D2.25, D2.31); the other two stay watched. |
| US HHS OCR | The breach portal lists breaches affecting 500 or more individuals reported in the last 24 months (761 on 2026-10-29): name of covered entity, state, entity type, individuals affected, submission date, type of breach and more; the CSV, XML, Excel and PDF exports are driven by the page's script, with no stable URL; no record id ([portal](https://ocrportal.hhs.gov/ocr/breach/breach_report_hip.jsf)). | Terms not found (hhs.gov answered 403; UNVERIFIED). | **Later**, if a stable export and terms appear. |

### D2.11 EU database of high-risk AI systems (Article 71)

The Commission sets up and maintains it; registrations under Article 49 are public apart from a secure section, and the information should be machine-readable ([Art. 71](https://ai-act-service-desk.ec.europa.eu/en/ai-act/article-71), [Art. 49](https://ai-act-service-desk.ec.europa.eu/en/ai-act/article-49), [Annex VIII](https://ai-act-service-desk.ec.europa.eu/en/ai-act/annex-8)). No page read says it is live or gives a URL, an API or an export. Article 113 as amended by the AI Omnibus (in force since 27 July 2026) applies the high-risk obligations of Annex III from 2 December 2027 and of Annex I from 2 August 2028 ([Art. 113](https://ai-act-service-desk.ec.europa.eu/en/ai-act/article-113), [Omnibus](https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/ai-omnibus-enters-force)). **Watch**; check again after 2 December 2027. It would serve `ai_specific`, at provider level.

### D2.12 Excluded by the keyless rule

- **SAM.gov exclusions**: the Exclusions API needs an API key from a SAM.gov account, and the extracts need an account and a key ([Exclusions API](https://open.gsa.gov/api/exclusions-api/), [extracts](https://open.gsa.gov/api/sam-entity-extracts-api/)).
- **The NVD with a key**: we keep to the public limit.
- **The FTC API**: an api.data.gov key, and no case data anyway.
- **The World Bank's internal endpoint**: undocumented, with a key embedded in its page.

### D2.13 Not VRDB sources by decision

Have I Been Pwned stays a lookup (category UNVERIFIED) and the status page stays a lookup (category VENDOR), as brief §2.8 fixes; aggregators such as OpenSanctions and OpenCorporates are excluded by §2.3 and were not evaluated.

**Amended on 2026-11-05 (Q30).** The same sentence also excluded the AI Incident Database, and the product owner's request for AI incident sources reverses that for AI-specific sources. An aggregator may be a VRDB source when its licence passes criterion (d), and its records carry their true class: an aggregator is UNVERIFIED (D5.3 step 3). The AI Incident Database is evaluated in D2.14, D2.15 to D2.17 evaluate the three other AI-specific sources that were built, and D2.18 lists the ones that were not.

### D2.14 AI Incident Database (AIID)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Responsible AI Collaborative, a nonprofit. The database is its index of alleged AI harms: editors summarise news reports, so an incident page restates allegations and not findings ([an incident page](https://incidentdatabase.ai/cite/1/)). |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: the developer and the deployer that an incident names. Class UNVERIFIED (an aggregator of allegations, D5.3 step 3), strength weak (D5.2). |
| Endpoint, format | The page `https://incidentdatabase.ai/research/snapshots/` links the weekly Excel export `AIID_Excel_Export-YYYYMMDD.xlsx`, kept in a public R2 bucket. The connector reads the links of the page, keeps those on the bucket's host over https (pinned in the connector: `pub-72b2b2fc36ec423189843747af98f80e.r2.dev`) whose file name has that form, takes the one with the greatest date and keeps its address in a local variable only; it then asks for it with a conditional GET, and a new week's file is a new address that simply answers 200. The snapshot holds the export's date (the version of the copy), the XML of the one sheet and the shared strings when the workbook has any. A link counts only when its address ends with the file name, so a name followed by a query, a fragment or more characters is another address. The address the answer comes from after redirects must be on that host too, for a 304 as well as a 200; otherwise the sync fails as `feed_without_file`. Checked on 2026-11-05 through the page guard: the snapshots page serves 14 links to exports as plain HTML, not built by a script, all on that host, the newest dated 2026-11-02. Observed on 2026-11-05: the export dated 2026-11-02, 2,611,619 bytes, with `ETag` and `Last-Modified`. The workbook has five sheets and only `Incidents` is read: a title row, a group row, then the header on row 3, and 1,784 incidents with ids up to 1797. It has no `sharedStrings.xml`: the cells are inline strings and numbers, and `date` is an Excel serial number. A bzip2 tarball of the whole database (about 120 MB) also exists; no reader exists for it and none is built. |
| Auth, limits | None. The page: 2 MiB, `text/html`, 30 s. The file: 16 MiB, the Excel and `application/octet-stream` content types, 120 s; the zip inside it: 64 entries, 48 MiB for one entry and 64 MiB in all, and only `xl/workbook.xml`, its relationships, the shared strings when present and the one sheet are read. The sheet is found by the name `Incidents` through the workbook's relationships, and the target must be exactly a `xl/worksheets/sheet<N>.xml` part (a target that starts with a slash is read from the root of the package, which is how the exporter writes it). The sheet is kept to 20,000 rows and 256 columns (the export has 1,787 rows and 59 columns), and a sheet past either is `too_large`. The header is the first of the first ten rows whose first cell is `Incident ID`, and the columns are read by name: `Incident ID`, `date`, `title`, `description`, `developer` and `deployer` are required (a sheet without one is not the list, `wrong_root`) and `report_count` is optional. The Excel is read with the zip reader and the XML scanner of the repository and not with SheetJS: SheetJS inflates every entry with no per-entry limit and has had prototype-pollution and ReDoS advisories, and this is a file that third parties can influence. The site's terms bar high-volume bot access, and the weekly export is the operator's own bulk channel: the job makes one GET of the page and one conditional GET of the newest export a day, downloads a new export once a week at most, and never fetches the page of an incident. |
| Cadence, increments | The export is weekly. The job runs daily at 04:11 UTC (once the gate is open); a copy is stale after 48 hours, and an incident that a later export no longer holds becomes `withdrawn`. No page listing an export, or a workbook with no sheet named `Incidents`, no header row or dates counted from 1904, fails the sync with a fixed code (`feed_without_file`, `wrong_root`). |
| Licence | **In conflict (Q29).** The terms of use (effective 2025-08-07, [terms](https://incidentdatabase.ai/terms-of-use/)) license the incidents, quickadd, duplicates, taxa, classifications, entities and entity_relationships collections under CC BY-SA 4.0 and exclude report text, submissions and media. The general terms also bar anyone from selling or commercially exploiting "any aspect of the Site" without express permission, and the two do not agree on their face. The operator names a contact for such questions, info@incidentdatabase.ai. Share-alike applies to the record texts that the database stores and shows; whether a finding that quotes them is adapted material is part of the question to the operator. The `vrdb_sources` row holds the CC BY-SA 4.0 licence with the note that commercial use awaits the operator's confirmation, and an attribution that says the records are adapted by VerifyWise and shared under CC BY-SA 4.0. |
| Record | One per incident (`ai_incident`, an event: `publishedAt` is the incident's date). The text starts with `AI Incident Database (incidentdatabase.ai).` and holds the incident number, the title, the date, the alleged developer and deployer, the editors' description (its first paragraph, cleaned of markup and of the characters a reader does not see, and cut at 600 characters at a word boundary, a cut one ending in `[shortened]`; the title and the names of the parties lose the same characters), the number of linked reports, and ends with the sentence that the database records allegations as reported and does not establish them. The address is `https://incidentdatabase.ai/cite/<id>`. **Left out:** `harmed` (it names people and small businesses), the editors' ids and notes, the data sources, the implicated systems, the taxonomy columns and the sheets of reports, entities and coverage map, so that no report text is stored (the licence excludes it as well). |
| Match | Name only, weak: each part of `developer` and of `deployer`, split at commas, is a `name:` key (the name as written and without its legal form, 4 characters or more), unless the part names a kind of party and not a party. A part is dropped when its last word is Developers, Deployers, Users, Operators, Companies, Researchers, Individuals, Systems, Actors, Creators, Scammers, Criminals, Cybercriminals, Hackers, Stalkers, Phishers, Students, Minors, Boys, Lawyers, Firms, Drivers, Authors, Writers, Teachers, Doctors or Tourists, or when the whole part is Unknown, None, Unnamed or Law enforcement (the whole part, because "Immigration and Customs Enforcement" is an agency and keeps its key). The first eight words were the first rule; the others were added when the real export showed that a quarter of its keys named a kind of party ("Scammers", "Deepfake creators", "Students"): the longer rule takes the keys of the export of 2026-11-02 from 4,392 to 3,405. A word is on the list only when no company is likely to end its name with it. Not `harmed`, not the implicated systems. |
| Verdict | **Built, behind the closed gate Q29.** The connector and its tests exist, but while the gate is closed the list is never downloaded, scheduled or looked up, no run shows it, and the coverage does not list it. Q5 and Q7 were accepted without a legal review because no term stood against the use; here a term of the operator does, so the gate (`AIID_PERMISSION_GRANTED` in `vrdb/gates.ts`) opens on the operator's written confirmation that a commercial product may store the export and show records derived from it under CC BY-SA 4.0, or on the product owner's explicit written acceptance of that risk after reading the clause above. Before it opens, a reviewer re-reads 20 random records for the names of private individuals, because the descriptions name victims and the accused. The same review looks at the `Alleged developer` and `Alleged deployer` lines and at the match keys: the connector drops kinds of party ("Scammers", "Developers") but not names of people, and the export of 2026-11-02 names some public figures there. Whether such a name may stay, or a person-name filter is built first, is decided at that review. If the gate stays closed too long, the richest AI incident list is missing from the runs while the other three deliver; if it opens wrongly, a commercial product breaches a nonprofit's terms on its best-known dataset, and the copy has to be removed. |

### D2.15 MITRE ATLAS case studies

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | MITRE, which maintains ATLAS, a knowledge base of adversary tactics and techniques against AI systems. It curates the case studies from published research and incident reports, with references for each ([atlas-data](https://github.com/mitre-atlas/atlas-data)). It is not an authority: its host is TRUSTED_PRESS in the registry (P80, `sr-6`), and the review process for contributions was not read. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: the target of the case study. Class TRUSTED_PRESS, strength weak (D5.2). |
| Endpoint, format | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mitre-atlas/atlas-data/main/dist/manifest.yaml` (6,486 bytes observed; releases newest first, each with `release`, `release-date` and versions that give a `format-version` and a `path`), then `…/dist/<path>` of the newest release that has a file of format `6.x.y` whose path is exactly `v6/ATLAS-<release>.yaml`, the release being a month with a patch number when it has one: `v6/ATLAS-2026.10.yaml` (1,154,660 bytes observed, format 6.1.0, with an `ETag`) or a patch release such as `v6/ATLAS-2025.11.2.yaml`, which the manifest already lists (a pattern of the month alone would skip a newer patch release and quietly keep serving the month before). A path with `..`, a query or another directory is not a file of the list. The release name (`2026.10`) is read from the path, travels to the parse as a second body and is the version of the copy. `dist/ATLAS.yaml` is an older format (5.6.0) and `ATLAS-latest.yaml` is a symlink; neither is read. The manifest and the file must each come from the repository's own path on `raw.githubusercontent.com` after redirects, and the 304 of the file is checked as well; a 304 of the manifest, which was sent no validator, is a failed fetch (`fetch_failed`). A manifest with no such version, an answer from anywhere else, a file whose `format-version` does not start with `6.`, one whose `case-studies` is not a map, one with more than 5,000 case studies and a YAML that breaks a guard (below) are not the list (`wrong_root`). |
| Auth, limits | None. The manifest: 256 KiB, 30 s. The data: 8 MiB, 120 s. Content types for both: `text/plain`, `application/yaml`, `application/x-yaml`, `application/octet-stream`. The YAML is read with the `yaml` package (2.9.1, ISC, a direct dependency of `Servers/package.json` pinned to that version) on the core schema, with no custom tags, and under guards of the connector's own. It calls `parseDocument` and makes the checks itself instead of passing the package's `uniqueKeys` option, because that check is quadratic in the number of keys and a file far below the size limit could keep the worker busy for a very long time; its own check is linear. The manifest and the file are both refused (as not the list) when the text has a syntax error or a warning (the package only warns about an unknown tag), more than one document, an alias (`maxAliasCount: 0` when the document is turned into values), an explicit tag, a map key that is not a scalar or that repeats (compared as the property it becomes, so `1` and `"1"` are one), more than a million lexemes (the file of 2026.10 has about 136,000), or anything that makes the parser throw, such as a very deep nesting. The lexeme budget also bounds the memory: without it a flat list that fills the size limit needs more than a gigabyte. |
| Cadence, increments | Monthly releases (2026.10 on 2026-10-28). The job runs daily at 03:17 UTC, and the conditional GET answers 304 between releases. |
| Licence | The Apache License 2.0 ([LICENSE](https://github.com/mitre-atlas/atlas-data/blob/main/LICENSE), "Copyright 2021-2026 MITRE"). The README also prints "ALL RIGHTS RESERVED. Approved for Public Release", which is MITRE's release marking, and the LICENSE file governs. **Accepted on 2026-11-05 (Q32).** The `vrdb_sources` row holds the licence and MITRE's copyright line with the year of the day, and says the records are adapted by VerifyWise. The repository has no NOTICE file (checked on 2026-11-05: the root of the `main` branch holds `LICENSE` and `README.md`, and `NOTICE`, `NOTICE.md` and `NOTICE.txt` answer 404), so Apache 2.0 §4(d) adds no text to the notice. |
| Record | One per case study (`ai_case_study`, an event: `publishedAt` is its start date). An entry that cannot be read is left out and counted: an id that is not `AML.CSnnnn` or not the key of its entry, a type that is neither Incident nor Exercise (any case), a name that is empty after cleaning, or a start date that is not a real `YYYY-MM-DD` day. Up to max(10, 1% of the entries) of them are tolerated, and more fails the sync as `parse_errors`, as for the CVE records (Q7). The type is spelled out in the text: **Incident**, an attack reported in the wild, or **Exercise**, a demonstration by researchers or a red team and not an attack in the wild; of the 77 case studies in the file of 2026.10, 27 are incidents and 50 are exercises, which is why the record type is not `ai_incident`. The text starts with `MITRE ATLAS case study (atlas.mitre.org).` and holds the id (`AML.CSnnnn`), the name (200 characters at most), the type, the target and the actor as ATLAS records them (300 characters at most each; the actor is left out when absent or `Unknown`), the start date, the summary (600 characters at most) and the number of references. A case study that ATLAS dates only to a year or a month (32 of the 77 in the file of 2026.10) is stored with the first day of it, and the text does not show a day that nobody gave: it reads `Start date: 2020.` for a year, `Start date: 2023-06.` for a month and `Start date: 2024-10-24.` for a day. The title is the id and the name. The address is `https://atlas.mitre.org/studies/<id>`, a single-page application that answers a plain request with 404 and renders in a browser. **Left out:** the references (counted only), the relationships and the procedure steps. |
| Text safety | **The descriptions hold live prompt-injection payloads** (found in the data: the case study of a computer-use agent, `AML.CS0046`, embeds `<atlas-example>` blocks that tell a reader to run a command, a base64 string piped into a shell, and a destructive command). A record's text is read by a model, so the connector cleans it before it is stored (`cleanCaseStudyText`). It first brings the text to the form it finally reads in: compatibility forms are folded (a full-width `<` is a `<`), control characters and characters a reader does not see (zero-width, right-to-left overrides, tag characters, variation selectors) are removed, and entities are decoded, again and again until nothing changes (`&amp;lt;` and `&#xFF1C;` both end as `<`), so that a tag written as entities is a tag from the first step. That is stricter than decoding last, which would leave the text of a block that was written as entities. It then removes each `<atlas-example>` block whole (blocks nest, and an unclosed or self-closing one runs to the end of the text); when that changed the text, the pass ends there and starts again, because a removal can join the halves of another block. Otherwise it strips every other tag and keeps the text between them (a tag that ends a line, such as `<br>`, leaves a space), reduces `[text](url)`, also as an image, to its text and drops `[[ref]]`. It repeats the pass until the text no longer changes, at most eight times. It then keeps the first paragraph, on one line, cut at a word boundary at the cap of the field: 600 characters for the summary, 200 for the name and 300 for the target and the actor. Shaping can bring the two ends of a `[[ref]]` closer than the pattern allows, so the shaped text is cleaned again until shaping changes nothing, at most four rounds. A text that does not settle in those steps (entities written many levels deep, blocks that keep forming blocks) becomes empty, so that the failure is closed, and a text of more than 20,000 characters is cut before it is read. The name, the target and the actor go through the same cleaner. It removes MITRE's own mechanism for quoting an attack; it cannot recognise an instruction written as plain prose in the first paragraph, and that is what the paragraph and the 600-character bounds, the `<evidence>` escaping and the untrusted-data line are for. The record goes through the escaping and the line like every source. |
| Match | Name only, weak, from the `target` field alone. Not the entities: `attributed-to` mixes attackers with the researchers who ran an exercise, so a key would call the researcher the culprit. Not `reported-by`, and no domain key from a URL in the target. The target is split at `,`, `;`, `/`, " and ", " or " and parentheses; in each part URLs, a trailing `'s` and the leading words the, users of, including, new, internal, commercial, enterprise, exposed, publicly, private and models from are dropped; the first one, two and three words of the part are names; and a name that is a generic word (users, models, services, systems, agents, cloud, llm, ai and the like) is dropped, so a vendor called Users or Cloud matches nothing. A short brand name that is also a product (a vendor called Copilot or Cursor) can match a target about another company's product; the note and the Low cap contain it. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q32).** The class is TRUSTED_PRESS, but every match is by name, so a record gives Low support and the name-only note, and D5.3 step 2 counts strong sources only: a weak case study never earns Medium. |

### D2.16 NHTSA Standing General Order crash reports

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The National Highway Traffic Safety Administration, US Department of Transportation. Its Standing General Order requires reports of certain crashes of vehicles with automated driving systems (ADS) and Level 2 driver assistance (ADAS), and NHTSA publishes them ([page](https://www.nhtsa.gov/laws-regulations/standing-general-order-crash-reporting)). It warns that the data may be incomplete or unverified, and that personal and confidential business information is redacted. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: the reporting entity. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak: a regulator's record of reports that manufacturers filed, as the SEC's filings are. |
| Endpoint, format | Three CSV files, `https://static.nhtsa.gov/odi/ffdd/sgo-2021-01/SGO-2021-01_Incident_Reports_ADS.csv`, `…_ADAS.csv` and `…_OTHER.csv`. Observed on 2026-11-05: 2,788,134, 2,468,633 and 33,723 bytes, each with `ETag` and `Last-Modified` (2026-10-15), 116 columns, and 1,595, 2,574 and 32 rows of reports from 2025-06-16 to 2026-09-15. A crash can have several reports, and a report up to five versions. `Incident Date` and `Report Submission Date` are months (`AUG-2026`), not days. Checked on 2026-11-05 through the page guard with the bot's User-Agent: the three files answer 200. The archive of 2021 to 2025 (`Archive-2021-2025/`), made under earlier versions of the order, is not read: NHTSA says its ADS or Level 2 classification was sometimes wrong, and leaving it out costs about seven months (2024-11 to 2025-06) of the 24-month lookback, a smaller loss than misclassified records. |
| Auth, limits | None. Each file: 32 MiB and 60,000 reports (the largest real file has 2,574; a file with more is not the list), content types `text/csv`, `application/csv` and `application/octet-stream`, 120 s. There is no conditional GET: a snapshot holds one pair of validators and there are three files, so the job downloads all three (5.3 MB) every day, in the fixed order ADS, ADAS, OTHER, and the hash of what it read finds a copy that did not change. The required columns are `Report ID`, `Report Version`, `Reporting Entity`, `Report Submission Date`, `Incident Date`, `Highest Injury Severity Alleged`, `State` and `Crash With`; a file without one is `wrong_root`. `Incident Date - Unknown` is read when the file has it, and no other column is read. A row is left out and counted when its number of cells is not the header's, its report id is not of the form the files use, its version is not a whole number, its entity has no letter or digit, or its incident date is neither empty, nor a month, nor flagged `Y` in `Incident Date - Unknown`; a submission month that does not parse keeps the row and only cannot give the version of the copy. More than max(10, 1% of the rows of the three files) of them, or a file that ends inside a quoted cell, fails the sync as `parse_errors`. A file that after redirects is not in NHTSA's directory is refused as `fetch_failed`. The version of a copy is the newest submission month among the reports (`2026-09` on 2026-11-05). |
| Cadence, increments | NHTSA refreshes the files monthly, on the 15th. The job runs daily at 13:05 UTC. |
| Licence | None published. The files are a work of the US Government (17 U.S.C. §105), **accepted on 2026-11-05 (Q31)** in the way of Q5, without a separate legal review. |
| Record | One per reporting entity and file, not one per report (`crash_report_summary`, an event): one entity has 2,211 reports in the current files, and per-report rows would let one entity flood a run and would push location data into the database. Per `Report ID` only the highest `Report Version` counts, across the three files (of two rows with one version the first file's stays): the 4,201 rows of 2026-11-05 are 4,010 reports, summarised in 44 records (19 ADS, 22 Level 2 and 3 OTHER). Entities are grouped by their name compared as letters and digits, so "Acme, Inc." and "ACME Inc" are one record, titled by the spelling the files use most, and every spelling gives keys. A report whose incident month is unknown (one real report: the date is empty and `Incident Date - Unknown` says `Y`) counts in the total and in the severity counts and is left out of the counts by year and of the newest reports. The text starts with `NHTSA Standing General Order crash reports (nhtsa.gov).` and holds the entity, the automation label (Automated Driving System, Level 2 driver assistance, or automation not identified for the OTHER file, whose reports all say `Unknown, see Narrative` for the system that was engaged), the number of reports in the current files with the first and last month, the counts by the highest injury severity alleged (fatality, serious, moderate, minor, no injury or property damage only, unknown), the counts by year of the incident, the three newest reports (id, month, state, what the vehicle crashed with, severity; the newest incident month first, then the newest submission, then the greater id), and ends with the sentence that the order requires these reports, that a report is not a finding of fault, and that counts depend on fleet size and reporting rules. `publishedAt` is the first day of the newest incident month. The address is the documentation page with the entity (as its compact name) and the automation type as query parameters, so that no two records share one address (the verifier maps an address to a record); it names a company, never a person. **Left out:** the VIN and the decoded VIN, the serial number, the same-vehicle id, latitude and longitude, address, city and zip code, the time of the incident, the narrative, the operating entity, the driver or operator type, and every column of confidential business information. The state is kept. |
| Match | Name only, weak: the `Reporting Entity`, and the text before ` (` and the text inside the parentheses of a name written like "Zorbex (American Zorbex Motor Co.)". Not `Make`, which is the make of the vehicle and not the reporter, and not the operating entity. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q31).** |

### D2.17 FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The US Food and Drug Administration. The list names devices that use AI and that the FDA has authorized for marketing ([page](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/software-medical-device-samd/artificial-intelligence-enabled-medical-devices)). It is a list of authorizations and not of incidents. It matters because AI in a medical device is high-risk under the EU AI Act, Article 6(1), through the MDR and IVDR. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: the company. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak: the regulator's own authorization list, matched by the company name only. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://www.fda.gov/media/178541/download?attachment`, a CSV (the same list is also published as Excel and XML). Observed on 2026-11-05: 141,468 bytes, 1,614 rows, `Last-Modified` 2026-09-04, no `ETag`, a `content-disposition` that names `aiml-devices-csv.csv`, and bot-manager cookies on the response. Columns: `Date of Final Decision` (MM/DD/YYYY), `Submission Number`, `Device`, `Company`, `Panel (Lead)` (filled), `Primary Product Code`; decisions from 1995-09-29 to 2026-06-29 and about 816 distinct spellings of a company name, which the grouping below turns into 640 records. Checked on 2026-11-05 through the page guard with the bot's User-Agent and no cookie: 200 with the whole file, and a second request with `If-Modified-Since` answered 304 (so the job is conditional on `Last-Modified`). Had the bot manager refused with 403, the list would move behind a gate; nothing retries around it. |
| Auth, limits | None. 2 MiB, content types `text/csv` and `application/octet-stream`, 60 s, with a conditional GET on `Last-Modified`. The conditional response carries no `content-disposition`, so the file's name cannot be checked; instead the answer must come from the host `www.fda.gov` and the header row must hold exactly the six column names, otherwise `wrong_root`. Dates are read by a function of their own (`fdaDayOf`), because the shared day reader expects dd/mm/yyyy and would drop or swap every date. The version of a copy is the day of the file's `Last-Modified`. A row with no company, no submission number or a date that is none is left out and counted, and only more than max(10, 1% of the rows) of them fails the sync as `parse_errors`. |
| Cadence, increments | No cadence is stated; the file changes from time to time. The job runs daily at 05:35 UTC. |
| Licence | fda.gov states that its content is in the public domain and may be republished freely. |
| Record | One per company, grouped by the company name compared as letters and digits and titled by its most frequent spelling (`ai_device_authorizations`, a **state**: no lookback, and the record is offered as of the copy; the latest decision day is stored as `publishedAt`). The text starts with `FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices (fda.gov).` and holds the company, the number of devices with the first and the latest decision day, the three newest devices (device, submission number, decision day, panel, product code), and ends with the sentence that the list records marketing authorizations of devices that use AI and is not a record of incidents, recalls or adverse events. The address is the documentation page with the company's compact name as a query parameter. Cells are cleaned: a tab or a line break around a name and the commas at its end go, a device name past 500 characters is cut and ends in `[shortened]`, and the 34 device names of the real file whose trade mark, dash or quotation mark was written as a control character (a windows-1252 byte that was read as ISO 8859-1) get the character back. |
| Match | Name only, weak: the `Company`, in every spelling the file uses for it. |
| Verdict | **Built, open.** An authorization is no risk by itself: the analysis prompt tells the model to raise no risk from the record and to use it only to say, in the description of another risk, that the vendor's AI is part of a regulated medical device. |

### D2.18 Not built

Twelve candidates were not built: seven on 2026-11-05 and the last five (SEC enforcement, the FTC AI cases, CourtListener, ICO enforcement and CFPB complaints) on 2026-11-07, when the product owner asked for every keyless list that can be added right away and D2.19 and D2.20 were built. The registry (`sr-6`, D3.4) names the hosts of four of the first seven, so that a page that a web search cites gets a reviewed label and a reason; no data is copied.

| Source | What it is | Why it is not built | Registry |
|---|---|---|---|
| AIAAIC repository | A volunteer-compiled repository of AI incidents and controversies, kept in a Google Sheet; its entries summarise media reports ([user guide](https://www.aiaaic.org/aiaaic-repository/user-guide)). | Fails (d) in its own words: the site footer says CC BY-SA 4.0, but the user guide expressly forbids using the data for commercial risk management and warns violators. Its terms page needs a Google sign-in. Nothing is built; a commercial licence could be requested if the product owner wants one. | `aiaaic.org`, UNVERIFIED (U2) |
| OECD AIM | The OECD's incident monitor. Titles and summaries are generated by a language model from news articles ([methodology](https://oecd.ai/en/incidents-methodology)). | All rights reserved (d); no API or export (c). | `oecd.ai`, UNVERIFIED (U3) |
| AVID | The AI Vulnerability Database: community-contributed reports ([terms](https://avidml.org/legal/terms-and-conditions)). | The site terms forbid data mining and commercialisation (d). The last data commit was on 2026-03-26. | `avidml.org`, UNVERIFIED (U4) |
| GW DAIL | A database of AI litigation, kept as a blog under `gwu.edu`. | No export and no licence found (c, d). | None: an entry for `gwu.edu` would label the whole university |
| MIT AI Incident Tracker | A reclassification of AIID incidents ([tracker](https://airisk.mit.edu/ai-incident-tracker)). | No download (c), and not an independent publisher: it is built on the AI Incident Database. | `airisk.mit.edu`, UNVERIFIED (U5) |
| Agent Incident Registry | A registry of 487 records, run by a security vendor. | No export and no licence found; its host was not verified. | None |
| MAUDE | The FDA's database of device adverse event reports, served through openFDA. | It is not AI-specific. | None: `fda.gov` is official already under O1 (`.gov`) |
| SEC enforcement (2026-11-07) | The SEC's litigation releases and administrative proceedings, as two RSS feeds, `https://www.sec.gov/enforcement-litigation/litigation-releases/rss` and `https://www.sec.gov/enforcement-litigation/administrative-proceedings/rss`: keyless, the title is the names of the defendants (people and companies), and nothing marks an item as about AI. | Each feed is a rolling window of 25 items, about three weeks. A mirror takes the whole list, and a record that the list no longer holds becomes `withdrawn` or `delisted`, so every item would leave the copy as it ages out of the window and the copy would hold three weeks of enforcement and not the record of it. The incremental path of the CVE records refuses a sync until an operator has loaded a baseline, and the older releases (12,009 of them) are only on HTML listing pages (about 480 pages if a page holds 25, not checked), which is scraping. It would need an additive append-only mirror mode, in which records are added and never taken off because the list no longer shows them: a change of the sync engine and a separate decision of the product owner. | None: `sec.gov` is an entry already (O21) |
| FTC AI cases (2026-11-07) | The FTC's cases about AI, in its Legal Library (D2.10). | It has no feed of cases. Its RSS feeds carry press releases in a rolling window, the problem of the SEC's feeds, and its API covers Do Not Call complaints and HSR notices only. The listing page of cases that was fetched is about 1 MB of HTML with no machine-readable form, and the site's `robots.txt` asks for `Crawl-delay: 5`, so a copy would be a scrape of listing pages at one request every 5 seconds (decision of 2026-11-07). The FTC's two datasets of 2026-11-10 (civil penalty cases and nonmerger enforcement actions, D2.25 and D2.31) are separate lists and not these cases. | None: `.gov` hosts are official already under O1 |
| CourtListener (2026-11-07) | The Free Law Project's archive of court opinions and dockets (O20). | Its API needs a membership or a commercial agreement with the operator, so it fails (b) (decision of 2026-11-07). | None: `courtlistener.com` is an entry already (O20) |
| ICO enforcement (2026-11-07) | The UK Information Commissioner's Office's listing of enforcement notices, reprimands, penalties and prosecutions (D2.10). | A dynamic list with no feed and no dataset (its RSS feed was unavailable when D2.10 was written), so a copy would be a scrape of a dynamic page; its notices stay on the site for two years (decision of 2026-11-07). | None: `ico.org.uk` is an entry already (O10) |
| CFPB complaints (2026-11-07) | The consumer complaint database of the US Consumer Financial Protection Bureau. | Very large (hundreds of MB), not AI-specific, and its content is consumers' own text that nobody has verified, which a model would read (decision of 2026-11-07). | None: `.gov` hosts are official already under O1 |

The `oecd.ai` entry labels every page of the host, the OECD's own policy pages included, as reviewed and UNVERIFIED, so it has to be revisited if a finding ever needs them.

**Not looked at (2026-11-07).** The consolidated sanctions lists of other governments (Canada, Australia and Switzerland, among others) were not looked at: their terms and formats were not read, so they are neither built nor rejected. A round of the criteria of D2.0 for them would decide. Canada's list was looked at in the research of 2026-11-09 and built on 2026-11-10 (D2.21, Q46); Australia and Switzerland stay as they were. The lists of that research that were not built are in D2.44.

### D2.19 US Consolidated Screening List (CSL)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The International Trade Administration (ITA), US Department of Commerce. The CSL is its compilation, in one file, of the lists of restricted parties that three departments publish: the Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS) of Commerce, the Department of State and the Treasury's Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC) ([page](https://www.trade.gov/consolidated-screening-list)). It is an aid to screening and not the official list: the page tells the user to check the official publication of restricted parties in the Federal Register and, when a match appears, to carry out further due diligence. |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor, by name: an entity named on a list of restricted parties. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak for a name (D5.2); the website, the LEI or the registration number of an entry of an OFAC list can make a match strong. A listing in force is a state and not an event: it does not age out after the 36 months of the category (most Entity List entries date from before 2023), so a `restricted_party` record has no lookback (D4.13). |
| Endpoint, format | Three whole-list downloads, keyless, linked from the page: `https://data.trade.gov/downloadable_consolidated_screening_list/v1/consolidated.json`, and the same with `.csv` and `.tsv`. The JSON is read, since the CSV would need a second schema for the nested addresses and ids. Observed on 2026-11-07: 34,186,586 bytes, with `ETag` and `Last-Modified` and `Cache-Control: no-store`; a HEAD request answers 404 on these addresses, a GET answers 200 and a range GET 206, so the job sends a conditional GET and never a HEAD; `If-None-Match` and `If-Modified-Since` both answer 304; through the page guard with the bot's User-Agent the host answers 200. The document is `{ results, search_performed_at, sources_used, offset, total }`: `sources_used` has a row for each of 13 lists, with its `source_last_updated` and `last_imported`, and `results` holds 26,265 entries over 12 of them (the Foreign Sanctions Evaders List has none today). An entry has `source` (the list's name, with its code in parentheses), `name`, `alt_names` (an array or null), `addresses`, `id` (the OFAC entity number for an entry that comes from OFAC, a hash for the others), `entity_number`, `type` (`Entity`, `Individual`, `Vessel` or `Aircraft`, on the OFAC lists only), `programs`, `ids` (an array of `{ type, number, country }`: registration numbers, websites and others), `remarks`, `federal_register_notice`, `start_date`, `end_date`, `license_requirement`, `license_policy`, `standard_order`, `source_list_url` and `source_information_url`, and more fields for people, vessels and aircraft.
