Text: `FTC civil penalty cases (ftc.gov), an enforcement or case list.`, the defendant, what the list holds of it (the orders, their years and their total), the newest orders (day, case number, the basis cut to 90 characters, the amount or `no amount stated`), then `The entry is the FTC's record of an order against a defendant of this name, which may be another company of the same name; it is not a finding about the vendor's current practices. Not endorsed by the FTC; adapted by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 588, maximum 818 on 2026-11-10); when the sentences are longer, the third and then the second newest order are left out and then the defendant's name is cut, and the closing sentence is never cut. The title and the summary show the defendant cut at 100 characters, with the marker; the identifier `defendant` holds the spelling that the most orders use, whole (a name is read to 300 characters), and `cases` the case numbers of the newest orders, each once, 10 at most. Address: `https://www.ftc.gov/data.json?dataset=civil-penalty-cases&defendant=<id>`. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never copied:** the case name is read only up to its first parenthesis that is no readable nickname (below): that parenthesis, closed or not, and all that follows it are cut before anything else is read (brands and often individual defendants are written there), so that a company named after it is lost with it (`Examplecorp Inc. (the brand) and Quorb LLC` and `Examplecorp Inc. (Brandname); Quorb LLC` store `Examplecorp Inc.`); on 2026-11-10, 100 of the 391 case names hold a parenthesis (2 of them leave one open) and 25 have text after the first closing mark; reading a name without its parenthetical groups instead would store another name for 3 of them; the `Office` column and every other cell that is not read (the case number, case name, amount, day and basis are read); the rest of the catalog. A party that the rule reads as a person is never copied: the party rule (`ftcPenaltyPartiesOf`, built on `looksLikePerson` and `hasLegalForm` in `kit/persons.ts`) drops a party that is a person's name, with its row's share of the case, before anything is stored. The kit's rule has a low precision on this list, so a firm whose name has a person's shape (two capitalized words and no legal form) is dropped too. On 2026-11-10, of the 391 case rows 65 have no party left after the rule, 3 have only a party of under 4 letters and digits, and the other 323 give the 293 companies. **Design decision:** the rule closes more shapes than the kit's does, and every one fails closed (a company is lost rather than a person kept; the cost in recall is accepted). A group after a comma that begins with `and`, `&` or `+` goes on with the group before it, and a person in such a unit drops the unit; `et al` is cut off, and a party that ended in it with no legal form is dropped. The `Last, First` shape (a one-word group with no legal form, then another one, legal wording or a group that the rule drops: `Sample, Casey`, `Sample, individually`) drops its whole party, as do two one-word brands in a row (`Alpha, Beta`) and a one-word brand before a person. With a surname that is no bare word (two to four words, a suffix such as `Jr.`, a particle that is not taken out, `Estate of`: `Sample Lee, Casey`) the person's group, the given name after it and everything after the given name go, because a firm can be named as a bare word, a comma and the rest of its name, and the rest alone would be a name with its front cut off; what stands before the person stays (`Examplecorp Inc., Sample Lee, Casey` stores `Examplecorp Inc.`). A given name that carries a legal form goes with it (`Sample Lee, Casey, LLC`), as does a one-word company with its legal form after a person's group (`Casey Sample, Zorbex, Inc.`); a bare surname keeps its form (`Sample, Casey, LLC` is stored whole). The particles `de del la las los el en` and `y` are taken out before a group is judged. `doing business as`, `trading as` and `t/a` split a name as `d/b/a` does (the person before it is dropped, the business after it stays), and a group of legal wording alone (`individually`, `jointly and severally`) is no party. A name that goes on under another name (`a/k/a`, `aka`, `f/k/a`, `n/k/a`) is the same defendant's: when every principal party is a person, only the business that `d/b/a`, `dba`, `t/a`, `doing business as` or `trading as` names is stored, and a name that the person goes by goes with the person. For the person test only, and never in the stored text, every quotation mark and every dash of every script is read through a fixed table (quotation marks and primes count for no character, dashes for a hyphen, and the backtick, the acute accent and a typographic single mark between two letters for an apostrophe), so that `Casey "Robin" Sample` is a person's name with a nickname; a name with an acute accent is stored with the apostrophe. The head of a name ends at a dash that separates (a blank, one or two dashes of any script, a blank): `Examplecorp Inc. - Casey Sample` stores the company, and `Casey Sample - Examplecorp Inc.` stores nothing. A nickname in parentheses, brackets or braces (one or two capitalized words or initials, no digit, no lower-case word, no mark) is left out of what the rule judges, so that `Casey (Robin) Sample` reads as `Casey Sample`; a parenthesis counts as one only when it is closed, a text stands before it and a word follows it, with none of a comma, a slash, a semicolon, an alias mark or a legal form between (`Zorbex (USA) Inc.` is cut at the parenthesis and stores `Zorbex`). What is stored is the group as it was written, cut at its parenthesis, so that a nickname in parentheses is never stored and what follows it in its group is lost with it (`Examplecorp Inc. (Brandname) and Quorb LLC` stores `Examplecorp Inc.`); brackets and braces are only left out of the judging and never cut, so that a nickname in them stays in the stored text of a group that stays (`Examplecorp Inc. [Brandname]` is stored whole). An estate is a person's: a piece that holds `Estate of`, or a name of more than one word that ends in `Estate`, is dropped, and a company that stands before it stays. **Design decision:** the shapes below are not closed, and each is stored as the rule gives it: a name of five words or more, once its particles are out, has no shape that the rule knows (`Mary Ann Casey Lee Sample` is kept); a digit makes a piece no person's (`Casey Sample 3rd`); a join with no blank round it, a `y` between a company with a legal form and a person, and `with`, `et`, `und` and `e` are no joins, so that such a party is stored whole with the person in it; a trust that names its trustee is stored (without a comma, the trustee's name with it), and so is a trust named after a person (`Robin Sample Revocable Trust`); a person whose surname is a word of the kit's lists (`Casey Hotel`) is read as a company and kept; a parenthesis that is no nickname is cut and its head is stored (`Casey (the owner) Sample` stores `Casey`), as is a nickname that no word follows or that a comma, a slash, a semicolon, an alias mark, a legal form, `&`, `+`, a word in quotation marks, a digit or a dash follows; a bracket or brace that is no nickname stays in the name; a run of three dashes, or a dash that touches the word after it, separates nothing, so that a person after it stays in the party; a firm named as a list that begins with a group of a person's shape (`Zorbex Lee, Quillon Brightzorb Inc.`) is stored without its first group; and four shapes that no case name of the file of 2026-11-10 has: a bracket or brace that holds a name after a company or an alias mark is stored whole, a nickname inside a company name stores the given name (`Casey (Robin) Sample LLC` gives `Casey`), separators and marks outside the ruled classes (a colon, `\|`, a tilde, a bullet, a middle dot, the CJK corner brackets, a combining mark with no precomposed form) keep a person, and estate wordings other than `Estate of` and a final `Estate` (`Estates of ...`, `Estate for ...`) are kept. No name, id or key is made of what the rule drops, and a text that was cut is never an id, a key or an identifier. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: the company's distinct spellings (5 at most, the ones that the most orders use first), the alias parties of its rows (the names after `d/b/a`, `dba`, `t/a`, `doing business as`, `trading as`, `f/k/a`, `a/k/a`, `aka` and `n/k/a`, which are names of the first company of the row; 3 at most) and, for a spelling that is a host (a name ending in `.com`, `.net`, `.org`, `.info`, `.biz` or `.us`), its label before the suffix; as written and without the legal form, of 4 compact characters or more. No `domain:` or `regno:` key. On 2026-11-10 the 293 records carry 558 keys (557 distinct), all of them name keys. **A vendor that really is the defendant gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2). |
| Verdict | **Built, open.** One whole workbook that states its own total and sum, with real defendant names. An entry says that the FTC's table holds an order against a company of that name, never that the vendor broke a rule now. Firms with person-shaped names are lost with the persons, and on 2026-11-10 one record of the real file (a firm named as a list behind a group of a person's shape) is lost to the given-name rule. **Design decision:** these costs are accepted: `Casey Sample, Examplecorp, Inc.` is lost, as `Sample Lee, Casey, LLC` cannot be told from it; `Zorbex Estate of Example Inc.`, `Examplecorp Real Estate of Ohio LLC` and a firm of two words that ends in `Estate` are dropped as an estate; `Zorbex (Robin) Brightzorb` and `Zorbex (USA) Brightzorb` (a one-word firm, a one-word parenthesis, a word, no legal form) and a US place name that begins with a capitalized Spanish particle and has no legal form (`Las Vegas Zorbex`) are dropped as a person's; a company that stands beside two nicknames goes with them (`Casey (Robin) Sample, Zorbex (Robin) Brightzorb Holdings` stores nothing); a mark glued to a comma after a nickname (`Casey (Robin) dba, Examplecorp Inc.`) is no alias mark, so that the name is read as a list and the given name stays in it. In the real file of 2026-11-10 no name has the word `Estate`, a one-word firm followed by a one-word parenthesis and a word, a capitalized Spanish particle as its first word, or an alias mark glued to a comma after a parenthesis. |

### D2.26 PipeRoll Agent Incident Registry

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | PipeRoll, an independent registry kept by one editor ([page](https://piperoll.org/data/)): editor-written accounts of incidents involving AI agents, each with a permanent `PIR` id. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · an AI incident list · vendor, by name: a company or product named as an agent's operator or as a brand in a title. Class UNVERIFIED (one editor), strength weak (D5.2). An event (`agent_incidents`): the 24-month lookback applies, by the newest entry day (a month or a year counts from its first day). It raises a risk: the analysis prompt's paragraph for AI incident lists words it "An entity named … appears in …", at most one risk, in `ai_specific`, Possible at most. Researched only for a vendor with a website. At most 1 record goes to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://piperoll.org/registry.json`, one static JSON export. Observed on 2026-11-10: 280,142 bytes, `application/json; charset=utf-8`, a weak `ETag`, `Last-Modified` 2026-11-04 13:09 UTC. No total: 110 records (106 corroborated, corrected or disputed, and 4 withdrawn), ids PIR-2026-0001 to PIR-2026-0116 with 6 retired, equal to the site's landing page and to the CSV export cell by cell. |
| Auth, limits | No key. At most 4 MiB, `application/json`, 60 s, a conditional GET on the weak `ETag` and `Last-Modified`. Answers must come from `piperoll.org`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. Fewer than 50 readable entries is not the list; more than 5,000 fails as `too_large`. A root that is not an object, or a `records` that is not a non-empty array, is not the list (`wrong_root`). An entry is unreadable when its id is not `PIR-YYYY-NNNN` or occurs twice (both are left out, so that the order of the file never decides which survives), its `sort_date` is not a day, a month with day 00 or a year with month and day 00 (41 of the 110 dates on 2026-11-10 are of the last two kinds; they are read as a precision and a first day), its status begins with none of `corroborated`, `corrected`, `disputed` or `withdrawn` (an entry that is withdrawn counts nowhere and is not unreadable), its `url` is not its own page, its title, cause or severity is empty, or it gives a name whose compact form is over 160 characters. The unreadable entries are counted, and more than the kit's allowance fails the parse. |
| Cadence, increments | Daily at 11:21 UTC (`21 11 * * *`); stale after 48 hours. A key that a later copy lacks, or spells differently, becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **CC BY 4.0, per the registry's `incidents/LICENSE` and its site; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q38).** The repository's MIT license covers the tooling only. This is not the registry that D2.18 lists as not built (487 records, a security vendor, no export, no license): it has 110 records, an export and one individual editor. Attribution: `PipeRoll Agent Incident Registry (piperoll.org), CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/); each record cites the PIR ids of the entries it shows and links the newest entry. Adapted by VerifyWise: titles shortened and edited, entries counted; not endorsed by PipeRoll.` |
| Record | One per name entity (`agent_incidents`; `publishedAt` is the day of the newest entry). An entry has no vendor field, so its names come from free text by two rules measured on the real file: rule P, the head of the first parenthetical of `operator_type` when the cell begins with `enterprise` or `startup` (one token that holds a capital letter; a slash gives two names); rule B, brand-shaped title tokens (a lower-case letter followed later by a capital, no digit) that are not person-shaped (see the next row). An entry counts once for each distinct key it yields; withdrawn entries (4) count nowhere, and an entry that gives no name is no entity (neither a record nor unreadable). The id is the compact name (at most 160 characters). On 2026-11-10: 36 records from the 110 entries (90 pairs of an entry and a key), at most 33 entries for one, 0 unreadable. A record keeps the entry count, counts by status word and by severity, the first and last year, the newest 3 entries (id, date at its precision, cause, severity, the title as the text quotes it, cut to 80) and the ids of the others (at most 100 ids in all). Text: `PipeRoll Agent Incident Registry (piperoll.org), an AI incident list.`, then `Each entry is one editor's account of an incident involving an AI agent, not a finding of any authority, and naming a company in an entry does not say that it caused the incident.`, the counts and `Source: PipeRoll, CC BY 4.0; titles shortened and edited, and entries counted, by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 658, maximum 1,084 on 2026-11-10). Address: `https://piperoll.org/pir/<year>-<number>/` of the newest entry. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never read or stored:** `aiid_incident_id` (not even counted; Q29); the `operator_type` cell beyond its category word and the head of its first parenthetical (one cell holds an e-mail address, one names a private person; the cell is read in `read` and never kept, and the category `individual` keeps an individual out of rule P); every prose field (editor text that quotes third parties and names persons), the `*_key` fields that no record uses, and the entry's `url` as written (it is checked against the id, and the record's address is built from the id). **Design decision:** the rules keep the natural persons whose shape they know out of the keys and of a record's text, and they fail closed, with no exception and no list of company names (a company that the operator cell names still has its key by rule P). A title word of rule B is never a name when it is person-shaped: a surname with a prefix (Mc, Mac, De, Di, Da, La, Le, Van, Von, Du, Del, Dela, Der, Den, Ter, Ten, Fitz, San or St, then one or more capitalized parts that each have a lower-case letter, with one capital glued in front of it at most, as the initial of a given name) or an account handle (three or more lower-case letters before the first capital). `ZoRB`, `DeepZorb` and `MacroZorb` have neither shape; single-capital words, acronyms and model versions are never names. A word that a letter or a combining mark touches (an accented letter, a letter of another script) is the stub of a longer word and is never a name. Only the part of a word before a hyphen or an apostrophe gives a name. In a case caption (`<party> v. <company>`, the separator being `v`, `v.`, `vs` or `vs.` in any letter case with a blank on each side) the text before the first separator, the first party, is a natural person in every caption of the real file and gives no key. A title that the reader cut (it ends in the cut marker) is read without its last word, which may be a fragment, so that no key is made of a cut text. The title of each of the newest entries is quoted in the text and kept in the stored row by one function: the text before the first separator of a caption is quoted as `A party` (the separator stays as written) and each person-shaped part of a word, every part of a hyphenated or apostrophe-joined word being judged, as `[name]`; the digits at the two ends of a part are no part of a name, a part of digits only is never one, and a cut title is quoted as far as it was read, then the cut marker. A product written like a handle (`macOS`, `openAI`) is quoted as `[name]` and gives no key. The name in the identifiers is the first spelling whole, and the title and the text show it cut to 60 characters. Residuals (quoted as written; a residual word with the brand shape of rule B, such as `QuillAnn`, is also a key): a person in the second position of a caption; a person whose name has neither shape (`QuillAnn`, `joZorb`, `xXzorbXx`, a lower-case letter or two capitals glued in front of a surname); the other part of a double-barrelled name of which one part only has a shape; a surname that apostrophes cut in the middle of its own parts; digits inside a word; a handle or a surname written wholly in letters that the ASCII reader of a title does not see (accented letters, Cyrillic), which gives no key and is quoted as written; a caption written with another word (`against`, `&`, `versus`) or with no blank on a side; the first party of a caption whose separator the cut of a title to 300 characters reached or went past. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: the key's distinct spellings (5 at most), as written and without the legal form, of 4 compact characters or more; no `domain:`, `lei:` or `regno:` key. Rule B keys a victim or a platform as well as a culprit, and a key can be a word that is no vendor; a company whose name in a title has a person's shape gets no key from that title (it still gets one when the registry names it as an operator: rule P), which is the cost of the rule. **A vendor that really is named gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2). |
| Verdict | **Built weak, open (Q38).** The export is the whole registry and is licensed, but it has no vendor field and one editor. An entry says that an editor's account names a company of that name, never that the company caused the incident. |

### D2.27 European Commission competition cases

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The European Commission, DG Competition: its open data on antitrust, cartel and Digital Markets Act (DMA) cases ([page](https://competition-policy.ec.europa.eu/about/news/dg-competitions-case-data-available-open-data-2025-03-18_en)). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · an enforcement or case list · vendor, by name: an undertaking named in a case. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An event (`eu_competition_cases`): the 36-month lookback applies, by the newest activity day (the latest decision, else the opening). It raises a risk, in the analysis prompt's paragraph for enforcement and case lists: "An entity named … appears in …", at most one risk, Possible at most; a case is not a finding. At most 2 records go to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | Two static JSON objects in the portal's storage bucket (`compcases-open-data-portal-files-prod.s3.eu-west-1.amazonaws.com`): `/case-data-AT.json` (antitrust and cartels) and `/case-data-DMA.json`. Observed on 2026-11-10: AT 2,868,173 bytes, 758 cases, `Last-Modified` 2026-11-09 05:00 UTC; DMA 282,052 bytes, 55 cases, `Last-Modified` 2026-11-10 00:00 UTC; keyed by case number. No stated total: the dates reach the start of each regime (AT 1974-01-04, DMA 2022-10-26) and the objects are rewritten daily. The merger and state-aid files are never requested. |
| Auth, limits | No key. AT at most 8 MiB, DMA at most 4 MiB, `application/octet-stream` or `application/json`, 120 s each. Both files are asked in full, one after the other, every week (no conditional GET) and the hash tells an unchanged copy. **Design decision:** the bucket's robots.txt answers 403 (no rules), so the house pause of 1 s (`HOST_PAUSE_MS` in `kit/pause.ts`) is waited once, between the two requests: not before the first, not after the second, and never when the first request failed or its answer was refused. Each answer must come from the bucket host at exactly `/case-data-AT.json` or `/case-data-DMA.json`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. AT with fewer than 600 readable cases or an earliest initiation after 1980-12-31, or DMA with fewer than 40 or after 2022-12-31, is not the list; a file that is not an object of case numbers is not the list; a file of more than 5,000 cases fails as `too_large` and never gives a shorter list. A case that does not have the shape (a key that is not its number, a value that is no array where the portal writes one, more than 60 companies or more than 30 decisions; the largest case on 2026-11-10 has 42 companies and 6 decisions) is unreadable: it is counted, and more than the allowance fails the parse. |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Monday at 17:51 UTC (`51 17 * * 1`); stale after 192 hours. The objects carry a 30-day lifecycle: if the portal stops rewriting them, the address answers 403 or 404 within a month and the sync fails visibly, never as an empty list. An undertaking that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **Commission reuse notice (Decision 2011/833/EU), CC BY 4.0; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q45).** The notice excludes logos and third-party rights and warns that private individuals may need clearing, so only the antitrust, cartel and DMA files are copied, without what the "Left out" row lists. Attribution: `Source: European Commission, DG Competition case data (open data), (c) European Union, reused under the Commission reuse notice (CC BY 4.0). Changes made by VerifyWise.` |
| Record | One per undertaking (`eu_competition_cases`; `publishedAt` is the newest activity day). A case counts once for each distinct undertaking it names. The id is the compact name of the short name. It keeps the case count, counts by kind (cartel, antitrust, DMA), the first and last initiation year and the newest 3 cases (number, kind, title of a cartel or DMA case, legal basis, number of parties, latest decision or else the opening day) and the numbers of the newest 10. On 2026-11-10: 2,166 undertakings from 813 cases (758 antitrust and cartel, 55 DMA), at most 16 cases for one, 194 with a newest activity day inside the 36 months before that day, 0 unreadable. Text: `European Commission competition cases (competition-policy.ec.europa.eu), an enforcement or case list.` and, last, `A case listing is not a finding of infringement against a listed party unless a decision addressed to it says so; mergers are not in this list. Source: European Commission, DG Competition case data, (c) European Union, CC BY 4.0; adapted by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 681, maximum 1,128 on 2026-11-10); when the sentences are longer the oldest of the newest cases are left out one at a time (the third, then the second), and the closing sentence is never cut. The title and the text show the name cut at 100 characters. Address: the page above with `?undertaking=<id>`. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never copied:** attachments, links, press releases and timelines; the title of every non-cartel antitrust case (574 cases on 2026-11-10); every other metadata value. What is read of a case: its number, the kind of its file, its companies, the day it was opened, the label of its legal basis, its title (cartel and DMA cases only) and, of each decision, its day and the label of its type. **Person rule** (`euOrgEvidence` in `kit/persons.ts`): in a non-cartel antitrust case a company name is kept only with an organization marker (a digit or symbol, a legal form, an organization word or an acronym of 2 to 6 capitals) or when the same name occurs in 2 or more cases of the two files (counted before the rule); cartel and DMA names are all kept. A name that shows none is a person's and is dropped before anything is built: it is not stored and not even counted. On 2026-11-10 it drops 206 of the 1,117 distinct names of the 574 non-cartel antitrust cases (880 are kept by an organization marker and 31 only by a second case); a hand reading made when the rule was designed found 11 natural persons among these names, and all 11 were among the dropped. It costs organizations known by one brand word in one case. **Residuals:** a natural person named in a cartel or DMA case, in two cases or more, or under a name that shows an organization marker (an acronym, a digit or a symbol) is kept as a name. **Design decision:** a company name longer than 120 characters once folded names no undertaking: it is left out of its case before anything is counted or grouped and is never cut into a key or an id (a cut of the name at 120 characters would make two long names that share a stem one undertaking); like a name of fewer than 4 compact characters it is neither a record nor an unreadable case, and the other companies of the case stay (the longest name of the files of 2026-11-10 has 100 characters). The name that is shown is cut at 100 characters; the id, the keys and the identifier `undertaking` hold the whole name, and a cut text is never an identifier. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: the undertaking's distinct spellings (5 at most), as written and without the legal form; a compact name under 4 characters gets no key. No `domain:`, `lei:` or `regno:` key. The same group under two names is two undertakings, so **a vendor that really is a party gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2) only under the spelling it uses. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q45).** Two whole objects whose dates reach the start of each regime, with a company field and a measured person rule. An entry says that a case names an undertaking of that name as a party, never that the vendor infringed; a merger is never listed. |

### D2.28 disclose.io program list

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | disclose.io, the community-maintained `diodb` repository ([page](https://github.com/disclose/diodb)): the vulnerability disclosure programs that organizations publish, as contributors report them by pull request. An entry is a contributor's claim, and a missing entry says nothing. |
| Category · level | `security_incidents` · a register or status list · vendor, by name or, under a strict rule, by the policy's own website. Class UNVERIFIED, weak by name and strong by `domain:` (D5.2). A state (`disclosure_program`): no date on a row, offered as of the copy, no lookback. Context only: the analysis prompt's paragraph for registers lets it describe another risk and raises none, and it never supports a credibility label: a finding that cites registers only reads "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk.", and a stale or withdrawn register record never names itself in the line of a label (`credibility.ts`). At most 1 record goes to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/disclose/diodb/master/program-list.json`, one static JSON array. Observed on 2026-11-10: 1,183,787 bytes, `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, a weak `ETag`, no `Last-Modified`, a CDN copy with `max-age=300`. 2,433 objects with distinct `program_name`s in strict code-point order (the project's CI sorts the file and enforces uniqueness). No total and no date per row: the live directory may be ahead of the repository. |
| Auth, limits | No key. At most 8 MiB, `text/plain` or `application/json`, 60 s, a conditional GET on the `ETag`. Answers must come from `raw.githubusercontent.com` under `/disclose/diodb/`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. More than 20,000 rows fails as `too_large`, before any other check; a root that is not an array, a duplicate `program_name`, names out of code-point order or fewer than 1,500 rows is not the list; a row with no text for `program_name` or `policy_url`, with a name over 80 characters or an address over 2,048 characters once cleaned (neither is ever cut or read in part: a cut name would be an id, a key and a title with the cut marker in it, and a cut address a host that may end inside the host), or with a name that has no letter or digit, is unreadable (more than the allowance fails the parse). |
| Cadence, increments | Contributions arrive by pull request. Weekly, Saturday at 20:08 UTC (`8 20 * * 6`); stale after 192 hours. No delta and no removal marker: a program that the file no longer holds becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **CC0 1.0 per the `diodb` LICENSE file; the README says CC BY 4.0, so the list is attributed as CC BY 4.0 requires; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q49).** Contact e-mails, contact URLs and the full policy URLs are not copied. Attribution: `disclose.io diodb (https://github.com/disclose/diodb), CC0 1.0 per its LICENSE, attributed as CC BY 4.0 per its README. Entries are community contributions; adapted by VerifyWise.` |
| Record | One per organization and policy host (`disclosure_program`; no `publishedAt`): rows grouped by the compact name of the short name of `program_name` and the policy host, up to 3 programs per record. The id is `<compact name>@<policy host>` (`@none` without a host), so two organizations with alike names on different hosts stay apart. **Design decision:** a key of more than 200 characters gives the id its first 183 characters, a tilde and the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the whole key, so that two organizations whose keys differ only after the cut never share an id (no id of the file of 2026-11-10 is that long: the longest has 70 characters). On 2026-11-10: 1,843 records: 1,870 organizations from 1,872 kept rows of 2,433 (21 rows with a dead policy link and 540 whose policy address is shared by 50 or more rows are skipped; 0 unreadable), 27 of which have no key of either kind and make no record. Per program: name, host, safe harbor (`full`, `partial`, `none` or `not stated`) and bounty (`yes`, `no`, `partial` or `not stated`). Text: `disclose.io program list (github.com/disclose), a register or status list.`, a sentence per program, then `Entries are submitted by contributors and are not verified by the organisation or by disclose.io; a missing entry says nothing. Attributed as CC BY 4.0 (disclose.io diodb); adapted by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 453, maximum 612 on 2026-11-10); when the sentences are longer the third and then the second program are left out, and the closing sentence is never cut. Address: `https://github.com/disclose/diodb?program=<id>`; no contributor-supplied URL is linked. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never copied:** `contact_email` (1,289 values) and `contact_url`; `pgp_key`, `hiring`, `hall_of_fame`, `securitytxt_url`, `preferred_languages`, `disclosure_timeline_days`, `launch_date`, `offers_swag` and `public_disclosure`; the umbrella scopes (540 rows whose policy URL is shared by 50 or more rows, among them 183 users' own subdomains on hosting platforms); dead links (21); the full policy URL, of which only the host is shown and keyed. Of a row only `program_name`, `policy_url`, `safe_harbor` and `offers_bounty` are read, and `policy_url_status`, which is compared with the word `dead` only; the body of a snapshot holds the organizations that the rows make and nothing else. The rows that share an address are counted over the whole file, dead and unreadable rows included. No person rule is applied to the program names, which are organizations and products: the profile of the list labeled all 184 names that a name-shape rule flags (none was a person) and a sample of 30 that it does not (none); the only names of individuals are the 183 program names that are a customer's or user's own subdomain on a hosting platform, and every one of them belongs to the umbrella scopes, which are never read; a person's mailbox is in `contact_email`, which is never read. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Name keys of each program name (`nameKeysOf`; 48 names under 4 compact characters give none). A strong `domain:` key from the policy host only for a row whose policy address is used by exactly one row of the file, whose host is an apex domain (two labels, or three whose last two are one of a short list of country suffixes; a country suffix itself is none), is not shared hosting and is not a bug-bounty or document platform (14 are named, among them `bugcrowd.com`, `hackerone.com` and `docs.google.com`). A record is keyed when any row of its group is, whether or not that row is one of the 3 shown. **Design decision:** a host is keyed only when exactly one record carries it (counted over the groups that would carry it as a key): when two or more organizations would carry the same `domain:` key none does (13 hosts and 30 records on 2026-11-10), so that a vendor matched by its domain never gets a record that names another program; each keeps its name keys, and a group with no other key makes no record. On 2026-11-10, 1,005 of the 1,843 records carry a `domain:` key. A group with no key of either kind is dropped and is not counted as unreadable (27 on 2026-11-10). |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q49).** One whole, CI-checked file, kept as a context-only register of unverified community entries. A domain match says that the policy page sits on the vendor's verified domain and nothing about its quality; a name match is a weak hint. It raises no risk. |

### D2.29 UK Competition and Markets Authority cases

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Competition and Markets Authority (CMA), which publishes its case pages on GOV.UK ([page](https://www.gov.uk/cma-cases)). |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · an enforcement or case list · vendor, by name: a party read from a case title. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An event (`competition_case_summary`): the 36-month lookback applies to the newest kept case; an entity with an open kept case has no `publishedAt`, so the lookback never hides a live case. It raises a risk, in the analysis prompt's paragraph for enforcement and case lists: "An entity named … appears in …", at most one risk, Possible at most. At most 1 record goes to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | The GOV.UK Search API: `https://www.gov.uk/api/search.json?filter_format=cma_case&count=1000&order=-public_timestamp`, 12 fields and `start`. Observed on 2026-11-10: 3 pages (`start` 0, 1000, 2000) of 768,792, 675,046 and 402,718 bytes, `application/json`, no `ETag` or `Last-Modified`; each page states `total` 2,593 and the 2,593 links are distinct. A case date is `closed_date`, else `opened_date`, never `public_timestamp` (the republication time). |
| Auth, limits | No key. Each page 8 MiB, `application/json`, 60 s; at most 5 pages, 400 ms apart, through the shared pager `govukSearchPages`; a first answer that needs more pages is not the list (`wrong_root`). Answers must come from `www.gov.uk` under `/api/`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. The search is not a snapshot: a case published between two pages shifts the rows, so the reader returns null (`wrong_root`) unless every page states the same `total`, begins where it was asked and the distinct links equal `total`; a failed sync withdraws nothing. A link that comes twice is one result. A case is unreadable when it has no text for a title or an address, no case type or no state, or when a kept case has a title that is empty once folded or longer than 500 characters, or an address that is not a page under `/cma-cases/` (200 characters at most); the unreadable cases are counted, and more than the allowance fails the parse. |
| Cadence, increments | The API sends no validator, so each run downloads the pages and the hash tells an unchanged copy. Weekly, Tuesday at 08:16 UTC (`16 8 * * 2`); stale after 192 hours. An entity that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **Open Government Licence v3.0; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q52, with the OFSI and MHRA lists), in the way of the UK Sanctions List (D2.3).** Only the GOV.UK search metadata of the case pages is read; case documents and party submissions are third-party content and are never fetched. Attribution: `UK Competition and Markets Authority cases, published on GOV.UK. Contains public sector information licensed under the Open Government Licence v3.0. Adapted by VerifyWise (party names extracted from case titles and cases counted); no endorsement by the Competition and Markets Authority is implied.` |
| Record | One per party entity (`competition_case_summary`), from the cases that can carry an intervention or an enforcement outcome: 456 of 2,593 on 2026-11-10 (consumer protection, competition, cartel and digital markets cases without a no-action or no-grounds outcome, and mergers that are open or ended in a phase 2 prohibition, a phase 2 clearance with remedies or phase 1 undertakings in lieu); 266 of them give an entity (268 name a party before the parties of under 4 letters and digits are dropped) and 190 give none, which is a gap of the list and not an error. The id is `cma:` and the compact name of the short name of the party; a former and a current name are two records unless a `(now X)` alias joins them. 454 entities and records, 0 unreadable, at most 6 cases for one, 63 with an open kept case. A record keeps the counts by case type, the open count and the newest 3 cases (title cut at 80 characters, type, state, the CMA's own outcome words and the day). Text: `UK Competition and Markets Authority cases (gov.uk), an enforcement or case list.`, then `A case entry names a party to a merger review or to a competition, consumer protection or digital markets case; it is not a finding against the party unless its outcome says so.`, the counts and `Adapted by VerifyWise from GOV.UK under the Open Government Licence v3.0.` Title `CMA cases: <party>`; address `https://www.gov.uk` and the link of the newest case, so that the entities of one case share an address. On 2026-11-10 the summaries are 497 to 808 characters (median 558.5), and the 454 records have 258 distinct addresses. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never stored:** `description`, `market_sector`, the constants and the envelope fields; every dropped case (a case that is not kept has no title read, and it never reaches the body); the case pages and documents. Disqualification cases are not kept. **Design decision:** the one rule of the connector about natural persons is the honorific: a party that begins with `Mr`, `Mrs`, `Ms` or `Miss` (with or without a full stop, then a blank) is dropped, and a former or new name of that form is left out of the names of its party; the case stays for its other parties, and the title of such a case, which the record of its other parties quotes among its newest cases, is the CMA's public wording and is not cut for persons (no real row is affected: 0 of 2,593 on 2026-11-10; the cost is a person's name inside a quoted CMA title of another party's record). A person named without one of the four honorifics is not caught: the name stays a party and a key (a residual that the rule does not close). A party over 120 characters, or with fewer than 4 letters and digits once its legal form is off, is dropped. **Design decision:** a kept case whose title is empty once folded or longer than 500 characters (the longest real title has 252) is unreadable and counted, never taken for a case that names no party and never cut to one that fits; a kept case that gives no party is read and gives no row. The outcome shown is the CMA's own words, whole up to 60 characters (the longest label has 57), and the title of a newest case is cut at 80 characters. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: every distinct spelling of the party and each `(now X)` or `(formerly X)` alias (10 at most), as written and without the legal form, of 4 compact characters or more. Parties come from titles by four rules measured in-sample on 2026-11-10 (57 of 62 merger names clean; 45 of 46 colon heads); about 8 % of merger names are not clean, and a new title shape gives no party. **A vendor that really is a party gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2). |
| Verdict | **Built weak, open (Q52).** The whole list is proven (3 pages, `total` 2,593, 2,593 distinct links), but the parties exist only in the case titles. An entry says that a CMA case that can carry an intervention or an enforcement outcome names a party of that name; a merger party is a party to a review, never a wrongdoer. |

### D2.30 Government of Canada AI Register

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Treasury Board of Canada Secretariat, which publishes the register of AI systems that federal departments use ([page](https://open.canada.ca/data/en/dataset/fcbc0200-79ba-4fa4-94a6-00e32facea6b)). |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · a register or status list · vendor, by name: a supplier named by a department. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). A state (`algorithm_register_supplier`): a release, offered as of the copy, no lookback. Context only: the analysis prompt's paragraph for registers lets it describe another risk and raises none, and it never supports a credibility label: a finding that cites registers only reads "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk.", and a stale or withdrawn register record never names itself in the line of a label (`credibility.ts`). Researched only for a vendor with a website. At most 1 record goes to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | Two requests to `open.canada.ca`: the dataset record `https://open.canada.ca/data/api/action/package_show?id=fcbc0200-79ba-4fa4-94a6-00e32facea6b` which names the CSV resource, then that resource, which answers 302 to the operator's storage host (named below) under a signed address that is never stored, logged or put in an error. Observed on 2026-11-10: the CSV is 687,081 bytes, `text/csv`, `Last-Modified` 2026-04-28 20:10 UTC; 412 data rows of 24 cells. The file name embeds the release, so the record is read first each time. |
| Auth, limits | No key. Record: at most 1 MiB, 60 s, from `open.canada.ca` under `/data/api/action/`. CSV: at most 8 MiB, 120 s, `text/csv` or `application/octet-stream`, answered by `opencanada.blob.core.windows.net` under `/opengovprod/resources/`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. The record must state the license `ca-ogl-lgo`, hold exactly one English CSV resource and give it at the portal's own address under the dataset's `/resource/` path, else `wrong_root`. The requests are 20 s apart (the Crawl-delay, `kit/pause.ts`); a validator `<resource id>\|<last_modified>\|<size>` from the record that equals the stored one gives `unchanged` after one request. A header without one of the six columns that are read by name (`ai_register_id`, `name_ai_system_en`, `government_organization`, `vendor_information`, `developed_by_en`, `ai_system_status_en`) or fewer than 200 data rows is not the list (`wrong_root`); more than 5,000 rows or vendors, a row of over 100 cells or a file that ends inside a quote fails as `too_large`. A row of another width than the header, or with a vendor cell longer than the kit reads (a megabyte), is unreadable: it is counted, and more than the allowance fails the parse. |
| Cadence, increments | One release with no update planned (196 days old on 2026-11-10). Weekly, Monday at 20:31 UTC (`31 20 * * 1`); stale after 192 hours (the age of the copy only). A vendor that a later release lacks becomes `withdrawn`; a new fiscal-year prefix of the register's own ids changes nothing. |
| Licence | **Open Government Licence - Canada 2.0, the dataset record's license (`ca-ogl-lgo`); decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q40).** Canada.ca's terms limit commercial use; the file is published through open.canada.ca. The exemptions (personal information, third-party rights, official symbols) are respected: only supplier, department and system names and statuses are kept. Attribution: `Contains information licensed under the Open Government Licence - Canada (https://open.canada.ca/en/open-government-licence-canada). Source: Government of Canada AI Register (Minimum Viable Product), Treasury Board of Canada Secretariat. Adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by the Government of Canada.` |
| Record | One per vendor key (`algorithm_register_supplier`; no `publishedAt`): the systems whose `vendor_information` names it. The cell is split at a semicolon, a line break, ` / ` and a comma that no legal form (`Inc`, `Ltd`, `LLC`, `Corp`, `Corporation`, `Limited`, `Subs`) follows. **Design decision:** an ampersand is no separator, because `Acme & Sons` is one firm and a split would make a record of a half name that is the exact name of another company (a cell that really names two vendors with an ampersand is one name: a weak miss, never a wrong match). A name gives a key when it is no placeholder and has at least 4 letters and digits once its legal form is off (`Abc` and `Xqz` give none). The id is the compact name of the short name, so `Open Zorb` and `OpenZorb` are one record and `Zorbex Cloud` stays apart from `Zorbex`. On 2026-11-10: 120 records from the 412 systems of the register (195 pairs of a system and a vendor), at most 41 systems for one, 0 unreadable. A record keeps the system count, counts by status, up to 3 departments and 3 system names; the register's own system ids carry the fiscal year and are not stable from release to release, so they are kept in the identifiers only (20 at most). Text: `Government of Canada AI Register (open.canada.ca), a register or status list.`, then `A record is federal departments' own statement that AI systems they use name this vendor in their vendor information; it says nothing about the vendor's conduct or the systems' quality.`, the counts and `The register is not planned to be updated. Contains information licensed under the Open Government Licence - Canada; adapted by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 583, maximum 761 on 2026-11-10), the closing sentence never cut. Address: `https://open.canada.ca/data/en/dataset/fcbc0200-79ba-4fa4-94a6-00e32facea6b?vendor=<id>`. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never read:** the departments' prose (descriptions, capabilities, data sources, results and personal information banks), every `_fr` column, the other flags, and the record's `maintainer_email` and every other contact field. Of the 24 columns six are read by name and the rest are dropped before a row is built (`developed_by_en` is read to know the file and never printed). No rule separates persons in the vendor column (the best rule found one in five): the one person that the file names among 127 distinct names is a sole trader that a department itself names as the supplier, the residual of under one percent is accepted, and a record names the operator's own statement, whoever the supplier is. **Design decision:** a text that was cut is never a key, an id or an identifier. The vendor cell is read to 200 characters; when it was cut (the line ends in the cut marker) it is read only up to its last real separator (a semicolon, which a line break becomes, or ` / `), and the last name goes with everything that follows that separator, commas included, because a comma before a legal form joins two parts of one name and a cut can fall right after that comma or inside the legal form; a cut cell with no such separator names no vendor (no real cell is that long: the longest has 95 characters). The id, the keys and the identifier `vendor` hold the whole name, and only the title and the text show it cut at 80 characters. The departments of a vendor are counted by the whole English part of the department cell (a cell that does not fit 300 characters as a line is left out of the count) and only the three that are shown are cut. A vendor cell longer than the kit reads makes its row unreadable, because it would read as naming no vendor. The signed address of the file is never stored, logged or put in an error. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: the key's distinct spellings (5 at most), as written and without the legal form. No `domain:` key (the cell has no URLs). A maker and its cloud product are two keys (`Zorbex` and `Zorbex Cloud` stay apart). **A vendor that really is named gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2). |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q40).** Complete by three checks (size, rows, row width), with a real supplier field. An entry says that federal departments report an AI system that names a supplier of that name, never anything about the supplier's conduct, and a missing entry says nothing. |

### D2.31 FTC nonmerger enforcement actions

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The US Federal Trade Commission's table of nonmerger (antitrust) enforcement actions, published as one CSV ([catalog record](https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/ftc-nonmerger-enforcement-actions)). The table has no party field: the only evidence of a company is the matter title. |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · an enforcement or case list · vendor, by name: a party read from a matter title. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An event (`competition_action_summary`): the 36-month lookback applies, by the newest matter day. It raises a risk, in the analysis prompt's paragraph for enforcement and case lists: "An entity named … appears in …", at most one risk, Possible at most. At most 2 records go to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://www.ftc.gov/system/files/ftc_gov/data/FTC-Nonmerger-Enforcement-Actions.csv`, one static CSV. Observed on 2026-11-10: 40,408 bytes, `text/csv; charset=UTF-8`, no `ETag`, `Last-Modified` 2026-11-09 17:20 UTC. 188 data rows of 7 fields. The header says UTF-8 but the bytes are windows-1252, so the connector decodes as windows-1252 whatever the header says. `MatterEnforcementDate` is `M/D/YYYY h:mm:ss`, month first. No stated total: the catalog states coverage from fiscal year 1996 through the third quarter of 2026, and the rows reach both ends (1995-10-26 to 2026-06-30). |
| Auth, limits | No key. At most 1 MiB, `text/csv`, 60 s, a conditional GET on `Last-Modified` (no `ETag`). Answers must come from `www.ftc.gov` under `/system/files/ftc_gov/data/`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. The header must be the 7 names in order (white space and case ignored); a row of other than 7 cells, a matter number that is not 7 digits and perhaps a letter, a title that is empty or longer than 200 characters once cleaned (it is never cut), or a date that is not a real day, has a time other than midnight, or whose fiscal year (1 October rule) differs from `MatterEnforcementFY`, is unreadable (more than the allowance fails the parse); fewer than 150 rows or an earliest day after 1996-12-31 is not the list; more than 5,000 rows or a row of over 16 cells fails as `too_large`. Crawl-delay 5 s: the job runs on another weekday than the civil penalty list. |
| Cadence, increments | A few rows a year. Weekly, Thursday at 04:57 UTC (`57 4 * * 4`); stale after 192 hours. A party that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **CC0 1.0, as the FTC's `data.json` and catalog.data.gov state for the dataset.** The FTC website policy asks for attribution where feasible, implies no endorsement and does not allow the FTC seal. Attribution: `Source: United States Federal Trade Commission, www.ftc.gov (nonmerger enforcement actions, CC0 1.0). Not endorsed by the FTC. Parties read from the matter titles and entries adapted by VerifyWise.` |
| Record | One per party read from the matter titles (`competition_action_summary`; `publishedAt` is the newest matter day). Every parenthetical group of the title is cut, the inner first (a group that is never closed is cut with all that follows it), the title is cut at the first `" - "`, a leading `FTC v` or `FTC v.` is dropped and the rest is split on `/`; a party under 4 compact characters gets no record. The id is the compact name of the short name. **Design decision:** an id of up to 100 characters is the compact name itself; a longer name gives its first 83 characters, a tilde and the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the whole compact name, so that two long names with one start are two records (no compact name holds a tilde; the longest id on 2026-11-10 has 69 characters). On 2026-11-10: 201 parties from 188 rows (total 201, 0 unreadable), at most 2 matters for one (193 parties have 1 matter and 8 have 2), 11 with a newest matter inside the 36 months before that day. A record keeps the matter count, the first and last year and the newest 3 matters (day, number, title cut to 80 characters, kind of action, industry), and the numbers of the newest 10. Text: `FTC nonmerger enforcement actions (ftc.gov), an enforcement or case list.`, `Party: …`, the matters and, last, `VerifyWise read the party from the matter title, so it may be another company of the same name or a group of firms; an enforcement action is not a finding that the vendor broke the law. Not endorsed by the FTC; adapted by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 611, maximum 791 on 2026-11-10). Address: the FTC page of the newest matter that has a link (the address between the first two `#` of the link cell, an `ftc.gov` address of at most 500 characters with no character that markup is made of, upgraded to `https`), else the catalog record with `?party=<id>`; two parties of one matter share the address, and the second is dropped as a duplicate of the same matter. **Design decision:** this is accepted, the one case of two different matters that the file gives one FTC link included: the ledger that drops a record whose address it already holds is that of one web session, a loss needs a single run to match both records, and the link is the FTC's own (on 2026-11-10 the 201 records have 175 distinct addresses; 199 have an FTC link, and the other 2 come from rows whose link cell is empty). |
| Left out and text safety | **Never copied:** every parenthetical group and everything after the first `" - "` of the title (2 of 188 rows name a natural person only there); `MatterEnforcementFY` (a check only); the hyperlink except as the record address. No person-shape rule is applied, because none that was tried works on this field: the parts of a title where a natural person is named (a parenthetical group, or what follows a dash) are cut before anything is read, and a person named in the party part of a title would be a party (a residual of the list, which the closing sentence calls a fragment of a title). **Design decision:** the identifier `party` holds the first spelling of the party whole (a title is read to 200 characters), and a cut text is never an identifier; the title and the summary show it cut at 100 characters, with the marker. A title that was cut (it ends in the cut marker) is unreadable, never the title of a matter. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: the party's spellings (3 at most), as written and without the legal form, of 4 compact characters or more. No `domain:`, `regno:` or `lei:` key. Topic titles become parties that only match a vendor of exactly that name. **A vendor that really is the party gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2). |
| Verdict | **Built weak, open.** One whole, small file (188 matters, matching the stated coverage) whose only company evidence is a free-text title; most parties of a sample of 30 named one organization. An entry says that the table names a party of that name in a matter title, which may be a group of firms or another company of that name; an action is not a finding that the vendor broke the law. |

### D2.32 US federal AI use case inventory

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The US Office of Management and Budget (OMB), which compiles the 2025 Federal Agency AI Use Case Inventory from the agencies' public inventories ([page](https://github.com/ombegov/2025-Federal-Agency-AI-Use-Case-Inventory)). |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · a register or status list · vendor, by name: a vendor that agencies name in their use cases. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). A state (`algorithm_register_supplier`, the type of the other supplier registers): the 2025 edition, offered as of the copy, no lookback. Context only: the analysis prompt's paragraph for registers lets it describe another risk and raises none, and it never supports a credibility label: a finding that cites registers only reads "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk.", and a stale or withdrawn register record never names itself in the line of a label (`credibility.ts`). Researched only for a vendor with a website. At most 1 record goes to a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ombegov/2025-Federal-Agency-AI-Use-Case-Inventory/main/Data/2025_individually_reported_AI_use_cases.csv`, one static CSV. Observed on 2026-11-10: 3,132,295 bytes, `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, a weak `ETag`, no `Last-Modified`. 3,611 data rows of 36 cells, equal to OMB's README totals (use cases, agencies, per-agency counts, 445 high-impact), and the git blob hash and size equal the repository listing. The COTS products file is not requested. |
| Auth, limits | No key. At most 24 MiB, `text/plain`, 120 s, a conditional GET on the `ETag` that the connector stored from its own full GET (the `ETag` differs by request kind). Answers must come from `raw.githubusercontent.com` under `/ombegov/2025-Federal-Agency-AI-Use-Case-Inventory/`, or the download fails as `fetch_failed`. A missing used column, fewer than 2,500 rows or more than 100 agencies is not the list; more than 10,000 rows or vendors, a row of over 100 cells or a file that ends inside a quote fails as `too_large` and never gives a shorter list; a row of another width than the header, whose `agency` is not 1 to 6 capital letters, or whose vendor cell is longer than the kit reads (a megabyte), is unreadable: it is counted, and more than the allowance fails the parse. |
| Cadence, increments | One edition a year, with its path fixed in code: a new edition is invisible until a release adds it. Weekly, Tuesday at 21:33 UTC (`33 21 * * 2`); stale after 192 hours. `main` is a mutable branch, so a new commit changes the blob and the next sync sees it. A vendor that the file no longer holds becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **None published; a work of the US Government (17 U.S.C. §105) compiled by OMB from the agencies' public inventories; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10, in the way of Q31 and Q33 (Q41).** No agency or contractor free text is stored, since contractor text may fall outside §105, and `contact_email` is never read. Attribution: `Source: US Office of Management and Budget, 2025 Federal Agency AI Use Case Inventory (compiled from agencies' public inventories). Entries adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by OMB.` |
| Record | One per vendor key (`algorithm_register_supplier`; no `publishedAt`) from `vendor_name` by rule V (addresses and parentheticals, an unclosed one included, cut out; split at a semicolon, a line break, a slash, `" and "` and a comma that no legal form follows; an ampersand is no separator, so `Acme & Sons` is one name; the blanks and `. , : -` cut off both ends of a name; a name over 60 characters, a placeholder, a name with the word PIID or a run of five digits, or one whose key has under 4 or over 40 characters, is dropped). A use case counts once for each distinct key. The id is the compact name of the short name; the edition is not in it. On 2026-11-10: 377 records from the 3,611 use cases of 41 agencies (804 pairs of a use case and a vendor), at most 110 use cases for one, 0 unreadable. A record keeps the use-case count, the agencies (6 shown), counts by stage (deployed or piloted, before deployment, retired, with no stage) and the high-impact count by the agency's own label. Text: `US federal AI use case inventory (github.com/ombegov), a register or status list.`, then `A record is US federal agencies' own report, compiled by the Office of Management and Budget, that a vendor was involved in developing or maintaining AI use cases they list; it says nothing about the vendor's conduct or the systems' quality.` and `Source: US Office of Management and Budget, 2025 Federal Agency AI Use Case Inventory; entries adapted by VerifyWise.` At most 1,300 characters (median 567, maximum 684 on 2026-11-10), the closing sentence never cut. Address: `https://github.com/ombegov/2025-Federal-Agency-AI-Use-Case-Inventory?vendor=<id>`. |
| Left out and text safety | **Never read:** `contact_email` (2,496 cells, personal addresses of federal staff); the agencies' and contractors' free text (`use_case_name`, `problem_solved`, `benefits` and others); the URL columns; `agency_bureau`; `id`; `operational_date`; the other `hi_*` and flag columns. Four columns are read by name (`agency`, `vendor_name`, `development_stage`, `is_high_impact`) and the rest is dropped before a row is built. No natural person was found among the vendor strings that were read (790 rows fill the cell, with 425 distinct texts on 2026-11-10), so no person rule is applied and none is kept apart; no host is a key, because the addresses in the cells are typed by agencies. **Design decision:** a text that was cut is never a key, an id or an identifier. A vendor cell longer than the kit reads (a megabyte: `lineOf` reads no further, so its first megabyte may be blank while a vendor stands after it) makes its row unreadable and adds nothing, not even its agency. Any other cell is cut at 400 characters, and a name that the cut ended is no name (the cut is told by its marker, never by the length of the line); a cut cell is read only up to its last real separator (a semicolon, a line break, a slash, `" and "`): its last name goes with everything that follows that separator, commas included, because a comma before a legal form joins two parts of one name and the cut can fall right after that comma or inside the legal form; what the cut left of an address (`https:` and at most two slashes at the end of the text) is taken off first, so that its slash is no separator; a cut cell with no real separator names no vendor (no real cell is that long: the longest has 138 characters). The identifier `vendor` is a name of at most 60 characters, under the 80 of the display, so it is never a cut text. Cells are cleaned (`lineOf`) and the record is written with `<` and `>` written as the angle quotation marks U+2039 and U+203A in the title, the summary and the identifiers, and the phrase "lookup note" written "lookup-note" in the title and the summary (`kit/record.ts`). |
| Match | Names only, weak: the key's distinct spellings (5 at most), as written and without the legal form, of 4 compact characters or more; no `domain:` key. A maker and its product do not merge. **A vendor that really is named gets Low and the name-only note** (D5.2). |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q41).** Its completeness is proved against two outside figures: the repository's blob hash and size, and OMB's README totals. An entry says that agencies report a vendor as involved in developing or maintaining AI use cases they list, never anything about the vendor's conduct; only 21.9 % of use cases name a vendor, so absence says nothing. |

### D2.33 Colombia SIC consumer protection sanctions

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | Colombia's Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio (SIC), on the national open data portal datos.gov.co ([dataset `73dx-n59j`](https://www.datos.gov.co/d/73dx-n59j)). The dataset lists the SIC's consumer protection sanctions: the fined party, the fine in Colombian pesos (COP), the conduct, the sector and the resolution with its day. |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor, by name: a company that the SIC fined (kind: an enforcement or case list). Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An **event** list: the 36-month lookback applies to the newest sanction. Raises a risk, at most one for each entry ("An entity named … appears in …", Possible at most); at most 2 records for a run; researched for every vendor. |
| Endpoint, format | Two keyless GETs on the Socrata resource API, JSON: `https://www.datos.gov.co/resource/73dx-n59j.json?$select=count(*)` and `https://www.datos.gov.co/resource/73dx-n59j.json?$select=:*,*&$order=:id&$limit=50000` (every row, ordered by `:id`; the bare address answers a window of 1,000 rows and is never used). Observed on 2026-11-10: `count(*)` 3,938; sanctions dated 2013-01-31 to 2026-05-29; `Last-Modified` 2026-07-30 21:03:09 GMT. |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `www.datos.gov.co` under `/resource/`. The count: at most 64 KiB, 60 s; the list: at most 8 MiB, 120 s; both `application/json`. The portal's robots.txt asks for a Crawl-delay of 1 s, kept with a pause of 1,000 ms between the two requests. The rows must be exactly as many as the count: a dataset replaced between the requests, or a cut answer, fails the sync and copies nothing; fewer than 3,000 rows, 50,000 or more, or an earliest sanction after 2014-12-31 is not the list. **Unproven:** the request with all columns and `$limit=50000` was proven for `:id` only; a first live sync that reads fewer rows than the count fails. |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Wednesdays at 03:04 UTC (`4 3 * * 3`), stale after 192 hours. Both requests are made in full every run (the portal's `ETag` belongs to one query), and `unchanged` comes from the body hash. The copy's version is the day the portal updated the dataset (2026-07-30 on 2026-11-10: the SIC publishes with a lag). A company that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q47).** CC BY-SA 4.0, as datos.gov.co states for the dataset; the portal's terms ask for a citation with the update day and forbid distorting the meaning, and the adapted records are shared under CC BY-SA 4.0 wherever they are shown. Attribution: `Fuente: Portal de Datos Abiertos www.datos.gov.co (Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio, consumer protection sanctions), CC BY-SA 4.0. Adapted by VerifyWise; the adapted records are shared under CC BY-SA 4.0.` |
| Record | One per sanctioned company, not one per sanction (`consumer_sanction_summary`, an **event**, dated by the newest sanction day): rows are grouped by the compact name of the party without its legal form and the tails ` EN LIQUIDACION...` and ` CON SIGLA ...`. On 2026-11-10: 2,218 companies from 3,938 rows (2,870 of the rows are kept, see Left out), at most 35 sanctions for one. The id is that compact name, made from the party after its persons are out, so it is never a person's name. The title and the text show at most 120 characters of the party, cut and marked; the body and the identifiers hold it whole. The text is `Colombia SIC consumer protection sanctions (datos.gov.co), an enforcement or case list.` `Sanctioned party: <name>.`, a sentence with the number of sanctions, the years and the fines in total in COP, `Newest:` up to 3 sanctions (day, resolution, conduct and sector as the SIC writes them in Spanish, fine) and the closing sentence `A sanction is listed when final and may later be annulled by a court; the entry names a Colombian party and may be another company of the same name. Fuente: Portal de Datos Abiertos www.datos.gov.co, data updated <day>; CC BY-SA 4.0, adapted by VerifyWise.`, which no cut reaches. The address is `https://www.datos.gov.co/d/73dx-n59j?party=<id>`. |
| Left out and text safety | **Persons** are judged in `read`, before anything is stored, and a row dropped for a person is not counted as unreadable. A cell is judged as a whole first: a party of two to six words in Spanish letters, at least two of them not particles (de, del, la, ...), with no organization or legal form word, digit, `&` or `/` is a person's (`looksLikePersonEs`; on samples seeded on 2026-11-10, 30 of 30 flagged names were persons and none of 30 unflagged). It is then judged side by side, because a cell can name several parties joined by ` Y/O `, `/`, a hyphen or a dash with a blank on at least one side (the en dash, U+2013, and the em dash, U+2014, count), ` Y `, ` E `, ` & ` or ` + ` (a blank each side, any case). Each side is judged without its tails (` EN LIQUIDACION...`, ` CON SIGLA ...`); a side with the shape of a person's name is removed with its tails, and the sides that remain are joined by ` Y/O ` (a cell with no person is kept byte for byte, keys included). The rule is asked of the text with typographic apostrophes (U+2018, U+2019) made ASCII and with a trailing parenthesis or identity or tax number (`C.C.`, `CC`, `NIT` and its number) taken off. When sides were removed, the row is made only if a side remains that a vendor could be matched by (4 letters and digits once its legal form is off, a word that is no legal form however it is written, no organization word, no particle and no family-firm word such as HIJOS): a person beside `HERMANOS`, `LTDA`, `CIA S EN C` or `HIJOS` alone leaves no company, and the row is dropped. Counted offline on 2026-11-10 with a copy of the rule that gives the same 2,218 ids as the build: 1,068 of the 3,938 rows (27.1 percent) are not kept (1,030 whole persons, 36 with no company left, 2 of persons only), and 67 of the 2,870 rows that are kept lose a person's side. **Residuals:** a hyphen or a dash with no blank on either side, a `+` with no blank, parentheses and a comma join nothing, so a person behind one stays in the party (a compound surname holds a hyphen); a name with a letter that Spanish does not use, a name of more than six words (particles count), the compound surname of a person that holds a ` Y ` (the cell is split there) and a person's name that holds an organization word are not read as persons, and a side of one word is no person; the rule reads the ASCII hyphen and apostrophe only, so U+2010, U+2011, U+2212, the middle dot U+00B7 or an apostrophe such as U+00B4, U+02BC or the backtick defeats it (U+2018 and U+2019 are folded first, and the en and em dashes split a cell when a blank is beside them); the list holds none of U+2010, U+2011, U+2212, U+2013, U+2018, U+2019 or U+00B7, and 4 acute accents and 1 backtick, on 2026-11-10. A number label other than `C.C.`, `CC` or `NIT` (`CEDULA`, or `N` with a degree sign before the number) and a bracket that is square, nested, never closed or placed before the name are not taken off, so a person with such a tail is not read as a person. The family-firm words are HIJOS, HIJAS, SOBRINOS, SUCESORES and FAMILIA; HEREDEROS and VIUDA are not among them (no case on 2026-11-10). A person beside generic organization words only (as `HOTEL`, `COLOMBIA` or `NACIONAL`) leaves no company and the row is lost: accepted, recall is given up for privacy. A company of two plain words that ends in a parenthesis reads as a person's once the parenthesis is off and its row is lost (no cell of the list is one), and a side of two words with no legal form and no organization word (a trade name, say) is taken for a person's and is not a name of the record, while the company beside it is. A party of more than 300 characters, whole or once its persons are out, is a row that cannot be read (the longest on 2026-11-10 has 227), never a cut name. **Never copied:** `:id`, `:created_at`, `:version`; `:updated_at` only as the update day of the citation. The conduct and the sector are free text: one cleaned line each, cut and marked at 240 and 80 characters (the text shows 70 and 40). |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak (no NIT, website or address in the data): the party without the tails, the whole party, the SIGLA and each side of ` Y/O `, `/` or a hyphen or a dash with a blank on at least one side (the en dash and the em dash count) that has a Spanish legal form, each with 4 or more compact characters; at most 6. A person's side is out of the party before any key is made, so it is never a key. **Design decision:** a cell of the shape `<company> <legal form> - EN LIQUIDACION` has its company as a side of its own, so its short name is a key too, as it is for a company written without the hyphen (29 records on 2026-11-10; the ids stay). Multi-party cells and SIGLA aliases widen a match. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q47).** An entry says that the SIC lists consumer protection sanctions against a party of that name, never that the vendor broke a rule: a sanction may be annulled and a name can belong to another company. The yield for software vendors is low (mostly Colombian retail and tourism). |

### D2.34 UK Algorithmic Transparency Recording Standard records

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Cabinet Office, on GOV.UK, for the UK public bodies that publish the records ([page](https://www.gov.uk/algorithmic-transparency-records)). A record of the Algorithmic Transparency Recording Standard (ATRS) is a public body's own description of an algorithmic tool that it uses; one section of its text names the external supplier, as free text. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: the supplier named in a record (kind: a register or status list). Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). A **state**: no lookback, offered as of the copy. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack (`algorithm_register_supplier`). Researched only for a vendor with a website; at most 1 record for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | One request to the GOV.UK Search API, keyless, JSON: `https://www.gov.uk/api/search.json?filter_content_store_document_type=algorithmic_transparency_record&count=1000&start=0&order=-public_timestamp` and a `fields[]` list of 16 fields (title, link, the record's own fields and `indexable_content`, which holds the text). Observed on 2026-11-10: 155 results, `total` 155 (three requests agree), 155 distinct links, 2,997,079 bytes, no `ETag` or `Last-Modified`. Without `order=` the API orders by popularity. |
| Auth, limits | None. Through the shared GOV.UK pager (`govukSearch.ts`): pinned to `www.gov.uk` under `/api/`, JSON only, 32 MiB and 60 s for the page, one page at most (a search that says more than 1,000 results is not the list), no validator (the API sends none). The rows are read only when the answer is one consistent search: `total` equals the results and the distinct links. More than 5,000 supplier entities is not the list either; a result with no text or no link is unreadable and left out, and so is one that names a supplier but whose link is not a page under `/algorithmic-transparency-records/` (200 characters at most); more than the allowance of the kit is refused (`parse_errors`). |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Mondays at 05:14 UTC (`14 5 * * 1`), stale after 192 hours. The API sends no validator, so about 3 MB are downloaded every week and `unchanged` comes from the body hash; the version is the newest `public_timestamp` day among the records that name a supplier. A supplier that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q51), with no gate.** The Open Government Licence v3.0: GOV.UK states that all its content is available under it except where otherwise stated, and no record states otherwise; the 13 of 155 records of bodies that are not Crown bodies (local authorities) rely on that statement. Contact e-mail addresses, named officials, linked documents and logos are excluded. Attribution: `UK Algorithmic Transparency Recording Standard records, published on GOV.UK. Contains public sector information licensed under the Open Government Licence v3.0. Adapted by VerifyWise (supplier names extracted and records counted); no endorsement by the Cabinet Office or the publishing bodies is implied.` |
| Record | One per supplier entity, not one per record: 125 entities from the 155 records on 2026-11-10 (82 of the records name a supplier that the rule accepts; 43 of the 125 entities carry a Companies House number). `algorithm_register_supplier`, a **state**, dated by the newest publication day of its records. The id is `atrs:<compact name of the supplier without its legal form>` (`atrs:acmewebservices`): 'Acme' and 'Acme Web Services' are two records.
