The text is `UK Algorithmic Transparency Records (gov.uk), a register or status list.` `A record is a UK public body's own description of an algorithmic tool it uses and names who supplied it; it says nothing about the supplier's conduct or about the tool's quality.`, the supplier as written, the records that name it with their phases, the newest 3 (title, body, phase, day), the Companies House number when given, and `Adapted by VerifyWise from GOV.UK under the Open Government Licence v3.0.` The address is `https://www.gov.uk` plus the newest record's link, so entities that share their newest record share an address (125 entities and 72 distinct addresses on 2026-11-10); the evidence ledger of one run keeps one record for an address, which is accepted. |
| Left out and text safety | Only two sections of the text are read (the supplier section, titled External supplier, Third party or Supplier, and the Companies House number), to 200,000 characters of text, 1,000 of a section and 1,001 of a line; the rest of `indexable_content` is read past and never reaches the database (contact e-mail addresses in 142 of 155 records on 2026-11-10, telephone numbers, URLs, named officials). A name is accepted when it has 2 to 80 characters and at most 7 words, starts with a capital letter or a digit, is no postcode and holds no colon, double quote or prose word (is, provided, in-house, ...). A country of the United Kingdom, the deploying body itself and a name with an `@`, `<`, `>`, half a bracket or no letter are no supplier, and the last name of a section that a limit cut is not kept. A heading whose title is over 300 characters, or whose line a limit cut, is no title of either section. A name is stored as one clean line of at most 120 characters: one that is longer once folded gives no row and is never cut. A record laid out without numbered headings (version 1.1 of the standard) gives no row and is not counted as unreadable. Measured on 2026-11-10: 29 of 30 accepted tokens were supplier names. No person rule is applied: a supplier that a record names by a person's name (a sole trader) is kept as written; none of the 30 accepted tokens that were read on 2026-11-10 is a person. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak: each distinct accepted spelling of the group (at most 10), as written and without the legal form, 4 compact characters or more. A `regno:` key comes from a Companies House number only when the record names exactly one supplier and one number (a record with several numbers gives none: 21 of the records with an accepted supplier on 2026-11-10), and is strong only beside a shared name. No `domain:` or `lei:` key. |
| Verdict | **Built weak, open (Q51).** An entry says that a UK public body named a supplier of that name for an algorithmic tool, never anything about the supplier's conduct or the tool: it is context, not a risk. The supplier is free text, so a name is only as good as the accept rule. |

### D2.35 NHTSA vehicle and equipment recalls

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The US National Highway Traffic Safety Administration ([datasets](https://www.nhtsa.gov/nhtsa-datasets-and-apis)). A recall campaign is a manufacturer's report of a safety defect or noncompliance in vehicles or equipment (a Part 573 report). The flat file holds only the campaigns reported since 2010-01-01, one or more rows for each. |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor, by name: the filer of campaigns (kind: a product safety list). The file covers every vehicle and equipment defect, not AI; the AI signal is the count of campaigns that name a driver assistance or automated driving component. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An **event** list (36-month lookback). Raises a risk, at most one for each entry ("An entity named … appears in …", Possible at most); at most 2 records for a run; researched for every vendor. |
| Endpoint, format | One static zip, keyless: `https://static.nhtsa.gov/odi/ffdd/rcl/FLAT_RCL_POST_2010.zip`, holding one entry `FLAT_RCL_POST_2010.txt` (TAB-delimited, 29 fields, no header row, CRLF on every line, dates `YYYYMMDD`). Observed on 2026-11-10: the zip is 14,995,295 bytes and the entry inflates to 312,829,632 bytes; 247,176 rows, 15,382 campaigns, earliest report day 2010-01-01; an `ETag` (the file's MD5) and `Last-Modified` 2026-11-09 08:03:40 GMT, so the object is replaced as a whole about once a day (one day observed). |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `static.nhtsa.gov` under `/odi/ffdd/rcl/`, a 304 included. One conditional GET reads the zip into memory (at most 64 MiB, 120 s) and its directory is checked (one deflated entry of that name, no Zip64). The **entry is streamed and never held in memory**: inflated in pieces of 64 KiB under a cap of 536,870,912 bytes (512 MiB), split into lines as it comes (1,000,000 at most) and reduced to one small state for each campaign and then each filer (120,000 campaigns and 8,000 filers at most: a file past a cap fails the sync as too large, never as a shorter list); its size and CRC-32 are checked before anything is returned, and `read` gets only the reduced JSON. Peak measured on 2026-11-10: about 100 MB resident, against about 0.7 GB holding the entry. Fewer than 10,000 campaigns or 1,000 filers, an earliest report day outside 2010 or under 95 percent of well-shaped rows is not the list. The reduced JSON that `read` gets is at most 16 MiB (a larger one is not the list). A line is unreadable, left out and counted, when its width is not 29, its row id is no number, its campaign number does not have the form of one (two digits, a type letter E, V, T or C, six characters), its type is not that letter, its report day is no day of the years 1900 to 2099, the filer's name does not fit in 80 characters once cleaned (it is never cut into an id or a key), a chosen field is not UTF-8, or the line is over 131,072 bytes. The allowance of the kit for those lines is measured over the lines of the file, not over the filers: a hundredth of them (2,471 of the 247,176 on 2026-11-10, where the filers alone would allow 16): more than that, up to one line in twenty, fails the sync as `parse_errors`, and more than one in twenty makes the file no list. A record that the kit refuses is measured apart, over the filers and the unreadable lines together, so the unreadable lines widen it by a hundredth of themselves at most; the sync also refuses an empty list and a shrink of more than 20 percent. On 2026-11-10 no line was unreadable. |
| Cadence, increments | Daily at 09:03 UTC (`3 9 * * *`), stale after 48 hours. The object was replaced at about 08:03 UTC on the one day observed, an hour before the run; the `ETag` is the validator, and the same file is `unchanged`. A filer that a later file lacks becomes `withdrawn`; a renamed filer is a new record. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q36).** In the way of Q31. NHTSA publishes no licence for the file: it is a work of the US government (17 U.S.C. §105), and DOT's data catalog tags NHTSA's recalls data public domain. The manufacturer-supplied fields 25 to 27 and the Part 573 texts are not used. Attribution: `Source: US National Highway Traffic Safety Administration, recalls flat file. Not endorsed by NHTSA. Entries adapted by VerifyWise.` |
| Record | One per filer (`MFGNAME`), not one per campaign: rows collapse into campaigns (`CAMPNO`) and campaigns into the filer, by the compact name without the legal form. On 2026-11-10: 1,616 filers from 1,947 spellings, at most 809 campaigns for one. `vehicle_recall_summary`, an **event**, dated by the newest report day; the id is the compact name without the legal form. A filer has a record when one of its names, as written or without its legal form, has 4 or more compact characters (all 1,616 have one on 2026-11-10); 11 of them have an id of fewer than 4 characters and are keyed by their full name (as `Abc, Inc.` is by `abcinc`). A short stem can merge two firms that share it (`Abc, Inc.` and `Abc Corp` are one record with the keys `abcinc` and `abccorp`), as longer stems with legal-form variants do by design: no unrelated pair does on 2026-11-10, and the property is accepted. The text is `NHTSA vehicle and equipment recalls (nhtsa.gov), a product safety list.` `Filer: <name>.`, the campaigns with the first and last year (how many NHTSA influenced, name a driver assistance component or carry a do-not-drive advisory), `By report year:` for the four years that end at the newest day of the file, `Newest:` 3 campaigns and the closing sentence `A recall campaign is the filer's report of a safety defect or noncompliance, not a finding of fault; the list holds an entry for the filer. Not endorsed by NHTSA; counts and component names adapted by VerifyWise from NHTSA's flat file.`, which no cut reaches. The address is `https://www.nhtsa.gov/nhtsa-datasets-and-apis?dataset=recalls&filer=<id>` (a query on a page that answers 403 to automated clients; not checked in a browser). |
| Left out and text safety | **Never decoded:** the Part 573 texts (fields 20 to 23: phone numbers, e-mail addresses in 1,884 rows, URLs, HTML tags), the manufacturer-supplied fields 25 to 27 and every field that is not needed (only fields 1, 2, 7, 8, 11, 12, 14, 16 and 28 are read). Each decoded cell is one cleaned line (controls and hidden characters removed), capped. The component of a campaign (the first by text among its rows) is shown, not matched: it is cut and marked at 112 characters, with angle brackets replaced, the text shows 40 of it, and it reaches no id, name or key. `MFGNAME` is cut by NHTSA at 40 characters (26 names), so a cut name matches nothing else. A name that does not fit in 80 characters once cleaned makes its line unreadable and is never cut. A line whose name is empty, has no letter or digit, is a placeholder (`unknown manufacturer`, `generic manufacturer`, `various manufacturers`) or holds 'redundant' (any case) names no entity: it is counted apart from the unreadable lines (none on 2026-11-10). No person filter: all 1,947 names were read and none is a natural person. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak: the filer's distinct spellings (at most 5) and, for a spelling with one parenthesis pair (`Zorbex (Quillon US, LLC)`), the text before and inside it (a parenthesis that is never closed runs to the end), without the legal form, 4 compact characters or more, at most 50. Never a make (`MAKETXT`) or `MFGTXT`; placeholder names (`unknown manufacturer`, `generic manufacturer`, `various manufacturers`, any name holding 'redundant') name no entity. No `domain:`, `lei:` or `regno:`. The filer can differ from the maker of the product (391 campaigns). |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q36).** An entry says that a filer of that name reported recall campaigns to NHTSA, never that a defect was found or that the vendor's AI failed. The file name fixes the window: `read` fails for a file whose earliest report day is not in 2010. |

### D2.36 Foundation Model Transparency Index

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Stanford Center for Research on Foundation Models (CRFM) ([repository](https://github.com/stanford-crfm/fmti)). The Foundation Model Transparency Index scores how much the developers of foundation models disclose; the scores file of the December 2025 edition marks each of 100 indicators 0 or 1 for each of 13 developers. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: a developer of foundation models (kind: a register or status list). Class TRUSTED_PRESS, strength weak (D5.2): a university center's index, not a regulator's record. A **state** of the December 2025 edition: no lookback. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack (`transparency_index_score`). Researched only for a vendor with a website; at most 1 record for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/stanford-crfm/fmti/main/Dec2025/Dec2025_scores.csv`, keyless, a CSV with the indicator names in its first column (header cell `Indicator`) and one column for each developer. Observed on 2026-11-10: 5,296 bytes decoded, `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, a weak `ETag`, no `Last-Modified`; 13 developer columns by 100 indicators, every cell 0 or 1. The edition path is a table in code whose only entry is `December 2025`: a later edition is invisible until a release adds it, since the text names the edition. |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `raw.githubusercontent.com` under `/stanford-crfm/fmti/`; 1 MiB (the file is 5 KB), 60 s, `text/plain`; one conditional GET on the weak `ETag`, no pause. Not the list (`wrong_root`): a first header cell other than `Indicator`, a header of more than 200 cells, fewer than 5 developer columns, fewer than 50 readable indicator rows, no developer with a key, or an indicator name that occurs twice. More than 200 indicator rows, a row of more than 200 cells below the header or a quote that is never closed is too large to read whole: the sync fails as too large, never as a shorter list. A row is unreadable and left out whole when it lacks the width of the header or an indicator name (200 characters at most) or holds a cell other than exactly `0` or `1`; a developer column is unreadable when its header is over 40 characters or its key is shared (both are left out). For 11 records the allowance of the kit is 2 (a tenth, rounded up; the rows and the columns that are left out count together), and more is refused (`parse_errors`): an indicator row that is left out changes every developer's count. `main` is a mutable branch: an edit changes the answer, and a moved path is a 404, which fails the sync and withdraws nothing. |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Fridays at 21:45 UTC (`45 21 * * 5`), stale after 192 hours. The file changes with an edition, not with a week; the copy's version is `Dec2025`. A developer column that a later edition removes is withdrawn; with 11 records the shrink check (20 percent) stops a loss of 3 or more at once. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q44).** CC BY 4.0, from the repository's `LICENSE.md` (the README says CC-BY); the December 2025 scores file only. The attribution credits the index's authors, as the licence asks: `Foundation Model Transparency Index (Bommasani, Klyman et al.), Stanford Center for Research on Foundation Models, CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Indicators counted by VerifyWise from the published scores file; not endorsed by Stanford CRFM.` |
| Record | One per developer column with a name key: 11 of the 13 on 2026-11-10 (two headers have 3 characters and give no key: such a column is counted as no entity, neither a record nor unreadable). `transparency_index_score`, a **state**, with no date. The id is the compact name of the header (`acme`, `zorbex21labs`), the same in every edition. The text is `Foundation Model Transparency Index (github.com/stanford-crfm), a register or status list.` `A record shows how much a developer of foundation models disclosed, as the index's team scored it; it measures transparency, not safety, quality or compliance.`, the developer as written (40 characters at most), `<edition> edition: the published scores file marks <n> of its <m> indicators as met for this developer (counted by VerifyWise from the file; the index team's own published scores are the only validated ones).` and `Source: Foundation Model Transparency Index, Stanford CRFM (Bommasani, Klyman et al.), CC BY 4.0; adapted by VerifyWise.` The address is `https://github.com/stanford-crfm/fmti?developer=<id>`, a query on the repository: the list is one file, so a record has no page, and no two records share an address. |
| Left out and text safety | The 100 indicator names (CC BY text that the record does not need) are read to check the rows and never kept; no indicator is re-scored, and the transparency reports, which the companies partly write, are never fetched. A header is one cleaned line of at most 40 characters, and a cut one is unreadable. No personal data: all 13 headers are organizations and no person is a developer of the index, so there is no person rule. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak: the header as written and without a legal form, whose compact name has 4 letters and digits or more. Headers are brand names ('Acme', 'Zorbex'), so a vendor stored as 'Acme Platforms, Inc.' does not match: the limit of exact keys. No `domain:` key. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q44).** A context-only register of one edition: it says how many of the index's indicators the published file marks as met for a developer, counted by VerifyWise from the file. It is never called the index's score, and it is no rating of safety or compliance and no finding. |

### D2.37 UK MHRA field safety notice lists

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency (MHRA), on GOV.UK ([page](https://www.gov.uk/drug-device-alerts)). A field safety notice is a medical device manufacturer's own corrective action. The MHRA publishes one list for each week. |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor, by name: the manufacturer in an entry's heading (kind: a product safety list). Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An **event** list (36-month lookback), dated by the newest listing. Raises a risk, at most one for each entry ("An entity named … appears in …", Possible at most); at most 1 record for a run; researched for every vendor. |
| Endpoint, format | The GOV.UK Search API, keyless, JSON: `https://www.gov.uk/api/search.json?filter_format=medical_safety_alert&filter_alert_type=field-safety-notices&count=1000&start=<start>&order=-public_timestamp&fields[]=title&fields[]=link&fields[]=issued_date&fields[]=public_timestamp&fields[]=indexable_content`. Observed on 2026-11-10: `total` 624 weekly lists in one page of 1,902,688 bytes, no `ETag` or `Last-Modified`; 8,485 reference lines in the text of the lists, 8,010 of them with a heading (the entries). A block of the text that begins with `MHRA reference` (any case) and goes on with a colon, a blank or nothing (never a letter: `MHRA references for this week` is prose) ends an entry; its heading is the first block since the previous such block whose first line has a colon within its first 80 characters, followed by a blank and a character, and that does not begin with the word of a model line (Model, Ref, Description, Batch, Serial, Lot, Catalogue, Part, Product code, Software): the manufacturer is before the colon, the product after it (the line that follows when the colon ends its line). |
| Auth, limits | None. Through the shared GOV.UK pager (`govukSearch.ts`): pinned to `www.gov.uk` under `/api/`, JSON only, 16 MiB and 60 s a page, pages of 1,000 results, at most 3 pages (a total that needs more is not the list), a pause of 400 ms before each page after the first. The pages are read only as one consistent search (the same `total`, each page where it was asked, as many distinct links as the total): a list published between two pages fails the sync instead of shifting the rows. More than 20,000 manufacturers or 50,000 notices is not the list (`wrong_root`). |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Thursdays at 22:22 UTC (`22 22 * * 4`), stale after 192 hours. No validator exists, so the 1.9 MB are downloaded every week and `unchanged` comes from the body hash. A notice that is listed again in a later week counts once; a manufacturer that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. The version of the copy is the newest day of its lists. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q52), with no gate.** The Open Government Licence v3.0, for the MHRA's weekly list pages on GOV.UK; the manufacturers' attached field safety notices (third-party documents) are never fetched, read or copied. Attribution: `UK Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency field safety notice lists, published on GOV.UK. Contains public sector information licensed under the Open Government Licence v3.0. Adapted by VerifyWise (entries counted by manufacturer); no endorsement by the MHRA is implied.` |
| Record | One per manufacturer, counting its distinct notices over all the lists: a notice is a manufacturer and a reference, once, however many lists name it, and its newest listing is kept. `field_safety_notice_summary`, an **event**; `publishedAt` is the newest list day. On 2026-11-10: 2,036 records from 7,643 distinct notices. The id is `mhra-fsn:` and the compact name of the manufacturer without the legal form ('Example Inc' and 'Example Ltd' are one, 'Example' and 'Example Medical' are two), 120 characters at most. The reference is the first slash form of digit groups (as `2026/010/009/601/195`), else the first number of 7 or 8 digits, else the whole text of the line without blanks, in lower case (a record shows its first 40 characters, with no mark of a cut; 15 of the 8,010 references have no known form on 2026-11-10). A reference line with no value takes the next block, but only when that block holds a reference of the slash form or a number and is neither a heading nor a reference line. A list's day is its `issued_date` unless the title names a year of the 2000s that the day's year is not (or one more): 7 of the 624 lists on 2026-11-10, whose notices count but are in no year, never the newest and show `date unclear`. The text is `UK MHRA field safety notice lists (gov.uk), a product safety list.` `The MHRA's weekly lists name the manufacturers that sent field safety notices about their medical devices; a notice is the manufacturer's own corrective action, not a finding of fault, and counts grow with the number of devices a maker sells.`, the manufacturer as written, the count with the first year and the counts of the 5 newest years of listing, the newest 3 notices (the product, `listed <day>`, `MHRA reference <reference>`) and `Adapted by VerifyWise from GOV.UK under the Open Government Licence v3.0; the notices themselves are not copied.` The address is `https://www.gov.uk` plus the link of the weekly list of the newest notice (a list that names several manufacturers is the page of each, so records can share an address). |
| Left out and text safety | Read from a list: `link`, `title` (for its years only), `issued_date` and `indexable_content` (a text of more than 100,000 characters is never cut: its list is unreadable; the longest real text has 6,352 on 2026-11-10). Not read: the model, batch, lot, serial and description lines of an entry, the linked documents (third-party text) and every other field of the result. Only the manufacturer (120 characters), the product (80), the reference (40 at most; the whole text of a reference of no known form, up to 300 characters, tells two notices apart before it is cut), the day and the path of the list reach the body. An entry gives no row, and is not counted, when it has no heading (an attached letter: 475 of the 8,485 reference lines on 2026-11-10), no manufacturer that can be kept (empty, over 120 characters once folded, ending in the mark of a cut, holding `<` or `>`, or with fewer than 4 letters and digits once its legal form is off), no product left, or no reference (none, over 300 characters or ending in the mark of a cut). A list with more than 1,000 reference lines, a text of more than 100,000 characters, a text that is not text, or a link that is not a page under `/drug-device-alerts/` (a slug, 300 characters at most, nothing hidden) is unreadable: it is counted (the allowance of the kit applies) and none of its entries is read. A product text such as 'Refer to FSN' is shown as listed. No person rule: the manufacturers are companies, and a rule of honorifics would only flag a company named after a person. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak: the distinct spellings of the heading's manufacturer in the group (at most 10, the most frequent first), without the legal form, 4 compact characters or more (a heading such as 'XQ' gives no row). The heading is free text: on 2026-11-10, 30 of 30 in a random sample were correct (a sample of the profile, not repeated for the final reader), and it is present in 94.4 percent of the 8,485 reference lines. No `regno:`, `domain:` or `lei:`. |
| Verdict | **Built weak, open (Q52).** An entry says that a manufacturer of that name sent a field safety notice that the MHRA listed: the maker's own corrective action, whose count follows the number of devices sold, and which says nothing about AI. Only medical device makers appear (Apple, Google, Microsoft and Amazon have no entry on 2026-11-10). |

### D2.38 Dutch Algorithm Register

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Dutch Ministry of the Interior and Kingdom Relations, which runs the Algoritmeregister of the Dutch government ([API documentation](https://algoritmes.overheid.nl/api-docs)); the publishing public bodies write the descriptions, which have, among other fields, a free-text provider. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: the provider named in a description (kind: a register or status list). Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). A **state**: no lookback. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack (`algorithm_register_supplier`). Researched only for a vendor with a website; at most 1 record for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://algoritmes.overheid.nl/api/downloads/NLD?filetype=csv`, keyless, a generated CSV of every description. Observed on 2026-11-10: `text/csv; charset=utf-8`, 6,716,963 bytes decoded (1,678,307 on the wire), 1,638 rows of 31 columns; no `ETag`, `Last-Modified` or `Cache-Control`. Seven columns are read, by name: provider, organization, `org_id`, status, publication category, publication date and `algorithm_id`. |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `algoritmes.overheid.nl` under `/api/downloads/`; 32 MiB, 180 s, `text/csv`. One request, no pause, and no conditional GET: the file is generated for each request. Not the list (`wrong_root`): a last byte that is not a line feed (a stream cut in a row has none), a header without one of the 7 columns, or fewer than 1,000 data rows. More than 20,000 rows, more than 5,000 providers, a row of more than 100 cells or a quote that is never closed is too large to read whole: the sync fails as too large, never as a shorter list. A row is unreadable when it is not as wide as the header, has a status or category outside the register's three values, an algorithm id that is not 8 digits or is held by another row too (both go), no publication day or no public body, an `org_id` of more than 20 characters, a public body's name of more than 80 (it is never cut) or a provider cell of more than 1,048,576 characters (refused before it is read); more than the allowance of the kit is refused (`parse_errors`). |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Wednesdays at 22:37 UTC (`37 22 * * 3`), stale after 192 hours. With no validator each run costs 1.7 MB on the wire and `unchanged` comes from the body hash; the version is the newest publication day. A provider that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`; one that renames itself is two records. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q42), with no gate.** A new precedent: the register names no licence, its copyright page says its content may be reused and forbids implying government endorsement, and the EUPL-1.2 covers only the register's code. Conditions: contact fields are never read, no attachment is used, a row whose provider cell holds the copyright sign or the word 'auteursrecht' is skipped (none of 1,638 on 2026-11-10), and each record names up to three publishing bodies and says that the Dutch government does not endorse it. Listed for the owner's confirmation; fallback: a gate is added (as for Q29) and the copy is deleted. Attribution: `Bron: Algoritmeregister van de Nederlandse overheid (algoritmes.overheid.nl); each record names up to three of the public bodies that published the descriptions it counts and links the register's search, which lists them all. Entries adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by the Dutch government.` |
| Record | One per provider key: 248 on 2026-11-10, from the 1,638 rows (none unreadable); a description counts once for each key it names. `algorithm_register_supplier`, a **state** (`publishedAt` only orders the records). The id is the compact name of the provider without the legal form (`acme`), so 'Acme B.V.' and 'Acme BV' are one. The text is `Dutch Algorithm Register (algoritmes.overheid.nl), a register or status list.` `A record is Dutch public bodies' own descriptions of algorithms they use that name this provider; it says nothing about the provider's conduct or the algorithms' quality, and the Dutch government does not endorse it.`, the provider as written, the count of descriptions and of distinct public bodies that name it with the counts by status (in use, in development, out of use) and the descriptions that the publishing body labels 'high-risk AI system', up to 3 bodies with the newest publication day (the text shows each body's name cut to 40 characters; the identifiers keep up to 30 whole names), and `Source: Algoritmeregister van de Nederlandse overheid; adapted by VerifyWise.` The address is `https://algoritmes.overheid.nl/nl/algoritme?searchtext=<display name, encoded>`; that the register's search takes this parameter was not verified (the fallback is the plain search page). |
| Left out and text safety | `contact_email` (1,638 cells) is never read, nor the free text of the public bodies (description, goal, legal basis, risks, methods) and the algorithm's name; the algorithm id is checked (8 digits, held by one row) and then only counted. A provider cell over 60 characters is a sentence and gives no name, even if it names a supplier: that loss is accepted. A cell that is kept as an id, a name or an identifier is never cut: a body's name of more than 80 characters, an `org_id` of more than 20 or a provider cell of more than 1,048,576 characters makes its row unreadable. **Persons:** a marker of a natural person is an academic title or an honorific, or a post-nominal after a name, and a piece that holds one is never a provider. A title is a word at the start of a name or after a blank, a colon, a semicolon, a comma or a bracket (round or square), in any case and whatever follows it: Prof, Dr, Drs, Ir, Mr, Dhr, Mevr and Mevrouw as whole words (with or without a full stop), and Ing and Bc with a full stop (a bare ING is the bank and a bare Bc is a word). A post-nominal (MSc, MBA, PhD, LLM and RA, in any case, with or without the full stops) after a name is a marker as a whole word or as the text of brackets; a name that begins with the letters of one (`MSC Zorbex`) is a company's. A title that follows the name it qualifies is the same person: brackets that begin with a title or hold only post-nominals drop the piece they belong to, and a piece that begins with a title (or holds only post-nominals) drops the piece before it when a comma separated the two. A title after initials or after more pieces makes a title piece (a piece that holds a marker and whose other words are initials or one capitalized word, with no legal form and no organization word), and the comma-separated pieces before it that are initials or one capitalized word go with it, back to a piece that holds a legal form or an organization word, has two capitalized words or a lower-case word, or was set apart by another separator. Another separator (a semicolon, a slash, the word en) separates two parties, so a company beside a titled person stays. The rule fails closed: a company whose name starts with a title word, is followed by a post-nominal or ends in LLM, MBA or RA is dropped as a person is, and so is a company before a comma and a title (`Acme B.V., dr. Voorbeeld`). **Residuals:** a title later in brackets, a title after other words of a piece that does not begin with one, a title glued to initials, a name with a particle or of two capitalized words before a title, a surname in lower case, an initial in lower case, a hyphenated initial, the abbreviation `mw.`, `t.a.v.` before a person, the post-nominals BSc, MA, MD, RC, RE and EMIA, and every person with no title at all. 4 provider cells (3 distinct texts) of the file hold a title marker on 2026-11-10. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak, from the provider cell (one over 60 characters is a sentence): web addresses and parentheticals (an unclosed one too) are cut; the cell is split on `;`, `,`, `/` and the word `en` (an ampersand and a hyphen are no separators: one name); a name is dropped when it starts with a marker of the body itself or of an empty field (is, intern, eigen, zelf, het, de, dit, deze, er, geen, niet, nvt, n.v.t, onbekend, gemeente, provincie; the short ones as whole words, so that 'Decozorb' stays), holds a marker of a natural person (see Left out), or has fewer than 4 compact characters (`Abc`, `XQZ`). No `domain:`: the public bodies type the URLs. On 2026-11-10 the 248 records carry 292 distinct keys. |
| Verdict | **Built weak, open (Q42), pending the owner's confirmation.** An entry says that Dutch public bodies named a provider of that name in descriptions of algorithms, never anything about the provider's conduct. The provider field is free text (137 of 1,277 filled cells on 2026-11-10 are sentences). |

### D2.39 NHTSA defect investigations

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | NHTSA's Office of Defects Investigation (ODI) ([datasets](https://www.nhtsa.gov/nhtsa-datasets-and-apis)), which inquires into possible safety defects in vehicles and equipment. The flat file has one line for each component and vehicle of an investigation (1 to 71,004 lines for one), for every investigation opened since 1972. |
| Category · level | `legal_regulatory` · vendor, by name: a manufacturer named in investigations (kind: a product safety list). The file covers every vehicle and equipment defect, not AI; the AI signal is the count of investigations that name a driver assistance or automated driving component. Class OFFICIAL, strength weak (D5.2). An **event** list (36-month lookback). Raises a risk, at most one for each entry ("An entity named … appears in …", Possible at most); at most 2 records for a run; researched for every vendor. |
| Endpoint, format | One static zip, keyless: `https://static.nhtsa.gov/odi/ffdd/inv/FLAT_INV.zip`, holding one entry `FLAT_INV.txt` (TAB-delimited, 11 fields, no header row, dates `YYYYMMDD`). Observed on 2026-11-10: the zip is 4,303,154 bytes and the entry inflates to 390,634,982 bytes; 154,460 lines, 5,357 investigations, the earliest opened on 1972-03-10; an `ETag` and `Last-Modified` 2026-11-09 09:03:46 GMT, so the object is replaced as a whole about once a day (one day observed). |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `static.nhtsa.gov` under `/odi/ffdd/inv/`, a 304 included. Read as the recalls file (D2.35), the **entry streamed and never held in memory**: the zip in memory (at most 32 MiB, 120 s), the entry inflated under a cap of 629,145,600 bytes (600 MiB) and 1,000,000 lines and reduced to one small state for each investigation and each manufacturer (capped: a file past a cap fails the sync as too large, never as a shorter list). Peak measured on 2026-11-10: about 85 MB resident, against about 0.83 GB holding the entry. Not the list: fewer than 4,000 investigations or 400 manufacturers with a record, or more than one row in twenty of the wrong shape. The reducer keeps at most 50,000 investigations, 2,000 manufacturers and 200,000 pairs of an investigation with a component or a manufacturer; the reduced JSON that `read` gets is at most 16 MiB. A line is unreadable, left out and counted, when its width is not 11, its id does not have the form of an investigation id (one or two capital letters, two digits, an optional capital letter and two or three digits), an opening or closing day is present and is no day of the years 1900 to 2099, the manufacturer's name does not fit in 80 characters once cleaned (it is never cut into an id or a key), a chosen field is not UTF-8, or the line is over 131,072 bytes. The allowance of the kit for those lines is measured over the lines of the file: a hundredth of them (1,544 of the 154,460 on 2026-11-10, where the manufacturers alone would allow 10): more than that, up to one line in twenty, fails the sync as `parse_errors`, and more than one in twenty makes the file no list. A record that the kit refuses is measured apart, over the manufacturers and the unreadable lines together, so the unreadable lines widen it by a hundredth of themselves at most (21 of the 583 manufacturers at most on 2026-11-10); the sync also refuses an empty list and a shrink of more than 20 percent. On 2026-11-10 no line was unreadable. |
| Cadence, increments | Daily at 10:01 UTC (`1 10 * * *`), stale after 48 hours. The object was replaced at about 09:03 UTC on the one day observed, an hour before the run; the `ETag` is the validator. A manufacturer that a later file lacks becomes `withdrawn`; a renamed one is a new record. The copy's version is the newest day of the file. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q35).** In the way of Q31. NHTSA publishes no licence for the file: it is a work of the US government (17 U.S.C. §105), and DOT's data catalog tags NHTSA's investigations data public domain. The summaries, which name petitioners, are never stored. Attribution: `Source: US National Highway Traffic Safety Administration, Office of Defects Investigation (defect investigations flat file). Not endorsed by NHTSA. Entries adapted by VerifyWise.` |
| Record | One per manufacturer (`MFR_NAME`), not one per investigation: the lines of an investigation collapse into one investigation, which counts once for each distinct manufacturer it names. On 2026-11-10: 587 manufacturers from 595 names, 583 of them with a record (a manufacturer has one when one of its names, as written or without its legal form, has 4 or more compact characters: 4 have none), at most 708 investigations for one; 12 ids have fewer than 4 characters, 8 of them keyed by the full name; a short stem can merge two firms that share it, as in D2.35 (no unrelated pair does on 2026-11-10; accepted). `defect_investigations`, an **event**, dated by the newest activity day; the id is the compact name without the legal form. The text is `NHTSA defect investigations (nhtsa.gov), a product safety list.` `Manufacturer: <name>.`, the investigations with the first and last year opened (how many are linked to a recall campaign, opened in the last seven calendar years with no close date recorded, or name a driver assistance component), `By year opened:` for the four years that end at the newest day of the file, `Newest:` 3 investigations (action number, opening and closing day, component, subject) and the closing sentence `An investigation is not a finding that a defect exists; the list holds an entry for the manufacturer. Not endorsed by NHTSA; adapted by VerifyWise from NHTSA's flat file.`, which no cut reaches. An empty close date is a missing date, not proof that an investigation is open: the text says 'no close date recorded' and never 'open' or 'ongoing'. The address is `https://www.nhtsa.gov/nhtsa-datasets-and-apis?dataset=investigations&manufacturer=<id>` (the page and the caveat of D2.35). |
| Left out and text safety | **Never decoded:** `SUMMARY` (field 11), NHTSA's detail text, which names petitioners and complainants (honorifics and names in 1,063 rows) and holds phone-like numbers and URLs: the reader of a line finds its tabs to check its width, and never decodes field 11. Only 7 of the 11 fields are decoded (id, component, manufacturer, the two days, the campaign number as a yes or no, subject). The component (the one that most rows of an investigation name) and the subject are shown, not matched: they are cut and marked at 112 and 135 characters, with angle brackets replaced, the text shows 40 and 80 of them, and they reach no id, name or key; two components that share their first characters count as one when the one to show is chosen (a display count, accepted). A line that names no manufacturer (an empty cell, a placeholder such as `unknown manufacturer`, or any name holding 'redundant') names no entity and is counted apart from the unreadable rows (1,401 of the 154,460 lines on 2026-11-10: 1,358 empty cells and 43 lines of placeholder names). `MFR_NAME` is cut by NHTSA at 40 characters (3 names). A name that does not fit in 80 characters once cleaned makes its line unreadable and is never cut. No person filter: all 595 names were read and none is a natural person. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak, as D2.35: the manufacturer's distinct spellings (at most 5) and the text before and inside a parenthesis (`Zorbex (American Zorbex Motor Co.)`), without the legal form, 4 compact characters or more, at most 50. Never `MAKE`, which a robotaxi operator can bear on a vehicle of another maker. No `domain:`, `lei:` or `regno:`. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q35).** An entry says that NHTSA's Office of Defects Investigation names a manufacturer of that name in investigations, never that a defect exists or that the vendor's AI failed. The category is `legal_regulatory`, not `ai_specific`, because the list covers every defect. |

### D2.40 FedRAMP Marketplace

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The US General Services Administration (GSA), which runs FedRAMP, the US government's authorization program for cloud services ([repository](https://github.com/FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data)). The Marketplace lists cloud offerings that are FedRAMP Authorized, Ready or In Process (and Agency In Process); the repository holds its file `data.json`. |
| Category · level | `certifications` · vendor, by name and by website host: the provider of a cloud offering (kind: a register or status list). Class OFFICIAL, strength weak by name and strong by a verified domain (D5.2). A **state**: no lookback. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack, even for a strong match (`cloud_authorization_status`). Researched only for a vendor with a website; at most 2 records for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data/main/data.json`, keyless, one minified JSON text served as `text/plain; charset=utf-8`. Observed on 2026-11-10: 4,383,933 bytes decoded (764,007 on the wire), a weak `ETag`, no `Last-Modified`. `data` holds `Metrics`, `Products` (656 products, 560 providers), `AtoMapping` and parts that are not read. |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `raw.githubusercontent.com` under `/FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data/`; 16 MiB, 120 s; `text/plain` or `application/json` (a check for `application/json` alone would fail every run); one conditional GET on the weak `ETag`, no pause. `read` fails as not the list when the file's own counts do not match its content (the `Metrics` totals and the `AtoMapping` ids against the products), with fewer than 400 products, or when the earliest authorization day is after 2014-12-31; more than 5,000 products or mappings fail as too large, before any other check. A product is unreadable, left out and counted, when its provider does not fit in 100 characters (it is never cut), its package id is not 3 to 20 letters and digits, it has no offering or no status of the four, or no day for its status; of two products that share an id the smaller text is kept and the other is counted as unreadable; more than the allowance of the kit is refused (`parse_errors`). |
| Cadence, increments | Daily at 19:06 UTC (`6 19 * * *`), stale after 48 hours. GSA uploads the file daily. `meta.last_change`, which moves with every upload, is kept out of the rows so that the body hash changes only when a status does; the copy's version is the newest status day. The repository labels the file 'Legacy FedRAMP certification data': if GSA stops updating it the sync still succeeds on a frozen file and the version shows as old (no alert is built). A provider that a later file lacks becomes `delisted`. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q48).** No published licence: the file is a work of GSA (17 U.S.C. §105), accepted for GSA's status fields only: the provider, the offering, the status and its date, the impact level, the authorization type, the agency count and the UEI; logos, provider descriptions and contact data are not copied. The host of an offering's website is a fact and a match key only, never shown or stored as text. Attribution: `Source: US General Services Administration, FedRAMP Marketplace data. Not endorsed by GSA or FedRAMP. Entries adapted by VerifyWise.` |
| Record | One per provider: products grouped by the compact name of `csp` without the legal form, so that two spellings that compact alike are one provider and two that do not stay two; 656 products and 560 providers on 2026-11-10, and 558 records. `cloud_authorization_status`, a **state** (`publishedAt` is the newest status day). The id is the compact name. A provider has a record when it has a name key (a spelling of 4 or more compact characters, as written or without its legal form: a short id such as `abc` is keyed by its full spelling `Abc, Inc.`) or a qualifying host; the 2 of the 560 that have neither make no record, which is no unreadable row and is not counted. The text is `FedRAMP Marketplace (github.com/FedRAMP), a register or status list.` `Provider: <name>.`, `GSA's FedRAMP Marketplace data lists <n> cloud offering(s) of it:` with the counts by status, `Listed:` up to 3 offerings (`"<offering>" (<package id>), <status> since <day>, <impact> impact, <type> authorisation, used by <n> agencies`; ranked by status, then newest day, then package id) and the closing sentence `A marketplace listing is an authorisation status, not a security assurance; the authorising agency's own authorisation may have expired. Not endorsed by GSA or FedRAMP; adapted by VerifyWise.`, which no cut reaches. The address is `https://github.com/FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data?provider=<id>`. |
| Left out and text safety | Never read: the logo, `service_desc` (provider-written text), `all_others`, `business_function`, `fedramp_msg`; the e-mail addresses of providers and agencies, the whole Assessors section (named contacts, e-mail, address), the reuse mapping and every other field. The `website` is never shown or stored: only a qualifying host becomes a `domain:` key. Cells are one cleaned line with no angle bracket: a provider of more than 100 characters is a product that cannot be read (it is never cut into a name, a key or an id), a `website` field of more than 300 characters has no host, and the offering (80 characters), the impact level and the authorization type (20 each) are for the text only and are cut and marked. The assessor, the service and deployment models and the initial authorizing agency are never carried. No person rule: the file names companies, and a rule would drop real products. |
| Match | `name:` of the provider's spellings (at most 5), weak. `domain:`, strong, from the host of an offering's website only when it is an apex domain (two labels, or three under a known country suffix; a country suffix itself such as `gov.uk` is no apex), is not a shared host, is claimed by exactly one provider in the file and its first label (3 characters or more) is related to the compact short name of one of the provider's spellings in one of three ways: it is that name, it contains it (a name of 4 characters or more) or it is the beginning of it (a label of 4 characters or more). A name of fewer than 4 characters inside another company's longer label, a label that is only a word in the middle or at the end of the name and a label of 3 characters that is not the whole name give no key (a few real provider domains are weak for that reason). A provider whose name begins with a generic word still gets the strong key of the host of that word when no other provider claims it (`Cloud Foo` and `cloud.gov`): accepted. At most 5 hosts, 3 UEIs and 10 package ids are kept. Measured on 2026-11-10: 424 hosts pass, on 424 of the 558 records (the rule is the strictest of the variants that keep at least 95 percent of the 441 hosts that the looser rule passed). `regno:` from a UEI of exactly 12 letters and digits, strong only beside a shared name. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q48).** An entry says that GSA's Marketplace data lists a cloud offering of that provider with a status: an authorization status, not a security assurance and not a current authorization to operate. A strong match does not lift a label, since the record is context only. |

### D2.41 Epoch AI Data on AI Models

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | Epoch AI, a research organization ([documentation](https://epoch.ai/data/ai-models-documentation)), whose database of AI models gives each model's developers, publication day and technical measures. The list is its file of all models, `all_ai_models.csv`. |
| Category · level | `ai_specific` · vendor, by name: an organization that Epoch labels 'Industry' among a model's developers (kind: a register or status list). Class TRUSTED_PRESS, strength weak (D5.2): a research organization's curated database, not an authority. A **state**: no lookback. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack (`ai_model_developer`). Researched only for a vendor with a website; at most 1 record for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://epoch.ai/data/all_ai_models.csv`, keyless, a CSV. Observed on 2026-11-10: `text/csv`, 6,934,395 bytes decoded (2,119,905 on the wire), a weak `ETag`, no `Last-Modified`; 3,708 rows of 57 columns, unique model names, newest first; the newest `Last modified` is 2026-11-09 21:03:11 UTC. Seven columns are read, by name: Model, Organization, Organization categorization, Publication date, Frontier model, Open model weights? and Last modified. The other subsets are not fetched. |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `epoch.ai` under `/data/`; 16 MiB, 120 s, `text/csv`; one conditional GET on the weak `ETag`, no pause. Not the list (`wrong_root`): fewer than 3,000 rows, a used column missing from the header (a new column passes), a repeated model name, more than 5,000 organizations, no row with an organization cell, or fewer than 90 percent of the rows with an organization cell having a label cell with as many items (a cell of more than 2,000 characters does not: see Match). Only a part of the list, so the sync fails as too large and never as a shorter list: more than 20,000 rows, a row of more than 128 cells, or a file that ends inside a quoted cell. Unreadable and left out: a row that is not as wide as the header or has no model name (or one over 200 characters, which is never cut), a row whose `Frontier model` or `Open model weights?` cell is over 1,048,576 characters (the first megabyte of a cell could say one thing and the cell another), and an 'Industry' item of more than 200 characters once its parentheticals are cut or a key of more than 160; more than the allowance of the kit is refused (`parse_errors`). |
| Cadence, increments | Weekly, Thursdays at 23:39 UTC (`39 23 * * 4`), stale after 192 hours. Epoch updates the file daily, but an organization's model count changes slowly. The file is newest first, so a cut body loses the oldest models and stays well-formed: the row floor and the shrink check (20 percent) guard it. The copy's version is the newest `Last modified` day. Each row carries its `Last modified`, so the shortcut of the body hash (`unchanged`) fires rarely and the weekly sync parses the 381 records again (an idempotent write). A record that a later copy lacks becomes `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q43).** Creative Commons Attribution: Epoch's data pages say the data are free to use, distribute and reproduce provided the source and authors are credited (version 4.0 on the companies page, none on the models documentation); every version allows commercial use and adaptation. Attribution: `Epoch AI, 'Data on AI models', published online at epoch.ai (https://epoch.ai/data/ai-models-documentation), under the Creative Commons Attribution licence. Counted and adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by Epoch AI.` |
| Record | One per organization key that Epoch labels 'Industry' (see Match): 381 records from 2,513 rows on 2026-11-10; a model counts once for each distinct key. `ai_model_developer`, a **state** (`publishedAt` only orders the records). The id is the compact name of the organization without the legal form (`acme`, `acmedeepresearch`). The title and the text show the first spelling cut at 80 characters; the identifiers and the names hold it whole (200 at most). Units of one company are separate keys. The record keeps the number of models, the first year and the newest day, the numbers marked frontier by Epoch (among the five largest by training compute at release; the cell is exactly 'True') and with open weights (exactly 'Yes'), and the newest 3 models (newest first, a model with no day after every model with one, ties by name; name cut and marked at 60 characters, day or 'undated'). The text is `Epoch AI Data on AI Models (epoch.ai), a register or status list.` `A record counts the AI models in Epoch AI's database that name this organisation among their developers; it describes model releases as Epoch classifies them and says nothing about their safety or the organisation's conduct.`, the organization as written, the counts, the newest models and `Source: Epoch AI, 'Data on AI models' (epoch.ai), Creative Commons Attribution; counted by VerifyWise, not endorsed by Epoch AI.` The address is `https://epoch.ai/data/ai-models-documentation?organisation=<id>`, a query on the documentation page. |
| Left out and text safety | Never read (counts of 2026-11-10): `Authors` (natural persons, 2,712 cells), the Hugging Face developer id (handles that may be personal), the abstract, reference, link and archived-link columns and every notes column (third-party and editor text, URLs, 16 e-mail addresses), the technical columns and the country. The cells that are read are one cleaned line: a model name of more than 200 characters makes its row unreadable and is never cut, and the text shows 60 characters of it, cut and marked. No person rule: none of 1,030 organization tokens was a natural person. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak: the `Organization` and `Organization categorization` cells are two lists whose items line up by position. A cell of more than 2,000 characters (UTF-16 units; the longest real one has 759 on 2026-11-10) is no list: the row does not line up and gives no key, and nothing of the cell is read, so that no name is ever the piece of one. Otherwise each cell is split alike on a comma that no legal form follows (optional blanks, then Inc, Ltd, LLC, Corp, GmbH, Co, SA, AG, PLC, Limited or Corporation, to the end of the word). A token is an organization only when the two lists have the same number of items and the label at its position is exactly 'Industry': a row whose label cell is empty or whose lists differ gives no key and does not count as lined up (95.3 percent of the rows with an organization line up on 2026-11-10; 171 rows do not: 144 whose lists differ and 27 with an empty label cell). Parentheticals are cut, an unclosed one to the end. A name of fewer than 4 letters and digits gives no key (`Abc`, `xQz`, `Z.q`, `A&Q`). A record carries its 5 most frequent spellings as names. No `domain:`. A vendor 'Acme' matches none of the units of Acme. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q43).** An entry says that Epoch's database lists AI models that name an organization of that name among their developers, with Epoch's own classification; it says nothing about their safety or the organization's conduct. |

### D2.42 C2PA Conforming Products List

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Coalition for Content Provenance and Authenticity (C2PA) ([repository](https://github.com/c2pa-org/conformance-public)), which runs the conformance program for products of its content provenance specification. The list is the program's own file: one entry for each listed product, with its applicant and its status. |
| Category · level | `certifications` · vendor, by name: the applicant of a conformant product (kind: a register or status list). Class TRUSTED_PRESS, strength weak (D5.2). A **state**: no lookback. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack (`c2pa_conformance`). Positive only. Researched only for a vendor with a website; at most 1 record for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/c2pa-org/conformance-public/main/conforming-products/conforming-products-list.json`, keyless, one JSON array with no paging, query or limit (it parses only when whole). Observed on 2026-11-10: 429,233 bytes decoded (31,283 on the wire), `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, a weak `ETag`, no `Last-Modified`; 322 products, 204 applicants, every status `conformant`; the oldest conformance day is 2025-06-27. |
| Auth, limits | None. Pinned to `raw.githubusercontent.com` under `/c2pa-org/conformance-public/`; 8 MiB (the file is 429 KB), 60 s, `text/plain` or `application/json`; one conditional GET on the weak `ETag`, no pause. Not the list (`wrong_root`): a root that is no array, fewer than 150 conformant products, or fewer than 100 applicants that have one (322 and 199 on 2026-11-10). A product is unreadable when it has no UUID as record id (a repeated id leaves out every copy, so the order of the file never decides which survives), no applicant of 1 to 100 characters with a letter or digit, no text status or no real conformance day; more than the kit's allowance of those is refused (`parse_errors`). More than 10,000 products fails the sync as too large, never as a shorter list. |
| Cadence, increments | Daily at 16:49 UTC (`49 16 * * *`), stale after 48 hours. The file changes daily; the version is the newest `lastModification` day among the products of the applicants that have a record. An applicant with no conformant product in a later copy is `withdrawn`. |
| Licence | **No Q row: CC BY 4.0 needs no decision.** The repository's LICENSE; the C2PA name and marks are not licensed and are used only to name the source. Attribution: `C2PA Conforming Products List, c2pa-org/conformance-public repository, CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by the C2PA.` |
| Record | One per applicant with at least one product whose status is exactly `conformant` (any other status is only counted): 199 records from 322 products on 2026-11-10 (204 applicants, 3 dropped as sole-trader businesses, 2 merged by punctuation; 2 records are named by the business that follows a trade-name mark, see Left out). `c2pa_conformance`, a **state**, dated by the newest conformance day (ordering only). The id is the compact name of the applicant as written (letters and digits, lower case): `Acme` and `Acme LLC` are two records; the spelling shown is the most frequent among the conformant products, a tie going to the first in the order of the characters. The title and the text show at most 80 characters of it; the identifiers hold it whole (100 at most). The text is `C2PA Conforming Products List (github.com/c2pa-org), a register or status list.` `A record says that the C2PA accepted conformance applications from this applicant for the products named; it says nothing about the applicant's other products, about the authenticity of any content, or about the applicant's conduct, and the C2PA can revoke a listing.`, then the applicant as written, the conformant products with the counts of generators and validators, the newest day and the count of listed products with another status, up to 5 product names with `and <n> more`, and `Source: C2PA conformance-public repository, CC BY 4.0; adapted by VerifyWise. The C2PA name and marks are the C2PA's.` last, which no cut takes. The address is `https://github.com/c2pa-org/conformance-public/blob/main/conforming-products/conforming-products-list.json?applicant=<id>`: the list is one file, so a record has no page of its own. |
| Left out and text safety | Never read or kept: `product.infoURL` (the applicant's own URL: never stored, fetched or used as a key), the technical fields (containers, live video, assurance, signals, versions), the certificate's unit and country, and the creation and disclosure days. Each cell is one cleaned line, capped (applicant and organization 100, product name 60, status 40); an applicant over 100 characters is unreadable, never cut (the longest real one has 61 on 2026-11-10). **Persons:** a product whose applicant holds one of these terms, in any letter case, is a person's business and is dropped as soon as its applicant is read, whatever else is wrong with it (its id included), counting nowhere (not even as unreadable). Parts of words: `sole propriet`, `sole trader`, `einzelunternehm`, `einzelkaufmann` or `einzelkauffrau`, `freelanc` and `self employed` (the words of a term of two words may be apart by up to three blanks and dashes of any kind, in any mix, or by none). Words of their own: `e.K`, `e. K`, `e.Kfm` or `e.Kfr` (the final full stop is optional), `e.U.` or `e. U.`, `Einzelfirma`, `eenmanszaak`, `EIRL`, `entreprise individuelle`, `auto-entrepreneur`, `micro-entrepreneur`, `empresario individual`, `autonomo` (with or without its acute accent), `ditta individuale`, `enskild firma` and `enkeltpersonforetak`, and `Sole Prop.` or `Inh.` (Inhaber). `EI` is no term. The same terms keep an organization name out of the keys, and the product stays. A company whose name holds one of these words is dropped too (it fails closed; a company really named `autonomo` would be). **Trade name:** an applicant `<name>, dba <business>` (also `d/b/a`, `doing business as`, `t/a` and `trading as`, in any case, after a blank or a comma and before a blank or the end of the name) is the business when the part before the first mark holds a letter or a digit and no legal form and no organization word of the kit's lists (the words that join the parts of a name, such as and, of, the, for and the particles of Spanish, are no organization words, since a person's name holds them). The part before the mark is then cut out of everything (the id, the keys, the names, the title, the text, the identifiers and the body), a chain of marks leaves the last business that no organization precedes, and a mark with nothing readable after it leaves a person's business, which is dropped. A before-part that holds a legal form or an organization word (`Zorb Inc., dba Zorb Studio`) is a business already, and the name is read as written. The terms above are asked of the whole cell before any mark cuts, and the certificate organization is cut by the same rule. On 2026-11-10 three applicants hold a mark (`dba` in one, `trading as` in two): one of them was already dropped as a person's business, and the other two records take the name of their business (199 records before and after, two ids replaced by two others, no merge, 343 keys before and 344 after). **Residuals:** a sole trader that does not say so, or says it in a form that is not listed (four separators between the words, a minus sign for a dash), is about 1 percent and accepted: a sole trader who applied for conformance is the vendor. The other spellings of a mark (`dba:`, `dba.`, `dba,`, `d.b.a.`, `trading as:`) and a mark inside brackets cut nothing. Trade-name shapes outside the five marks stay as they are: `operating as`, `o/a` and `trading under` (the whole applicant is kept, the person is in the key), the reverse order `<business> dba <person>` (the person is promoted to be the business), and `<person>, dba <business> (<person>)` and `<person>, dba <person> Photography` (the person stays in the business name); on 2026-11-10 the file holds none of the shapes that use one of the five marks (`operating as` and `o/a` were not counted). The short legal forms of the kit (`Co`, `Sa`, `As`, `Y`) make a before-part an organization, and a person who names no business and uses no term is a key like any other name. **Accepted residual:** a certificate organization that is only a person's name, or `<business> (<person>)`, is read as a second name of the record (also after an applicant `<person>, dba <business>` is renamed to its business), so the person's name can become a key and a name in the stored body row, and can reach the text where a product name or a certificate name shown there holds it (the title and the identifiers do not show it); the three mark applicants of the file all have a certificate organization that carries the mark too and is cut, so none reaches it, and the cheap fix (refuse a certificate organization that equals or contains the cut part) is not built, because the list is context only. |
| Match | `name:` keys only, weak: up to 3 spellings of the applicant, each as written and without the legal form, and up to 2 certificate organizations (`product.DN.O`, a second legal-entity name in 8 of 322 products on 2026-11-10) that are another name (one over 100 characters, under 4 letters and digits, holding a person term or cut by the trade-name rule to nothing is none). The 199 records carry 344 keys on 2026-11-10 (340 distinct). On 2026-11-10, 18 applicants end with a form that the legal-form rule does not strip (A/S, S.L., ApS, KG, SE, OU, e.V.) and keep it in the key. No `domain:` from `infoURL`: the host is the applicant's own claim, and a strong match would drop the lookup note. |
| Verdict | **Built, open.** No Q row decides it. An entry says that the C2PA's program accepted conformance applications from an applicant of that name for the products named; it never says anything about the authenticity of content or the applicant's conduct, and a missing entry is no finding. |

### D2.43 GLEIF LEI records

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Operator | The Global Legal Entity Identifier Foundation (GLEIF) ([API](https://www.gleif.org/en/lei-data/gleif-api)), which keeps the golden copy of the Legal Entity Identifier (LEI) system: reference data that each legal entity reports and its LEI issuer validates. |
| Category · level | `financial_health` · vendor, by LEI: **a stored LEI only**. The source is never asked by name (the name completions mix in funds and subsidiaries); a vendor without a stored LEI reads `not_researched`. Class TRUSTED_PRESS (reported by the entity and validated by its issuer, not a regulator's record), strength strong. A **state**: no lookback. Context only: it raises no risk, supports no credibility label and is given up first by the evidence pack (`lei_record`). At most 1 record for a run. |
| Endpoint, format | `https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records?filter[lei]=<LEI>`, keyless: one request for each stored LEI, in the filter form, whose answer is a list of at most one entry with a `meta.pagination.total` of 0 or 1 (an unknown LEI answers 200 with a total of 0; the path form answers 404 and is not used). It is asked through the run's `fetchJson`, whose `Accept: application/json` makes GLEIF answer `application/json`. Observed on 2026-11-10: 2,940 bytes. No `include=` and no request for a parent: the answer's `relationships` say, by the kind of link, whether a parent is reported, and that is all the record says. **Not verified:** that the filter form answers `application/json` for an LEI with a reporting exception. |
| Auth, limits | None. GLEIF allows 60 requests a minute for each user: a window shared by all workers in Redis keeps this list to 50 a minute and refuses a turn more than 1,500 ms away. The address is built from the stored LEI in upper case and nothing else; an answer is at most 128 KiB, and the run's own signal bounds the request (the 8 seconds that the read-through has for all its sources together). A failure keeps the stored records in use, reads `failed`, and the key is not asked again by any run for 5 minutes (not when the run was stopped or the turn was too far off). |
| Cadence, increments | **Not a schedule: a read-through** (as the NVD, D2.7). No sync copies the list. For a vendor with a stored LEI the stored answer is used while it is younger than `ttlHours` 24; an expired one, or an LEI never asked, is asked on the turn of the shared window and stored; an answer with no entry is stored as empty for 24 hours too. A key counts as stale after 48 hours (`staleAfterHours`). |
| Licence | **Decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q53), with no gate.** The data are CC0 1.0, under GLEIF's LEI Data Terms of Use: access only through GLEIF's access services, no claim of rights in LEI data, and no statement that implies that VerifyWise or its products are provided, supported, authorized or associated by or with GLEIF (liquidated damages of CHF 100,000 per case of non-compliance). The attribution and the second sentence of every record carry that duty. Attribution: `GLEIF LEI data (CC0 1.0), from the Global Legal Entity Identifier Foundation's API. VerifyWise is not associated with GLEIF, and GLEIF does not provide, support, authorise or endorse VerifyWise or its products.` |
| Record | One per stored LEI (a vendor has at most one): `gleif:<LEI in upper case>`; `lei_record`, a **state**, dated by the UTC day of the registration's last update; the title is `LEI record: <legal name>` (the LEI when the answer has no name). The text is `GLEIF LEI records (gleif.org), a register or status list.` `An LEI record is reference data that the entity reports and its LEI issuer validates; a lapsed registration means the data were not renewed on time, not anything about the entity's conduct, and GLEIF is not associated with VerifyWise.`, which no cut reaches and no omission drops, then the legal name, LEI, jurisdiction, legal form code and category, the entity and registration statuses with the next renewal and last update days (UTC days: GLEIF writes local midnights in UTC, so a day can be one early), the direct and ultimate parents as `reported (not looked up)` or `an exception is reported instead`, and `Golden copy of <day>.` A part that the answer does not give is left out. The address is `https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records/<LEI>`. |
| Left out and text safety | Read past and never copied: the addresses, the BIC, MIC, OCID, QCC, GEM and S&P numbers, the LEI issuer and how it validated the entity, the number in the entity's own register, other names, successor and associated entities, the links and the paging. Each kept field is read in its own form, and the legal name (200 characters at most), the one free text, is one cleaned line with no angle bracket; a name over 200 characters is cut and marked in the title and the text and is left out of the identifiers, which hold a name only whole. The answer is read as hostile: it must be what a filter by one LEI gives (at most one entry, a total equal to that number, the LEI asked, valid check digits), else the lookup fails as an unreadable format. The category can be `SOLE_PROPRIETOR`: no person filter is applied, since the LEI is the vendor's own stored identifier and the record holds no address. |
| Match | `lei:` only, strong: `keyOf` gives the key of the vendor's own stored LEI when it has a valid format and check digits, else none (the source reads `not_researched`). No `name:`, `regno:` (the `registeredAs` number is register-local) or `domain:` key (GLEIF has no website field), so a name collision cannot reach the record and it never carries the lookup note. |
| Verdict | **Built, open (Q53).** A read-through by a stored LEI only. An entry says what an entity reports and its issuer validates about its LEI and whether it reports parents; a lapsed registration is a maintenance status, never a statement about the company, and no text says that GLEIF endorses or certifies anything. |
### D2.44 Not built (2026-11-10)

The research of 2026-11-09 listed 34 rows of sources that could be added at once, and on 2026-11-10 the product owner asked for them to be added and delegated the decisions (Q35 to Q53). Twenty-three lists were built (D2.21 to D2.43), one was folded into a list that is built (D2.20, Q37), and eight were not built; the three rows of the research for the FDA's registration and listing, UDI and classification datasets count as one candidate below. The registry (`sr-6`, D3.4) is unchanged: no host is added, and no data is copied. A count below was observed on 2026-11-10.

| Source | What it is | Why it is not built | Registry |
|---|---|---|---|
| Health Canada recalls and safety alerts | The recalls and safety alerts that Health Canada publishes as open data, in one CSV that also holds the notices of the CFIA and Transport Canada. | The file has no company column. Of the notices last updated since 2023-11-10, only 38 have a title that names a firm (the form `<firm> recalls ...`), and 34 of those are cannabis licensees; the rest of the titles name brands or vehicle makes, which would match unrelated vendors, and the only date is the day a page was last edited. The list was not proven whole either, and a list that is not proven whole is no mirror. The licence (OGL-Canada) is not the obstacle. It can be added once the export gains a recalling-firm column. | None: `canada.ca` is official already under O8 |
| FDA MAUDE | The FDA's database of device adverse event reports (26.6 million), served through openFDA. D2.18 refused it on 2026-11-05 as not AI-specific. | The only way to tie the reports to the AI-enabled devices is a count by device number. The batched form of that count was refused by openFDA (403, a missing key), and the per-device form needs about 1,600 requests for each sync against the 1,000 a day that every openFDA list shares; spread over several days it would never give one consistent whole copy, which a mirror needs. The field the count relies on is undocumented, and MAUDE moves to the FDA's AEMS in 2026. The reports of a device number also cover the whole device, often an imaging system, and not its AI function. It would be reconsidered if the batched count were accepted. | None: `fda.gov` is official already under O1 (`.gov`) |
| FDA device registration and listing, UDI and device classification | Three openFDA datasets: the registration and listing of the firms that make devices (337,116 records), the unique device identification database (5,182,695) and the device classification (7,097). | None can be copied whole or adds a sound key. The first two are past openFDA's skip cap, so they could only be joined by K and PMA number, a join that was never tested. It would add names to a record that is context only, and the establishments include exporters, importers and contract manufacturers, which would attach the AI device list to the wrong companies. A UDI search also puts the GMDN terms (licensed separately) and customer contacts on the wire. The classification names no organization, its device class is already implied by the submission type on the FDA's AI list, and it would make that single-file mirror depend on api.fda.gov. | None: `fda.gov` is official already under O1 (`.gov`) |
| UK OPSS product safety alerts, reports and recalls | The notifications of the Office for Product Safety and Standards on GOV.UK (3,822), through the GOV.UK Search API. | The company is in no structured field: it is only in the free text of each notification, which was fetched for 500 of the 3,822. The Brand row there is a marketing label whose accuracy as a company name was never measured, and it can contradict the notification's own title. 17.5 % of the titles name a marketplace such as Amazon, and AI and software vendors almost never appear (Microsoft 1, Google 2 of 3,822). A copy of the whole list would need about 9 MB of text in 8 requests a week. The licence (OGL v3.0) is not the obstacle. | None: `gov.uk` is official already under O4 |
| US Department of Justice press releases | The Department's press releases, through its API: a title search, with no downloadable list. | A read-through was not verified: the title filter is a substring match (a search for Boeing matches an airport), and for the one vendor measured only 8 of 29 titles (28 %) concerned the company's own conduct. There is no whole list to copy instead. The search needs the company's name with its spaces, which the database's matching keys do not keep, and the site asks for 10 seconds between requests, which a vendor review cannot wait for. The titles also state allegations at the charging stage ("Charged", "Indicted"). | None: `justice.gov` is official already under O1 (`.gov`) |
| Court of Justice of the European Union judgments | The judgments of the Court, as metadata of the EUR-Lex CELLAR SPARQL endpoint of the Publications Office of the European Union. | No whole list was proven (26,210 judgments by a count; the row cap was not tested), so it could only be a read-through, and that fails the budget: each query by party name scans the corpus in 2.9 to 3.5 seconds even when nothing is found, about half of the 8 seconds that all the read-through sources of a run share. The endpoint's own terms for scheduled queries could not be read, and the party strings name natural persons in 12 of 30 sampled cases. A weekly copy that keeps only company parties is the way to add it later. | None: `europa.eu` is official already under O6 |
| Orca AI Incident Archive | A company-run archive on GitHub of AI incidents and vulnerabilities (415 entries), licensed CC BY 4.0 for its own compilation, classification and analysis. | It has no field that names the company involved. The words its titles would give are about half attack and malware codenames: the brand-shaped rule that works for the PipeRoll registry gives 66 keys over 97 of the 233 incident and vulnerability entries (42 %), and about 30 of the 415 titles name natural persons. It is run by a company and its licence covers its own summaries only, and its vulnerability entries repeat the CVE and NVD records that the database already holds. It can be added once the archive records a developer, deployer or vendor for each incident, with names that measure at 80 % or better. | None: `github.com` is not an entry |
| OSV PyPI advisories | The advisories of the GitHub Advisory Database for Python packages, through OSV (a zip of 6,831). | They name Python packages and not companies: none of the 6,831 names a vendor, so a match would need a package-to-company table kept by hand, which would be VerifyWise's own attribution and a new match method. Package names such as `torch` or `datasets` would match unrelated companies, and 29 % of the advisories of the last 24 months belong to packages of personal accounts. 98 % of the AI-package advisories are also CVEs, so they can reach a vendor through the NVD and, once its gate is open, through the CVE list. The licence (CC BY 4.0) is not the obstacle. It can be added once the product owner approves a table that ties each package to its organization. | None: `osv.dev` and `github.com` are not entries |
| FDA device enforcement reports (folded) | openFDA's device enforcement reports (40,450), which hold the FDA's classification of each recall (Class I, II or III). | Folded into the FDA recalls of D2.20 under Q37, and not built as a list of its own. The reports are past openFDA's skip cap of 25,000 (stated, not tested), the one bulk file is 90.78 MB zipped and several hundred MB of JSON, and no report carries a K or PMA number, so they reach the AI list only through the recall numbers that the join of D2.20 already holds.
