- **The ids and tables.** The 23 ids are in `RECORD_SOURCE_IDS` and `VRDB_SOURCE_IDS` (`domain.layer/interfaces/i.vendorResearch.ts`) and in the client's copy, in the order of `VRDB_SOURCES`, which is also the order of the records within a category (the evidence pack, below); the category of each is in `RECORD_CATEGORY` (`records/types.ts`), its cap in `MAX_RECORDS`, its class in `RECORD_CLASSES` (`sourceRegistry/classify.ts`), its database name (the mark its text starts with, which its connector exports as `<ID>_RECORD_MARK`) in `VERIFIER_DATABASE_NAMES` (`prompts.ts`, 39 names with the 16 of before), and its name in the briefing in `gather.ts`. A copy is stale after 48 hours for a daily list and 192 hours for a weekly one, and a record that its list no longer holds becomes `withdrawn`, or `delisted` for the sanctions list of Canada and for FedRAMP. The connector of each mirror is in the connector map (`CONNECTORS` in `vrdb/worker.ts`), and, as for every list that is taken whole, each gets a first sync at the first start after the upgrade.
- **Event or state.** Twelve of the 19 new types are events and are in `isEvent` (`vrdb/lookup.ts`, 18 types in all): the summaries of the incident, case, penalty, recall, investigation, notice and breach lists, each dated by its newest matter, so that the lookback of its category applies (24 months for `ai_specific` and `security_incidents`, 36 for `legal_regulatory`). The other seven, and `sanctions_entity`, are states: no lookback, and the record is offered as of the copy, because a listing in force, a registration or a status does not age out. A record with no day (an entity whose CMA case is still open) is kept by the lookback.
- **Downloads are pinned by host:** a connector accepts a file only from the operator's host after redirects, a 304 included (`kit/address.ts`), and a download from another host fails and never enters a record, a log line or an error text. The hosts, sizes, content types and time limits of each list are in D2.21 to D2.43.
- **The schedule.** Each of the 22 mirrors has a cron minute of its own, so that with the 12 mirrors of before 34 mirrors run at 34 distinct minutes (`sources.test.ts` keeps them distinct). The 8 daily lists are stale after 48 hours and the 14 weekly lists after 192, so that a weekly list is stale only after a missed week. No new list runs at hours 0, 6, 12 and 18, where the five lists that run every 6 hours do, and no two lists of one host run in the same hour of a day. The two NHTSA lists run about an hour after the times at which NHTSA's files were seen to be replaced on 2026-11-09 (08:03 and 09:03 UTC; one day observed). **First syncs:** `scheduleVendorRiskDatabaseSync` queues a first sync of every mirror that has no copy, in the order of `activeMirrors()`, and the worker runs them one at a time, so that on the first start the 22 new lists run back to back and the spacing of the crons does not apply; the order of `VRDB_SOURCES` keeps the lists of one host apart, and every connector pauses between its own requests. **Job time:** the worker sets no `lockDuration`, so BullMQ's default holds (a lock of 30 seconds, renewed every 15 by a timer while the event loop is free); the pauses are timers, so a job of about 2 minutes is safe, and no connector may do more than about 10 seconds of synchronous work in one go (the NHTSA zips are inflated and split as a stream, never in `read`).
- **The kit.** The connectors are built on one kit (`vrdb/connectors/kit/`). The factory `createFileConnector` (`fileConnector.ts`) holds what every list shares: the host pin, the limits of size, type and time, the validators with the version of the reading in the etag, and `ReadResult.complete`, so that a partial read is no read (a reader that stopped at its row limit, at a quote that was never closed, at a row of too many columns or before the last page has given part of a list, and the download fails as too large). The tolerance of rows that cannot be read is ten, or a hundredth of the rows when that is more, and never more than a tenth rounded up (`unreadableAllowed` in `clean.ts`: 12 rows allow 2, 100 allow 10, 5,000 allow 50); more is a new format and fails the sync. A reader that groups rows into entities counts in entities, and its `total` is the larger of the raw rows it saw and its rows plus its unreadable ones, since one raw row can name several entities (a body whose `total` is below that sum is refused). A reader whose unreadable ones are counted in raw lines says so with `allowanceBasis`, and the two kinds of loss are then measured apart: the two NHTSA files, which held 247,176 and 154,460 lines for 1,616 and 583 entities on 2026-11-10, are read so. The record builder (`record.ts`) is the one place where a record is made: an id of more than 200 characters is no id and the entry is refused, a title (200 characters) and a summary (1,300) that are longer are cut and say so (` [shortened]`), an address must be https and hold no character that a model would read as markup, `<` and `>` (and the characters that a fold turns into them) are written as the angle quotation marks (`withoutAngles`), and the phrase "lookup note" is written "lookup-note" in a title and a summary, because the prompt reads it as the way the vendor was matched and only the lookup says that. `lineOf` (`clean.ts`) is the function through which every cell that someone else wrote passes: one clean line, folded (NFKC), with no control or hidden character, read to 1,048,576 units at most and cut with the marker. A name, a number or a title that does not fit is never cut and then used: `lineWithin` gives a line that fits or nothing, `endsCut` tells a text that ends in the marker, and such a text is never a match key, an id or a whole name, so that a long name is left out and never cut (the lists that refuse a long name say so in their own sections, D2.21 to D2.43). The rest of the kit is the readers of tables, JSON, pages, archives and streams, the summary composer, which keeps the closing sentence whole and last (`summary.ts`), the pause (below) and the person rules (next).
- **Natural persons.** The rule is that a natural person is never an id, a key, a title, a display name, an identifier or a fragment of the text of a record, and a test fixture never uses a real name. Each list ensures it by one or more of the means below, and its own section (D2.21 to D2.43) says which. Two things stand beside the rule and are stated below: the one sole trader that the Government of Canada AI Register accepts, and the residuals that no rule closes. **First, the entry says what it is:** `canada_sema` reads the file's own elements for an individual or a ship (a given name, a family name, a date of birth, an IMO number) before any other cell of an entry, and an entry that has one is skipped, not copied and not counted. **Second, the columns that name persons are never read:** the case names, pointers, sources and texts of the AI Hallucination Cases database (its party column gives only the role of the filer, from its first words, and the only free-text cell that it reads is the tool cell), the paper authors of Epoch AI, the contacts and the free text of the registers and inventories, the named officials of the ATRS records, the assessors of FedRAMP and the sample notices of the California list. **Third, a rule measured on the real list.** The four rules of `kit/persons.ts` (`looksLikePerson`, which `ftc_penalties` widens with closures of its own in its connector, `euOrgEvidence` of the Commission's cases, `looksLikePersonEs` of the Colombian list and the practice rules of the California list) keep the word lists of their own measurement and are never merged, so that each keeps the precision and the recall that were measured; the rules of OFSI and of the CMA, the academic titles of the Dutch register, the title shapes of PipeRoll and the terms of a person's business in the C2PA list stay in their connectors. A rule is applied to the whole name and, where a cell names several parties or parts, to each of them, and what it takes for a person is taken out before anything is stored: a whole row, or only the person's side or part when a company stands beside it, so that the company stays. `ftc_penalties` drops a party that is a person with its row's share of the case, and the other parties of the case stay; `eu_dg_comp` keeps a name in an antitrust case that is no cartel only when it shows an organization or occurs in two cases or more; `co_sic_penalties` judges the whole cell and then each side of it, removes the sides that are persons and keeps the company sides; `california_breaches` judges each part of a name that an alias mark or a bracket sets apart, cuts out a part that is a person and keeps the business; a row with nothing left is dropped and counted nowhere. PipeRoll never takes a title word of a surname-prefix or a handle shape for a name, drops the first party of a case caption, and quotes it as "A party" in the text of a record. **Fourth, no rule, because the names are organizations:** every name of the real files of the NHTSA recalls (1,947) and investigations (595) was read and none is a person, and the same holds for the Foundation Model Transparency Index (13 of 13), Epoch AI (1,030 tokens), the federal AI use case inventory (430 strings), disclose.io (its only individuals are 183 program names that are a user's own subdomain, every one a row of two scope entries, which are never read), the supplier tokens of the UK ATRS records (30 read, 2026-11-10), the MHRA lists and FedRAMP, where a rule by the shape of a name had no precision or would drop real products; the Government of Canada AI Register has no rule either, and accepts the one sole trader that a department names as a supplier among its 127 names (under one percent); GLEIF is asked only by the vendor's own stored LEI and applies no filter by the kind of entity, so the record of an LEI whose category is `SOLE_PROPRIETOR` holds that person's legal name (the vendor itself) in its title and text. `ftc_competition` reads parties out of matter titles, where it has no person rule that works: it cuts every parenthetical group, where a person is named, and everything after the first hyphen with a blank on each side before it reads a party. The residuals are not closed, and the sections of the lists that have them state each one (D2.22 to D2.27, D2.29, D2.30, D2.31, D2.33, D2.34, D2.38 and D2.42): a UK ATRS supplier written as a person's name (a sole trader); a person's name of five words or more among the FTC penalty parties, and the other shapes that D2.25 lists (a digit in a name, a join with no blank round it, a trust, among others); a person named in the party part of an FTC nonmerger matter title; in an OFSI notice, a person-shaped name outside the closed vocabularies of its shape rule (a person named first in a title's list, a joiner or a spaced dash that the rule does not read, a name of one word or of six words or more, and the other shapes that D2.23 lists; none in the real page of 2026-11-10), and the address that GOV.UK keeps when a notice is retitled; a CMA party that is a person named without an honorific; in a PipeRoll title, a person whose name has neither of the two shapes that the rule knows (an intercapped given name, for one, which is also a key), and the other residuals that D2.26 lists; a tool cell of the AI Hallucination Cases database that names a person (none of the 109 distinct values of the real export does, 2026-11-10); a person behind an alias mark that the California rules do not split (`d.b.a.`, `doing business as`, `t/a`, `dba:`), behind a comma or an ampersand with no blank, `and` or a hyphen, a name that the middle-initial rule does not read (an accented letter, lower case, a title such as `Mr.`, a particle, last name first, a possessive), and a few plain two-word names of persons; in the Dutch register, a person with no title, honorific or post-nominal that its rule reads, and the title shapes that D2.38 leaves open (a title glued to initials, in later brackets or after other words of a piece, after a name with a particle or of two capitalized words, or after a surname or an initial in lower case or a hyphenated initial); a trade-name shape of the C2PA list outside the five marks, and a C2PA certificate organization that is only a person's name, which can reach the keys, the body row and, through a product or certificate name, the text; a colon, a bar, a tilde, a bullet, a middle dot, the CJK corner brackets or a combining mark with no precomposed form in an FTC party (none in the case names of 2026-11-10); a natural person named in a cartel or DMA case of the Commission's data, in two of its cases or more, or under a name that shows an organization marker; in a Colombian party, a person behind a hyphen, a dash or a `+` with no blank, a parenthesis or a comma, a name with a letter that Spanish does not use or of more than six words, a compound surname that holds ` Y `, a side of one word, and a person's name that holds an organization word; and, in a Colombian name, a hyphen, a minus sign or a middle dot that is not ASCII (U+2010, U+2011, U+2212, U+00B7) or an apostrophe other than the ASCII one and the typographic pair (U+00B4, U+02BC, the backtick), which its rule does not read (the real list holds 4 acute accents and 1 backtick, 2026-11-10).
- **The pause between requests.** A connector that makes more than one request to a host waits between them what that host asks for (`HOST_PAUSE_MS` in `kit/pause.ts`, five hosts): 20 seconds for `open.canada.ca`, 5 for `www.ftc.gov`, 1 for `www.datos.gov.co`, 400 milliseconds for `www.gov.uk` and, as the house pause where a host states no `Crawl-delay`, 1 second for the Commission's open data bucket (`compcases-open-data-portal-files-prod.s3.eu-west-1.amazonaws.com`, whose `robots.txt` answers 403), which `eu_dg_comp` waits once, between its two files. The FDA recalls keep a pause of their own, 500 milliseconds between requests (D2.20). `Connector.download` gets no abort signal, so the pause is a plain promise that a test replaces: a connector that makes several requests is built by a factory that takes it (`createFdaRecallsConnector({ sleep })` and the same for the others), and its tests assert the pauses and never wait.
- **No `robots.txt`, by design.** The connector fetch of `vrdb/worker.ts` reads a list through the page guard with no `robots.txt` hook, as the per-run lookups do, because a list is a file that its operator publishes for download; whether it may be used is a question of its licence and terms, which Q35 to Q53 answer list by list. The two operator signals that ask for more care are the Content-Signal `ai-train=no` of the AI Hallucination Cases database (Q39: its data never trains or fine-tunes a model, and only counts per tool reach the analysis model) and the reuse grant of the Dutch register, which names no licence (Q42). Both lists are built open on the delegation and listed for the product owner's confirmation, and the fallback is a gate, as for Q29, and the deletion of the copy. The `Crawl-delay` of an operator is kept by the connector between its own requests (above); a host that asks for 10 seconds and gets one request a sync (`oag.ca.gov`) needs no pause.
- **Every record has an address of its own.** The ledger drops a record whose address is already in it (`addRecords`: `duplicate`), and the provenance and the verifier map a record by its address, so that a list whose records all pointed at one page would lose every record of a vendor but the first. Sixteen lists have no page for an entity, and the address of a record is the page of the list or of its dataset with a query for the entity and the encoded id of the record, as `fda.ts` does with `?company=` and `csl.ts` with `?list=…&entry=…`: `?tool=` (the AI Hallucination Cases database), `?developer=` (the Foundation Model Transparency Index), `?vendor=` (the US federal AI use case inventory and the Government of Canada AI Register), `?organisation=` (Epoch AI and the California list), `?applicant=` (the C2PA list), `?party=` (the Colombian sanctions), `?provider=` (FedRAMP), `?program=` (disclose.io), `?undertaking=` (the Commission's cases), `&entity=` (the list of Canada), `?dataset=civil-penalty-cases&defendant=` (the FTC penalties), `?dataset=recalls&filer=` and `?dataset=investigations&manufacturer=` (the two NHTSA lists) and, for the Dutch register, the register's search with `?searchtext=` and the provider's name. A test of each asserts that two records of the list differ. The lists that point at the page of their newest item (the four GOV.UK lists, and PipeRoll, whose address is the permalink of the newest entry) have a cap of 1, and so does GLEIF, whose address is the API's record of the LEI; the link to the newest matter of `ftc_competition` (the dataset's page with `?party=` when no matter has a link), with a cap of 2, can be the same for two parties of one matter, and the second is then dropped as a duplicate of the same matter, which is accepted.
- **Records need a website, for some.** `ai_specific` and `certifications` are site categories (`SITE_CATEGORIES` in `categories.ts`), researched only for a vendor with a domain, so that a vendor with no website gets none of the records of `charlotin`, `piperoll`, the registers and indexes of AI (`canada_ai_register`, `omb_ai_inventory`, `nl_algoritmeregister`, `uk_atrs`, `epoch_ai` and `fmti`), `fedramp` and `c2pa_conforming`, and the coverage lists them as `not_researched`. The other categories are researched with no website, so the sanctions, enforcement, product safety and breach lists look a vendor up by name; `gleif` is asked only when the vendor has a stored LEI, and is `not_researched` otherwise.
- **Registers never raise a risk and never support a label.** Ten lists are context only (`CONTEXT_ONLY_RECORD_SOURCES`, `records/types.ts`): `canada_ai_register`, `omb_ai_inventory`, `nl_algoritmeregister`, `uk_atrs`, `epoch_ai`, `fmti`, `c2pa_conforming`, `fedramp`, `disclose_io` and `gleif`. A record of one is a register's statement and not a finding: the prompt raises no risk from it, and a vendor that a register lacks is no risk either. The credibility rules skip them: a source whose record is of one of these lists is never a supporting source (`isContextOnly` in `credibility.ts`, in the sentence builder of `credibilityText.ts` and in `ruleSentence` of the client's `vendorResearchText.ts`), so that a finding that cites only a FedRAMP listing as context, which can match strongly through a verified domain, is Low by the last rule and is not High as an official record, and its line reads "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk." (the same sentence in the server and in the client). A register never names itself in the line of a label, withdrawn or not, and the day of its stale copy is not said in "The source has not been updated since {day}."; the name-only flag and its note are still computed over all the sources. `fda_ai_devices` raises no risk either, but keeps its shipped behavior.
- **Weak lists, on by default.** Every raising list is found by name alone, so that each of its records carries the lookup note and gives Low support (D5.3), and the prompt lets an entry raise at most one risk, written as an entry of the list and worded "appears in" (the sanctions risks say "is on", D4.13, and the risk of the California list says "reported a data breach to the California Attorney General"), with a likelihood of Possible at most and an action plan that checks who the vendor is against the entry. Eight lists were built weak, after a measurement of the names that their text gives, and are on by default: `uk_atrs`, `uk_cma`, `uk_ofsi`, `uk_mhra`, `piperoll`, `charlotin`, `nl_algoritmeregister` and `ftc_competition`; a name that a list has to read out of free text (a case title, a notice, a heading) can be wrong, and the precision measured on 2026-11-10 is in each section. A wrong match costs one Low risk that tells the reader to check the identity (`uk_atrs` and `nl_algoritmeregister` are context only: a wrong match of theirs is a wrong entry of context and raises no risk). No list was refused for this reason.
- **The evidence pack.** A run can now be offered up to 78 records, which at their caps are about 161.5 KB against the 100,000 bytes of the pack (`MAX_EVIDENCE_BYTES`); the lists of before could already reach 93 KB, and in practice a vendor matches a handful of lists (a large AI vendor reaches 15 to 25 records, 20 to 40 KB). The trimming pops the last source of the category with the most bytes, so that a register record would outlive the news article that shows a real incident. A record of a context-only list is therefore removed first (`trimLargest` in `evidence.ts`: of the categories that hold two or more sources, at least one of them such a record, the one with the most bytes loses its last such record), and the order of `VRDB_SOURCES` puts, within a category, the sanctions before the enforcement before the product safety lists, and the raising lists before the registers, so that the sanctions entries are the last to go. `MAX_EVIDENCE_BYTES` and `MAX_SNIPPET_CHARS` do not change.
- **The coverage a person reads.** The lists and lookups of before keep their lines as they were, and `canada_sema` joins them as a named list: a screening against a sanctions list is information that is named, so the Checked line gives its name and its count and, when it held nothing, the line "No records in {sources} as of {dates}" says so. The other 22 lists are shown when found (`LISTS_SHOWN_WHEN_FOUND`, `Clients/src/application/utils/vendorResearchText.ts`): the Checked line names one only when the run was offered at least one record of it, and counts the lists that were asked and held nothing in one last item ("{count} more lists with no records", or "{count} lists with no records" when no list is named before it, with the singular forms); a list that failed is always named in the Not checked line, since a silent gap would read as a clean screening; the stale lines are never capped, and a list whose copy is stale gets its line whenever the run asked it, with or without a record; and the Data sources line carries the attribution of a list only when a record of it was offered, so that a run that offered none owes nothing (the attribution follows the data). The server has a twin of the set, `LISTS_NAMED_WHEN_FOUND` (`records/types.ts`, the same 22 ids), for the briefing of the research loop: `describeGathered` (`gather.ts`) names such a list only when it gave records or failed, and counts the others in one line, because one line for each of 37 lists on every step of the loop costs about 5 KB a step.
- **Prompt `vr-15`** (the version of 2026-11-10, after `vr-14`). The analysis prompt words the new lists by their class, in the span between the sanctions bullet and "How to rate a risk:", so that the rest of the prompt is the `vr-14` text byte for byte. The sanctions list of Canada joins the sanctions bullet. New bullets word the AI incident lists (`charlotin`, `piperoll`: naming a tool does not show that it caused the error, and a registry's entries are reports that nobody has checked), the enforcement and case lists (`eu_dg_comp`, `ftc_penalties`, `ftc_competition`, `uk_cma`, `uk_ofsi` and `co_sic_penalties`: a case, an investigation or a notice is not a finding or a ruling, and a notice of OFSI is not a designation), the product safety lists (`nhtsa_recalls`, `nhtsa_odi` and `uk_mhra`: a recall or a field safety notice is the maker's own correction of a product, and an investigation is not a finding that a defect exists), the data breach list (`california_breaches`: the organization itself reported a breach, and the entry says nothing of its cause or its harm) and the registers (the ten lists above): such an entry raises no risk, whatever it says, serves only as context for a risk that other sources show, and is never evidence that the vendor or its product is safe, compliant or approved. An entry of a list that can raise a risk raises at most one, in the category of its list, written as an entry of the list and never as a finding against the vendor, with a likelihood of Possible at most and an action plan that checks who the vendor is against the entry. The verifier prompt does not change; `VERIFIER_DATABASE_NAMES` gains one name for each new list, so the verifier is told the database of a cited record. `vr-15` has not been run against the golden set (a run costs the research key's credits), as `vr-13` and `vr-14` were not; its wording reaches only a source whose text begins with one of the new marks.

---

## D5. Credibility rules

### D5.1 What the label says

How well the cited sources establish the facts a finding rests on. It does not say how likely or how serious the risk is (likelihood and severity do), and it is not a risk colour. Code computes it from the categories of the cited sources, the number of independent publishers and, for a database record, how the record was matched and whether it is still active. The model never sees or writes it.

### D5.2 Inputs

For each source a finding cites, after the validation and the snippet check:

- **Category** from the registry (D3), after its flags: DOWNGRADE makes it UNVERIFIED. SYNDICATED with `canonical` gives the copy the category and publisher of the page its canonical URL names, when that is another registry host, and UNVERIFIED otherwise; with `issuer` (BAILII, CourtListener) the issuing court is the publisher. BLOCKED never reaches a finding.
- **Publisher:** the entry's `publisher` when it has one, else `publisherKey(url)` as today; all the vendor's pages are one publisher; a record's publisher is its operator.
- **Kind**, for a VENDOR source: statement or self-claim (§D5.4).
- For a VRDB record: **match strength** (strong or weak), **status** (active, withdrawn, rejected, delisted), **freshness** (fresh or stale) and the **date of the data**.
- **Reading:** read (a page the research read, or a record), unconfirmed (a search result whose page could not be read), contradicted (the page was read and does not hold the quote).

**Records of the AI-specific lists (Q30).** The class of a record is that of its list and is not always OFFICIAL (D3.2): `aiid` UNVERIFIED (an aggregator of allegations, D5.3 step 3), `atlas` TRUSTED_PRESS (MITRE curates it with references, but it is not an authority, as its registry entry P80 says), `nhtsa_sgo` OFFICIAL (a regulator's record of reports that manufacturers filed, as with SEC filings) and `fda_ai_devices` OFFICIAL (the regulator's own authorization list). All four are weak, because every key is a name and none of the lists carries an identifier, a domain or a vendor field of a vulnerability list. Under step 0 below every such record therefore gives Low support and the name-only note, and the class decides only the publisher label and the rationale. A class that is too strict leaves these findings at Low unless the press corroborates them, which is safe; a class that is too loose would let one name collision earn High.

**Records of the two lists of 2026-11-07 (Q33, Q34).** `csl` and `fda_device_recalls` are OFFICIAL: a government's own compilation of its lists, and the regulator's own record of a recall. Both are weak when found by name, so under step 0 below a record gives Low support and the name-only note, as an AI-specific record does. A `csl` record that carries the vendor's verified website or its LEI (the OFAC lists only) is strong, as a UK or EU record is, and may reach High. The lists of Commerce and State (the Entity List, the Denied Persons List, the Unverified List, the Military End User List, the ITAR Debarred List and the Nonproliferation Sanctions) carry no website and no identifier, so a vendor that really is the listed entity is Low with the note. That label says that the list is official and that the identity is not proven by a key; it does not say that the finding is unreliable, and the finding's own text ("An entity named … is on …") and its action plan (check the address, the country and the owners) carry the weight. A Low label on a real hit is safe in the direction that matters, since a person reads the finding before it reaches the register, while a High label on a name collision with a short name would be worse. A listing on the Entity List or the Military End User List is a licence requirement and not a sanction, and the analysis prompt says so (`vr-14`, D4.13). A recall is Low with the note for the same reason, and because the recalling firm may be a subsidiary or a distributor of the maker, and a recall is not a finding that the AI failed.

### D5.3 The rule

0. A contradicted source, and a record that is withdrawn, rejected or delisted, gives no support. A weak record gives only Low support, whatever its class: it counts toward neither High nor Medium, and it is no publisher for step 2.
1. **High** when at least one supporting source is
   - **H1** OFFICIAL: a page on an OFFICIAL host, or a VRDB record with a strong match; or
   - **H2** a VENDOR statement (§D5.4); or
   - **H3** TRUSTED_PRESS from at least two different publishers.
2. **Medium** when the supporting sources include TRUSTED_PRESS from exactly one publisher, however many of its pages and with or without other sources. The sources counted here are the strong ones, as in step 1: a weak record is no publisher for this rule either (amended on 2026-11-05, Q30, and built in `credibility.ts`). This matters for the ATLAS case studies, whose class is TRUSTED_PRESS: a finding whose only TRUSTED_PRESS source is a weak ATLAS record is Low with the note, and one with a strong trusted publisher and a weak ATLAS record is Medium, the ATLAS record not counted as a second publisher.
3. **Low** otherwise: only UNVERIFIED sources (unknown hosts, aggregators, users' posts, downgraded pages, copies that cannot be credited), only the vendor's self-claims, only weak records, or nothing left after step 0.
4. **Unconfirmed cap:** when every source that earned High is an unconfirmed search result, the label is Medium.
5. **Name-only note:** whenever a cited record matched by name only, the chip carries "Matched by name only: this may be another company." Brief §2.6 says a weak match "caps at Low"; this design reads it as a cap on what the record contributes (step 0), so that other sources can still earn Medium or High (case 9b) while the note stays. The strict reading caps the whole finding at Low; Q24 asks which one the brief means.
6. **Stale data** does not change the label; the rationale gives the date of the data, and the coverage block says the source is not up to date.

Match strength applies to VRDB records. A web page counts for the vendor through `identity.ts` (the name, an other name or the domain as a whole word) and the verifier's `same_company` question, as today; making web pages "name only" would make nearly every press finding Low (Q17).

### D5.4 Vendor sources (rule V)

The brief makes the vendor asymmetric: an admission of an incident is strong, a positive self-claim is never independent. Code tells the two apart without a model:

- A VENDOR source is a **statement** when the finding is in an event category (`security_incidents`, `availability`, `legal_regulatory`, `financial_health`), where a vendor page cited for a risk reports an incident, an outage, a legal matter or a financial event of the vendor itself; or when the page is one of the vendor's status, trust, security or policy pages (the pages `classifySitePage` in `gather.ts` already serves to `certifications` and `ai_specific`, and the status page hosts), which a finding cites for what they bind the vendor to or report about it.
- Every other VENDOR source is a **self-claim**: a marketing, newsroom or blog page of the vendor, or a wire release, cited in a `certifications` or `ai_specific` finding. A self-claim never counts as independent support and never raises a label.
- A wire release is VENDOR only when its text carries an e-mail address on the vendor's own domain, such as its media contact. A link to the domain or a mention of it is not enough, so a security firm's release that links the vendor's advisory and a law firm's investor alert about the vendor are UNVERIFIED (Q14, narrowed by Q27).
- User content is never VENDOR, even on the vendor's own domain: a community thread or a post on a platform is UNVERIFIED (§D3.2 step 1, Q25).

The limit (Q12): a positive claim on a trust page, cited by a finding about something missing, gets High. The verifier's `quote_supports_claim` and the person who accepts the finding are the remaining checks.

### D5.5 Rationale and note

One line, generated by code from `credibility_basis`, translated on the client:

| Rule | English |
|---|---|
| H1 record | High: {operator} lists it ({record}), as of {date}. |
| H1 page | High: an official source reports it ({host}). |
| H2 | High: the vendor states it on its own pages ({host}). |
| H3 | High: {count} independent trusted publishers report it ({publishers}). |
| Medium | Medium: one trusted publisher reports it ({publisher}); no official source and no second independent publisher. |
| Cap | Medium: only search results show it; the pages could not be read. |
| Low, unverified | Low: only unverified sources report it ({hosts}). |
| Low, self-claim | Low: only the vendor's own claims support it. |
| Low, weak | Low: the {list} entry matches the vendor by name only. |
| Low, contradicted | Low: the pages do not hold what the search results quoted. |
| Low, inactive | Low: {operator} {rejected / withdrew / removed} the record on {date}. |
| Stale (added) | The source has not been updated since {date}. |
| Backfill (added, after the rule's line) | Rated again with the current rule. |
| Legacy (the whole line) | Rated with the earlier confidence rule. |
| Note | Matched by name only: this may be another company. |

### D5.6 Behaviour changes against `confidenceCeiling`

1. **The model's view is gone.** Today the label is the lower of the model's confidence and the ceiling; the new label ignores the model. Replay case `long-policy-part` goes from low to High because the model's "low" no longer counts.
2. **One official source is enough for High.** Today a single official page or record is Low (one URL that is not the vendor's): a KEV record, a regulator's page, an SEC filing.
3. **One vendor page** is Medium today; it becomes High as a statement and Low as a self-claim.
4. **Pages of unknown publishers** make Medium today when there are two URLs (two pages of one unknown site, as the brief notes, or of two sites); they become Low. Six of the 22 replay findings change this way.
5. **One page of one trusted publisher** goes from Low to Medium.
6. **A vendor page plus any second publisher** is High today (the vendor's tier is credible); it stays High only if the vendor page is a statement, otherwise the other source decides (Medium for a trusted publisher, Low for an unknown one).
7. **Aggregators and users' posts** are "no publisher" today but count toward the two URLs of Medium; they become UNVERIFIED and never rise above Low on their own.
8. **A syndicated copy** with a canonical link to a registry publisher counts as that publisher; today it is no publisher.
9. **Paid wires** are aggregators today; they become VENDOR when they carry an e-mail address on the vendor's domain (statement or self-claim by rule V), else UNVERIFIED (Q27).
10. **Unconfirmed snippets** keep any confidence today (R26); they are capped at Medium.
11. **Contradicted snippets** are Low, as today.
12. **Records** are classified by their operator, not by the host of their link.
13. **Name-only matches** are Low with the note; with two trusted publishers they are High with the note, as High today but without one.
14. **Inactive records** no longer support a label; today no status exists.
15. **BAILII and CourtListener** are credible today and OFFICIAL by SYNDICATED: no change in effect.
16. **Forbes contributor pages** and other downgraded paths are major-publisher pages today and UNVERIFIED (if G1 and the path rules are approved, Q11).
17. **Unverified government suffixes** are unchanged if Q8 keeps them, UNVERIFIED if not.
18. **The eval's `overconfident` measure** (High with fewer than two publishers) would flag a correct single-official High, so it is replaced (D6.1).

### D5.7 Worked cases

"Today" is what `confidenceCeiling` allows (the stored value can be lower if the model said less).

| # | Situation | Sources and their support | Today | Proposed | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | One KEV entry for the vendor's product | KEV record CVE-2026-31337, OFFICIAL, strong (`vendorProject` is the vendor's name), active, fresh | Low (one URL; replay case 17) | **High** (H1) | High: CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities lists it (CVE-2026-31337), as of 28 Oct 2026. |
| 2 | Reuters and BBC report the same breach | `reuters.com` and `bbc.co.uk`, TRUSTED_PRESS, two publishers, read | High | **High** (H3) | High: 2 independent trusted publishers report it (Reuters, BBC). |
| 3 | One BBC story on `bbc.co.uk` and `bbc.com` | TRUSTED_PRESS, one publisher (BBC), two pages | Medium (two URLs) | **Medium** | Medium: one trusted publisher reports it (BBC); no official source and no second independent publisher. |
| 4 | Five syndicated copies of an AP story | copies on `yahoo.com` and `msn.com` whose canonical URL is on `apnews.com` (AP), and three unknown local sites (UNVERIFIED) | Medium (three unknown publishers, none credible; two or more URLs) | **Medium**; **Low** if no copy carries a canonical link to a registry host | Medium: one trusted publisher reports it (AP); … |
| 5 | A single Medium post alleges a breach | `medium.com`, UNVERIFIED | Low | **Low** | Low: only unverified sources report it (medium.com). |
| 6 | A vendor press release claims ISO 27001 | a `certifications` finding citing `vendor.com/press/…` (VENDOR, self-claim), or the copy on a wire (VENDOR if it carries an e-mail on `vendor.com`, then a self-claim; else UNVERIFIED) | Medium (one vendor page); Low for the wire copy | **Low** | Low: only the vendor's own claims support it. |
| 7 | The vendor's own incident notice | a `security_incidents` finding citing `vendor.com/blog/security-update` (VENDOR, statement: event category) | Medium (one vendor page) | **High** (H2) | High: the vendor states it on its own pages (vendor.com). |
| 8 | A news article cites an ICO fine, and the `ico.org.uk` page was read | an unknown news site (UNVERIFIED) and `ico.org.uk` (OFFICIAL, read) | High (two publishers, one credible) | **High** (H1) | High: an official source reports it (ico.org.uk). |
| 9 | A sanctions entity has the vendor's name | OFAC SDN entity record, OFFICIAL, weak (name only) | Low (one URL) | **Low** and the note | Low: the OFAC SDN list entry matches the vendor by name only. |
| 9b | The same, and Reuters and BBC report this vendor's designation | the weak record and two TRUSTED_PRESS publishers | High | **High** and the note (Q24; **Low** and the note under the strict reading) | High: 2 independent trusted publishers report it (Reuters, BBC). |
| 10 | A CVE is later marked REJECTED | the record's status becomes `rejected` (the CVE mirror, M2) | no status; the finding keeps its value | New runs: the record is not offered, so no finding. A pending finding that cites it: **Low** (status job, M2), and the source shows the status | Low: the CVE Program rejected the record on {date}. |
| 11 | A sanctions entry is delisted | the entity is missing from today's full file: `delisted` on the day of the sync | the next run does not find it; an older finding keeps its value | New runs: not offered, no finding. A pending finding: **Low** (status job) | Low: OFAC removed the record on {date}. |
| 12 | A VRDB source is stale | the OFAC sync has failed for three days (stale after 48 h); a stale KEV mirror still holds the record of case 1 | — | The label stays (case 1: **High**); the coverage block lists OFAC as not up to date; where nothing was found: "No records in OFAC SDN list as of 26 Oct 2026" | High: CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities lists it (…), as of 26 Oct 2026. The source has not been updated since 26 Oct 2026. |
| 13 | Two trusted publishers seen only in search results | `reuters.com` and `bbc.com` results, neither page readable (unconfirmed) | High (R26 keeps it) | **Medium** (cap) | Medium: only search results show it; the pages could not be read. |
| 14 | A contributor post on `forbes.com/sites/…` | DOWNGRADE, so UNVERIFIED | Low with one URL, Medium with two | **Low** | Low: only unverified sources report it (forbes.com). |
| 15 | One trusted publisher and the vendor's marketing page, in a `certifications` finding | TRUSTED_PRESS (one) and a VENDOR self-claim | High (the vendor page is a credible publisher) | **Medium** | Medium: one trusted publisher reports it (…). |
| 16 | The vendor's status page lists a long outage | the status host, VENDOR statement | Low (one record; replay case 19) | **High** (H2) | High: the vendor states it on its own pages (status.vendor.com). |
| 17 | A Have I Been Pwned breach entry | `haveibeenpwned.com`, UNVERIFIED (a lookup, §2.8) | Low | **Low** | Low: only unverified sources report it (haveibeenpwned.com). |
| 18 | A law firm's investor alert on a paid wire | a release that does not name the vendor's domain, so UNVERIFIED | Low (aggregator) | **Low** | Low: only unverified sources report it (businesswire.com). |

## D6. Evaluation plan

### D6.1 The existing replay cases

The reason for every change below is brief §2.5: the model no longer gives a confidence and the label is computed.

- **Remove `confidence`** from the recorded analysis of the 18 cases that have findings (01, 03, 04, 06, 07, 08, 10 to 21).
- **Add the expected label** to each expected finding (`credibility` on `ExpectedFinding` in `eval/types.ts`), with the values of Appendix B.
- **Replace `maxOverconfident` by `maxMislabelled: 0`** in the 21 cases: a kept finding whose label differs from the label of its expected finding. `overconfident` leaves `scorer.ts`; its own publisher key stays for the duplicate measures (R15).
- Case 18 (SEC) expects Low with the note until Q3; case 20 expects the note.
- Every case whose label changes gets one sentence in its comment saying why (Appendix B). The cases use `.example` hosts for publishers on purpose; under the registry these are UNVERIFIED, which is what the six Medium-to-Low changes show.

### D6.2 New replay cases

One for each worked case that no existing case covers. The runner takes the VRDB's answer from the case (`recording.vrdb`: records with their source, status, match strength and data date, and the coverage), the way it takes `recording.records` today; no database, no network.

| Case | Worked case | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| 22 `two-trusted-publishers` | 2 | High |
| 23 `syndicated-copies` | 4 | Medium; a variant without canonical links: Low |
| 24 `user-content-only` | 5 | Low |
| 25 `vendor-self-claim` | 6 | Low |
| 26 `vendor-incident-notice` | 7 | High |
| 27 `regulator-page` | 8 | High |
| 28 `rejected-cve` | 10 | the record is not offered; no finding |
| 29 `delisted-sanctions-entry` | 11 | not offered; no finding |
| 30 `stale-source` | 12 | High with "as of"; coverage `stale` |
| 31 `same-name-on-two-lists` | 9, OFAC and UK entries of one name | one finding, Low, the note |
| 32 `unconfirmed-trusted-snippets` | 13 | Medium |
| 33 `contributor-page` | 14 | Low |

### D6.3 Unit tests, written before the code

- **`sourceRegistry/__tests__/classify.test.ts`:** a host and its subdomains; the longest match wins; suffix rules (`example.gov` yes, `notgov.com` no, `x.gov.uk`, `.int`, `europa.eu`); the vendor's domains first; BLOCKED; DOWNGRADE per host and G1; SYNDICATED with and without a canonical URL on a registry host; a wire with and without an e-mail on the vendor's domain in its text (Q27); user content on the vendor's domain and on a platform that is its website (Q25); an EDGAR filing, a public comment and a RECAP filing against the official pages of the same hosts (Q26); an unknown host is UNVERIFIED and unreviewed; a record is classified by its source, not its host.
- **`sourceRegistry/__tests__/entries.test.ts`**, the registry's hygiene: every entry has a reason, evidence with a status and a review date; hosts are lowercase and unique; no TRUSTED_PRESS entry is without evidence; a review older than 12 months is printed, one older than 18 months fails.
- **`__tests__/credibility.test.ts`:** one test per worked case (18 and 9b), plus an inactive record, a weak record with two trusted publishers (High and the note under the recommended answer to Q24), a vendor statement with an unknown site (High), a self-claim with one trusted publisher (Medium), only contradicted sources (Low), an unconfirmed official page (Medium), and the content of the basis.
- **Connectors**, with fixtures written from the documented formats and not copied from the real lists, so that no personal data enters the repository: KEV (fields, `dateAdded`, removal by diff), OFAC (CSV without a header, `-0-`, entities only, the ALT join), UK (XSD 4.33.3 fields, entities only, website and registration number keys), EU (XSD 1.1, `classificationCode` E and P, `delistingDate`); later UN, CVE (`REJECTED`), NVD and SEC.
- **`vrdb/__tests__/sync.test.ts`:** insert, update on a changed record, an unchanged hash, delisting by diff, the shrink guard, a parse failure that keeps the mirror, the sync run rows, staleness.
- **`vrdb/__tests__/lookup.test.ts`:** keys, one query, strong and weak, the match rows, the caps, the coverage, the off switch.
- **Integration:** `tests/integration/vrdb.sync.test.ts`, `tests/integration/vrdb.lookup.test.ts`, `tests/integration/tenant-isolation/vendor-vrdb-matches.isolation.test.ts`, and the audit script.
- **Client:** `FindingRow` (the chip, the rationale of every rule, the note, the badges, an inactive record), `RunSummary` (the checked sources, "No records in … as of …", stale sources, the counts, "Public databases turned off"), and the i18n test for de, fr and es.

No test reaches the network: fixtures and fake fetches, as today.

### D6.4 Golden set

24 vendors, one per profile below, run live with the flag off and on (keyless sources, the organisation's LLM key) and compared with a person's labels.

| # | Profile | # | Profile |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1–4 | Products in the KEV catalog (network appliance, file transfer, IT management, VPN) | 13 | Certifications only in its own claims |
| 5–6 | A breach reported by several trusted publishers | 14–15 | Its name, or nearly, on a sanctions list (a trap) |
| 7 | Its own published incident notice | 16 | A rejected CVE in its products (a trap) |
| 8 | A data protection fine on a regulator's site | 17 | Coverage by unknown blogs only |
| 9 | A US registrant with an 8-K Item 1.05 filing | 18 | A small vendor with nothing to find |
| 10 | A long outage on its status page | 19 | No website domain |
| 11 | Layoffs, insolvency or a sale in the press | 20 | Press in German or French |
| 12 | AI terms that allow training on customer data | 21 | A wire story copied on aggregators |
| | | 22 | Contributor or opinion pieces only |
| | | 23 | Law firms' investor alerts on wires (a trap) |
| | | 24 | A delisted sanctions entry (optional) |

**What I need from you for each vendor:** (1) confirm the vendor (name, website, other names) and that it fits its profile; I can propose candidates in Phase 2 after checking each against its profile; (2) the risks a careful analyst would raise from public sources inside each category's lookback, each with the sources they would cite; (3) the label they would give each under D5; (4) the traps: findings that must not appear (another company of the same name, a delisted entry, a rejected CVE, an old event). That is about 30 to 45 minutes per vendor.

**Proposed bars** for turning the flag on by default: precision of at least 0.9; recall not below the flag-off run; label agreement of at least 80 %; no finding labelled High that the labeller rates Low; no sanctions finding that states a match as a fact; the coverage block right for every vendor.

---

## D7. Risks and open questions

### Open questions (each with a recommendation)

| # | Question | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Should vendors get identifiers (country, company registration number, LEI, SEC CIK)? They would turn name-only matches strong: the UK list carries registration numbers and websites, the EU list identification numbers. | Yes, as optional fields on `vendors` in M2; no parallel identity table. Not in the MVP. |
| Q2 | Is the EU list's fixed public token keyless? | Yes: the operator publishes it to everyone in its own feed; read the file URLs from that feed at every sync, so no token sits in our code or configuration. If no, the EU list moves to later. |
| Q3 | A CIK found by name alone: strong (brief §2.6, "the vendor's own SEC CIK") or weak (a name match)? | Weak until a second identifier confirms it (a CIK stored on the vendor, Q1). SEC is M2. |
| Q4 | The SEC asks for a User-Agent with a company name and a contact e-mail; ours has a URL and no e-mail. Which address may it carry? | A role address VerifyWise controls, chosen by you, as a constant in code (not a setting). **Decided on 2026-10-31:** `tech@verifywise.ai`, the Technical Support address that verifywise.ai/contact publishes (a role address on VerifyWise's own domain, not a person's), held as the constant `SEC_CONTACT` in `vrdb/gates.ts`. The User-Agent is `VerifyWise tech@verifywise.ai`, the form the SEC asks for. The gate is open. |
| Q5 | OFAC publishes no licence. Does 17 U.S.C. §105 cover the SDN files for storing them and showing derived data commercially? | Build the MVP behind the flag; get a legal confirmation before the flag is on by default. **Decided on 2026-10-31:** accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-31, without a separate legal review. |
| Q6 | The UN terms of use could not be read here. | A person reads them in a normal browser; the UN list waits until then. **Decided on 2026-10-31** (the terms were read that day): they allow personal, non-commercial use only, the copyright page reserves every other use (storage in a retrieval system included) for written permission, and UN Publications asks for written permission to reuse content from a UN database. That fails D2's criterion (d), as the World Bank's terms do (D2.9). The gate stays closed (`UN_PERMISSION_GRANTED = false`) and opens only with the UN's written permission, which is not sought: the UK list holds all 274 UN entities, 269 with their UN reference number, which UK and EU records now show (D2.5). The connector was checked against the real file the same day and stays behind the gate. |
| Q7 | The CVE terms require MITRE's copyright notice and the licence with every copy and do not mention commercial use; the full archive is about 650 MB. | A legal review before M2; then mirror only the fields we need for CVEs inside the lookback, and store the notice with them. Before the gate opens (M2 final review): refuse a scheduled sync while no baseline set the cursor (`no_baseline`; done 2026-10-31); let the baseline's batches leave `last_outcome` and `last_success_at` alone until its last apply (done 2026-10-31); decide whether one record that does not parse fails its day; retire the stored copy of a record whose change no longer passes the filter, as the NVD read-through does; date a rejection by the record's `dateRejected` (done 2026-10-31). **Decided on 2026-10-31:** accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-31, without a separate legal review. The remaining items are done: a day applies the records it can read and leaves out, and counts, the entries it cannot, and fails as `parse_errors` only when they number more than max(10, 1% of its record entries); a day without an end-of-day release is read from its last hourly release (the tags of the day, listed through the `git/matching-refs` request) and refused only when it has no release at all; a stored record whose change no longer passes the filter is retired as the NVD read-through does (its keys off, its content hash invalidated, its status kept, except that a rejection the CVE Program has since replaced by a published record becomes active again; no relabel); the baseline is loaded once per deployment by the operator, with `scripts/vrdbCveBaseline.ts`, and nothing downloads it. The gate is open. The notice is stored with the copy: MITRE's copyright designation as the attribution and the CVE Usage paragraph as the licence. |
| Q8 | The unverified government suffixes (`.mil`, `bund.de`, `gc.ca`, `govt.nz`, `gov.br` and the generic country patterns of `GOVERNMENT_HOST`): keep (no change) or limit to the verified ones? | Keep them provisionally; verify them in the review before the flag is on by default; drop what cannot be verified. **Decided on 2026-10-31:** narrowed to explicit hosts, and the regex removed (87% of the 1,240 names it matched were ordinary registrations). Kept: `.mil`, `gc.ca`, `gov.br`, `go.id`, `go.th`, `go.tz` (V), `bund.de` (P) and twelve exact pairs whose rule or holder was read (`gov.sg`, `gov.my`, `gov.pk`, `gob.pk`, `gov.lk`, `gov.za`, `gov.co`, `gov.pl`, `gov.hk`, `gob.gt`, `gov.de`, `gov.fi`). Dropped, so that their hosts are unverified until a person reads their rule: `govt.nz`, `go.ke`, `go.ug`, `gov.ug`, `gov.ie`. Registry `sr-5`. |
| Q9 | 37 TRUSTED_PRESS entries could not be read here and 11 only from snippets: keep them provisionally? | Keep them (no change in behaviour); a person verifies them before the flag is on by default. **Decided on 2026-10-31**, with a rule on evidence: a page counts as evidence for a publisher only when its site's `robots.txt` does not bar Anthropic's agents. `bbc`, `theguardian` (on its editorial code alone, since theguardian.com bars Claude-User), `usatoday`, `cnbc`, `smh`, `bloomberg`, `washingtonpost`, `forbes`, `cbc`, `darkreading` and `scworld` took the reason, the evidence and the paths to downgrade that Q9 gave them; `thehackernews` is unverified; `npr`, `cnn`, `cnet`, `axios`, `spiegel` and the other publishers carried over stay as they were, with their review of 2026-10-29. Registry `sr-5`. |
| Q10 | Entries whose own pages show a gap: `krebsonsecurity.com` (one author, no editorial oversight shown), `iapp.org` (contributed opinion on news paths), unmarked contributed or sponsored content (`securityweek.com`, `theinformation.com`, `thehackernews.com`, `helpnetsecurity.com`, `bankinfosecurity.com`), no corrections policy found on 17 sites, and VentureBeat's press releases under `/business/`. | Make `krebsonsecurity.com` UNVERIFIED; keep the others as TRUSTED_PRESS with the path rules that could be verified, and review them first. |
| Q11 | Apply the generic DOWNGRADE segments (G1) to every TRUSTED_PRESS host? | Yes: deterministic and on the safe side, with the per-host rules on top. |
| Q12 | Rule V for vendor sources (§D5.4), with its known limit. | Accept it for v1. |
| Q13 | Cap unconfirmed snippets at Medium (this changes R26). | Yes. |
| Q14 | A wire release counts as VENDOR only when it names the vendor's own domain, otherwise UNVERIFIED. | Yes. |
| Q15 | Block `pastebin.com`? | Yes. |
| Q16 | Rejected, withdrawn and delisted records: not offered to the model; the status job relabels pending findings that cite them; should an organisation also be notified? | No notification in the MVP; the status job in M2. |
| Q17 | Match strength applies to VRDB records, not to web pages (§D5.3). | Confirm. |
| Q18 | Registry reviews: who owns them, and fail the hygiene test at 18 months? | You or a named owner; yes. **Open on 2026-10-31:** no owner is named yet. **Still open on 2026-11-05:** the six `sr-6` entries (reviewed on 2026-11-05) need a named owner too; the hygiene test lists them as due for review from 2027-11-05 and fails on them from 2028-05-05. |
| Q19 | Where to show the attribution (the NVD notice, the OGL statement, "© European Union", MITRE's notice)? | A "Data sources" line in the coverage block and in the docs. |
| Q20 | The backfill of D4.6 (recompute pending findings, keep decided ones). | Confirm. |
| Q21 | The licence of the Bing RSS feed (roadmap idea 36) is still open, and brief §2.2 makes the web search run always. | It stays your decision and is a launch question for the new pipeline as much as for today's. **Decided on 2026-10-31:** Bing is removed (its feed notice, Services Agreement clauses 14.f.i and 3.a.vi, a `robots.txt` that disallows `/search`, an API retired on 2025-08-11 and no route to a licence). A run searches with the engine of its research key: Claude web search for an Anthropic key (about $10 per 1,000 searches), and for every other key the GDELT news index (news only, the last 3 months, one request per 5 seconds per address, the project credited). See [Web research toolkit](../../technical/infrastructure/web-research.md#search-engines-q21-decided-2026-10-31). |
| Q22 | The FTC datasets and the HHS breach portal: watch them? | Yes; re-check in M3. **Re-checked on 2026-10-31** (Appendix A): none of them, nor the EDPB feed, meets D2's bar yet, so all stay watched and nothing is built. The FTC now lists four enforcement datasets, keyless and in the public domain, but the one of consumer protection covers civil penalty actions only, states no cadence, and whether it holds privacy and data-security cases can be read only in the file, a download that waits for your permission; the HHS exports still have no stable address, no record id and no readable terms. **Decided on 2026-10-31:** keep watching, build nothing. The one file that could have changed the answer, the FTC's consumer protection civil penalty actions, was refused with a 403 when this phase tried to read it, so its columns and whether it holds privacy and data-security cases stay unverified. |
| Q23 | Unknown hosts are reviewed through a maintainer script (hostnames and counts across organisations), not a screen. | Confirm. |
| Q24 | Brief §2.6, "Weak (name only) caps at Low": does the cap hold for the whole finding, or only for what the weak record contributes? Strict: case 9b is Low with the note even when two trusted publishers report this vendor's designation. Contribution only: case 9b is High with the note (§D5.3 step 5). | Contribution only: the verifier and `identity.ts` check the publishers' articles for the vendor's identity independently of the list, and the note still warns the reader. Your reading of the brief decides. |
| Q25 | User content on the vendor's own domain is VENDOR, and so a statement in an event category: a thread on `community.openai.com`, or a member's post on `linkedin.com` when the vendor's website is a LinkedIn page. | Make user content UNVERIFIED before the vendor's own domains (§D3.2 step 1): rule G11 for community and forum hosts and sections, and the platforms of G6–G9 except the vendor's own subdomain of one. |
| Q26 | Documents that others file with an authority share its credit: an 8-K read from `sec.gov` as a page is High with no note, while the same filing as a SEC record is Low with the note (Q3); a public comment on `regulations.gov` is High; a party's filing on CourtListener is an allegation (O20). | DOWNGRADE path rules make them UNVERIFIED: `sec.gov` under `/Archives/` (O21), `regulations.gov` under `/comment/` (O22), `storage.courtlistener.com` under `/recap/` (O20). The authority's own pages stay OFFICIAL. Once a CIK is stored on the vendor (Q1, M2), an EDGAR filing under that CIK can become VENDOR. |
| Q27 | A wire release that only names the vendor's domain is VENDOR, and a statement in an event category: a security firm's release that links the vendor's advisory. | Narrow Q14: VENDOR only when the release carries an e-mail address on the vendor's own domain; otherwise UNVERIFIED. |
| Q28 | Others file about a company under that company's EDGAR folder: investors' Schedules 13D and 13G, a bidder's SC TO-T, a dissident's DFAN14A, insiders' Forms 3, 4 and 5. Under Q26 a filing under the vendor's stored CIK is VENDOR, so such a document reads as the vendor's own word (M2 final review, Minor 8). The address does not tell the filer: the accession number starts with the CIK of whoever submitted it, often a filing agent for the company's own filings. | Open, raised 2026-10-31. Once Q4 opens, read the filer from the filing's index and keep VENDOR only for a filing the vendor filed, UNVERIFIED otherwise; until then the Q26 rule stands. Your decision. **Decided and built on 2026-10-31** (Q4 is open): the filer is read from the filing's SGML header (`https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/<cik>/<accession without dashes>/<accession>.hdr.sgml`, which the SEC documents), and a filing under the vendor's stored CIK stays VENDOR only when that CIK holds a FILER, FILED-BY or REPORTING-OWNER role; as the SUBJECT-COMPANY or ISSUER only, absent, or when the header cannot be read, the filing is UNVERIFIED and downgraded (Q26, registry `sr-5`). Checked on 2026-10-31 on three filings under CIK 0000320193: the company's own 10-K has a FILER block, a Schedule 13G has SUBJECT-COMPANY and FILED-BY, and a Form 4 has REPORTING-OWNER and ISSUER. |
| Q29 | The incidents, entities and classifications collections of the AI Incident Database's weekly export are CC BY-SA 4.0, but the site's terms bar anyone from selling or commercially exploiting "any aspect of the Site" without express permission. May a commercial product store the export and show records derived from it? | **Built on 2026-11-05 behind a closed gate; the permission is open.** The list is built and tested behind the gate Q29 (`AIID_PERMISSION_GRANTED = false`; D2.14, D4.12). It opens on the written confirmation of the operator, the Responsible AI Collaborative (info@incidentdatabase.ai), that a commercial product may store the export and show records derived from it under CC BY-SA 4.0, or on the product owner's explicit written acceptance of that risk. Share-alike applies to the stored and shown record texts; whether a finding that quotes them is adapted material is part of the question to the operator. |
| Q30 | D2.13 excluded the AI Incident Database as an aggregator, without evaluating it. Should AI-specific sources be evaluated and built? | **Decided on 2026-11-05:** D2.13 is reversed for AI-specific sources at the product owner's request. An aggregator may be a VRDB source when its licence passes criterion (d), and its records carry their true class (an aggregator is UNVERIFIED). Built: MITRE ATLAS, NHTSA SGO and the FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices (open), and the AI Incident Database (Q29). Not built, because their terms fail (d): AIAAIC (no commercial adaptation or redistribution), OECD AIM (all rights reserved; summaries generated by a model) and AVID (the terms forbid data mining and commercialisation). Not built, because there is no export or licence: GW DAIL, the MIT AI Incident Tracker and the Agent Incident Registry. Not built, because it is not AI-specific: MAUDE. Their hosts enter the registry (`sr-6`). D2.0 has rows 14 to 17, and D2.14 to D2.18 the sections. |
| Q31 | NHTSA publishes no licence for the Standing General Order files. | **Decided on 2026-11-05 (delegated):** as a work of the US government (17 U.S.C. §105) they are accepted in the way of Q5, without a separate legal review. |
| Q32 | The README of `mitre-atlas/atlas-data` prints "ALL RIGHTS RESERVED. Approved for Public Release", and its LICENSE file is the Apache License 2.0. Which governs? | **Decided on 2026-11-05 (delegated):** the LICENSE file governs; the README line is MITRE's release marking. The source row carries MITRE's copyright line and the licence. |
| Q33 | The US Consolidated Screening List publishes no licence statement, and the developer terms of the International Trade Administration (ITA) ask a service to show a notice and bar modifying content while naming ITA as its source. May a commercial product store the lists and show records derived from them? | **Decided on 2026-11-07 (delegated):** yes, with no gate. The CSL publishes no licence. It is a compilation by the US International Trade Administration of lists that the Departments of Commerce, State and Treasury publish, a work of the US Government (17 U.S.C. §105), accepted in the way of Q5 without a separate legal review. ITA's developer terms allow a service to search, display and analyze the data. They ask (should, not must) for the notice "This product uses the International Trade Administration's Data API but is not endorsed or certified by the International Trade Administration." VerifyWise reads the downloadable file and not the Data API, so it shows the adapted sentence "This product uses data from the International Trade Administration's Consolidated Screening List but is not endorsed or certified by the International Trade Administration. Entries adapted by VerifyWise." as the source's attribution. The terms bar modifying content while naming ITA as its source, so every record says it is adapted and every cut is marked. The endorsement disclaimer page (read 2026-11-07) is about ITA's own links and use of trade names, and adds no restriction. The missing licence statement is the posture of OFAC (Q5) and NHTSA (Q31): the terms grant display and analysis, and nothing reserves rights or limits commercial use. If ITA ever objects, the copy is deleted and a gate added. |
| Q34 | openFDA's device recall data is CC0 1.0, and the GMDN content of its `openfda` block is licensed separately. | Q35 | NHTSA publishes no licence for the defect investigations flat file (`FLAT_INV.zip`), and its website policies answered 403. May a commercial product store it and show records derived from it? | **Decided on 2026-11-10 (on delegation):** yes, with no gate, in the way of Q31. It is a work of the US government (17 U.S.C. §105), from the same publisher and host as the SGO files. DOT's own data catalog tags NHTSA's "Investigations Data" "Public Domain U.S. Government"; the catalog's flat-file entry carries no licence value, and NHTSA's and DOT's policy pages could not be read (403). Conditions: no wording or logo that implies NHTSA's endorsement; the investigation summaries (`SUMMARY`), which name petitioners, are never stored; every record says that an investigation is not a finding that a defect exists, and that its counts are adapted. Attribution: "Source: US National Highway Traffic Safety Administration, Office of Defects Investigation (defect investigations flat file). Not endorsed by NHTSA. Entries adapted by VerifyWise." |
| Q36 | NHTSA publishes no licence for the recalls flat file (`FLAT_RCL_POST_2010.zip`), and its layout labels fields 25 to 27 "Manufacturer-Supplied". Fields 25 to 27 are third-party text that 17 U.S.C. §105 does not cover, and the defect, consequence, corrective and notes texts (fields 20 to 23) summarize manufacturers' reports: none of the seven is stored or shown. | Q37 | openFDA's device enforcement reports are CC0 1.0, but their documentation says the data should not be used to track the lifecycle of a recall, and the FDA does not update a recall's status after classification. Only the class and the classification day of each recalled product are carried; the status (which the FDA does not update after classification), the recalling firm, the address, the distribution, the lot codes, the reason, the description and the `openfda` block of a report are not. A report with a class and no readable classification day keeps its class, and the record says "classification day not stated"; of two reports of one recall number the more serious class wins, so that the order of openFDA's answer decides nothing (D2.20). The source row's licence and attribution name both endpoints, and the attribution adds the FDA's requested credit, "Data provided by the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (https://open.fda.gov)". |
| Q38 | The PipeRoll Agent Incident Registry is licensed CC BY 4.0 in its `incidents/LICENSE` while the repository's licence field says MIT, its records carry cross-references to the AI Incident Database, and Q30 refused an "Agent Incident Registry" for having no export and no licence. May it be mirrored? | **Decided on 2026-11-10 (on delegation):** yes, built weak, with no gate. The CC BY 4.0 grant in `incidents/LICENSE` and on the site covers the records ("free to share and adapt this data for any purpose, including commercially, provided you give appropriate credit"); the MIT licence covers the tooling. The `aiid_incident_id` field is never read, so nothing of the AI Incident Database (Q29) passes through it. Each record cites the PIR ids of the entries it shows (the newest 3 with their titles, and up to 6 more ids in the text, up to 100 in its identifiers) and links the newest entry's permalink. A title is edited before it is shown, so that no natural person of a shape that the rules know reaches a record (the residuals are in D2.26): the first party of a case caption is quoted as "A party" and a title word of a surname-prefix or handle shape as "[name]" (D2.26, D4.14). The registry that Q30 refused (487 records, run by a security vendor, no export) is not this one (110 records, an individual editor, an export), and even if it were, Q30's reasons (no export, no licence) no longer hold. The class is UNVERIFIED (one editor; the registry records incidents of its own operating agent). Attribution: "PipeRoll Agent Incident Registry (piperoll.org), CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/); each record cites the PIR ids of the entries it shows and links the newest entry. Adapted by VerifyWise: titles shortened and edited, entries counted; not endorsed by PipeRoll." |

Run-time reading by the analysis model (`ai-input`) is not reserved. Records hold per-tool counts, dates, roles and countries only, never a party, a court, a file name or case text. Attribution: the credit line of the export's own header (the database's name and author, its address and CC BY 4.0), followed by "Counted and adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by its author." Source: Government of Canada AI Register (Minimum Viable Product), Treasury Board of Canada Secretariat. Adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by the Government of Canada." |

| Q48 | The FedRAMP Marketplace data on GSA's GitHub has no licence file. Only GSA's status fields are proposed for copying, but the provider-supplied `website` field, which is not one of them, gives a strong match. May the list be mirrored? | **Decided on 2026-11-10 (on delegation):** yes, with no gate, as a work of GSA (17 U.S.C. §105), copying only these fields: provider, offering, status and its date, impact level, authorization type, agency count and UEI (GSA also publishes the service and deployment models, the assessor and the initial authorizing agency, which the licence note allows, but no sentence of a record shows them, so the rows do not carry them). The host of an offering's website is a fact, not expression. It may serve as a match key (never shown or stored as text) when it is an apex domain (a country suffix is no apex), not a shared host, claimed by exactly one provider in the whole file, and its brand label of three characters or more is the provider's name, contains it (a name of four characters or more) or begins it (a label of four characters or more); a host whose brand label has two characters, such as `go.gov`, is excluded by this test. The rule is the strictest of those that keep at least 95 % of the domain keys that the real file had before it (424 of 441, 2026-11-10). Logos, descriptions, contacts and the Assessors section are never copied. The record says that a listing is an authorization status, not a security assurance. Attribution: "Source: US General Services Administration, FedRAMP Marketplace data. Not endorsed by GSA or FedRAMP. Entries adapted by VerifyWise." |

| Q50 | The California Attorney General's conditions page says its content is "considered in the public domain ... as permitted by law", excluding material it does not own, such as the breached organizations' sample notices. Only the Department's list is copied: organization, breach dates and reported date. The sample notices are never fetched or linked. The conditions page's no-warranty clause is recorded: the list carries no guarantee of accuracy, and naming a firm implies no endorsement. Person-named practices (rules P and M, 54 rows on the profile of 2026-11-10) are dropped before anything is stored; a person in an alias part or in brackets is cut out and the business stays, and a practice written in pieces (a person behind a comma or an ampersand) is dropped when no word of the name is a legal form or an organization word (6 of the 28 such names); the few plain person names that the rules miss are shown only on a match, and a person behind `d.b.a.`, `doing business as`, `t/a` or `dba:`, behind a comma or an ampersand with no blank, `and` or a hyphen, or in a name that the middle-initial rule does not read is not seen (D2.22). Attribution: "Source: State of California Department of Justice, Office of the Attorney General, data security breach list. Not endorsed by the Department. Entries adapted by VerifyWise." |
| Q51 | The ATRS records are published on GOV.UK, but 13 of the 155 come from local authorities, whose records are not Crown copyright. Contact e-mail addresses and named officials are dropped: only two sections of a record's text are read, and the rest never reaches the database. Linked documents and model-provider material are not followed. No logos are used, and no endorsement is implied. A record is the public body's own description, shown as context and never as a risk. |

Case documents, party submissions and the manufacturers' attached field safety notices are third-party content and are never fetched. | Q53 | GLEIF's LEI data are CC0, but GLEIF's LEI Data Terms of Use bind users not to imply that their products are provided, supported, authorized or associated by or with GLEIF, with liquidated damages of CHF 100,000 per case. The record is shown as context only and never as a certification. The API's limit of 60 requests a minute is kept by a shared spacer at 50 a minute.
